# GLM review 2026-10-19 (glm-5.3): 346 findings, 145 high severity, 11 shard(s)

Revision 288638b1d9ef. Shards: agentbox#2, agentbox#3, nostr-rust-forum, ontomark, sidestr-rs, solid-pod-rs, visionclaw#2, visionclaw#3, visionflow, visiongraph, vowl-wasm. Unverified hypotheses: reproduce each with a failing test or check (build-with-quality) before acting.

## agentbox#2 / critical

### F-01 — Nearly all agent side-effect surfaces bypass the governance pipeline entirely
- Topics: AB-14.1, AB-14.2, AB-14.14
- Evidence: The AgentActionPipeline and ExecutionJournal are fully implemented and contract-tested (`tests/contract/agent-action-pipeline.contract.spec.js`), but `GOVERNANCE-capabilities.md:276-281` names "no single policy decision point" as the TOP OPEN RISK. AB-14.1 shows only `POST /v1/tasks` routes through the pipeline. Direct tool calls, code-mode sub-calls, ACI shell, consultant actions, dream-engine side effects, beads work-DAG children and alternate harness paths all reach `EXEC` with no interceptor. The dream engine "records only, never polices" via `POST /v1/exec/record`.
- Failure: An agent executing a shell command via ACI, spawning a subagent via code-mode, or dispatching through an alternate harness performs mutations, egress, or spend with no approval gate, no journal entry, and no capability token. A compromised or misbehaving agent can exfiltrate data or spend budget with zero governance trace.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, "TOP OPEN RISK" — but the material says it is "narrowed, not closed," which understates that the narrowing is from zero surfaces to one)

### F-02 — Every signing key readable by every co-resident process when role_isolation is off (the shipped default)
- Topics: AB-16.1, AB-16.7, AB-16.13
- Evidence: `GOVERNANCE-capabilities.md` states "a process running as the same devuser can still read the token file." With role_isolation off, every supervised program, MCP server, skill and agent runs as uid 1000. The bridge key is `0400 devuser` (`entrypoint-unified.sh:1082-1091`), per-agent DID keys are `0600 devuser` (`agent-identity.js:165-166`), the AoE daemon token is in an owner-only `0700` directory, and ROLE variables including signing keys stay in PID 1's environment and reach every child (`entrypoint-unified.sh:424, :468`).
- Failure: Any compromised MCP server, skill script, or agent process reads the bridge signing key, every per-agent DID key, and the AoE bearer token from the filesystem or environment, then impersonates any identity in the container. The seccomp profile blocks `ptrace` and `process_vm_readv` but not `open`/`read` on same-uid files.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in AB-16.1 N4 and AB-16.7, described as "residual" — but the systemic consequence that ALL keys are simultaneously exposed is not drawn out as a single compound risk)

### F-03 — Gateway signs with the ROOT operator key by default
- Topics: AB-16.5
- Evidence: `gateway.cjs:192-193` — the default `AGENTBOX_GATEWAY_IDENTITY` is `'operator'`, so `sk = rawSk` and the gateway signs AS the operator. The derived-child mode (`HMAC-SHA256(operator_sk, 'agentbox-gateway-v1')`) exists but is opt-in via env. The material marks this as DIVERGENCE: "out of the box the gateway holds and signs with the ROOT operator key."
- Failure: Compounding F-02: the gateway is a devuser process holding the root key. Any co-resident process that can read the gateway's env or the key file obtains the root operator identity — the identity that commands the tmux fleet, spawns agents, and authenticates to relays. The root key never needs to leave the box by design, but in this configuration it is effectively available to every process in it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-04 — Journal attribution forgeable via X-Agentbox-Pubkey at the verified commit
- Topics: AB-14.14, AB-17.2
- Evidence: At verified commit `3f05ae9e`, `resolveAgentDid` (`action-plane.js:221-231`) accepts `X-Agentbox-Pubkey` from any peer, and `server.js:49` binds `0.0.0.0`. The material records this was fixed on main `b9e0ea9a8` (2026-10-12) by requiring a loopback TCP peer, but the fix postdates the stamp. The same vulnerability applies to `agent-event-auth.js:157-160` for agent-event attribution.
- Failure: Any `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY` holder on the docker network puts an arbitrary `did:nostr` into the journalled `agent_did` of every task-spawn and every emitted agent event. Provenance records attribute actions to identities that did not perform them.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, "resolved 2026-10-12" — but resolution postdates the production stamp; also notes a same-container process can still forge over loopback)

### F-05 — Two independent inbox consumers with different allowlists write the same path
- Topics: AB-13.6, AB-13.7
- Evidence: The Rust `spawn_consumer` (`lib.rs:759-809`) and the JS `RelayConsumer` (`relay-consumer.js:546`, wired at `server.js:1366-1428`) subscribe to the same relay and write to the same `pods/NPUB/events/inbox/` path. Each has its own allowlist (`BridgeConfig.allowed_pubkeys` vs `AGENTBOX_RELAY_ALLOWED_PUBKEYS`). The material marks this as DIVERGENCE. Post-ADR-2065, the Rust consumer is the sole inbox writer when the pod-bridge daemon runs, but the JS consumer still writes governance/payment/intent events — the boundary between which consumer writes which kinds is an env-var switch, not an architectural guarantee.
- Failure: An allowlist mismatch between the two consumers means an event rejected by one authorization policy is accepted by the other, or the same event is processed twice with different authorization outcomes. If `AGENTBOX_POD_INBOX_WRITER` is misconfigured, both write concurrently with no locking, risking data corruption in the inbox.
- Confidence: medium
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in AB-13.6 note)

### F-06 — Production dream engine is the pre-extraction binary, missing guard-before-apply and finished-night rule
- Topics: AB-23 (audit qualification), AB-23.1, AB-23.4, AB-23.7
- Evidence: The audit qualification states "the loaded Nix-store binary is still the pre-extraction one, and the guard-before-apply, the finished-night rule and the corrected dry streak are source behaviour, not live behaviour, until the image is rebuilt." The guard-before-apply (`candidate.rs:237`, refusing protected paths before any git command) and the finished-night rule (`runstate.rs:401`, preventing repeat publishes on restart) are critical safety fixes that exist only in the unbuilt source.
- Failure: The running dream engine can modify protected paths (no guard before apply) and, after its own ledger commit moves HEAD and thus the run id, a restart can repeat a publish — opening a duplicate pull request or repeating an externally visible effect that was already published. The corrected dry-streak rule (REJECT resets instead of advancing) is also absent, so the running engine accumulates dry streaks on rejections it shouldn't.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (audit qualification, ADR-2141 `activation_status: staged` — but the material frames it as "pending with the operator" without a deadline or escalation path)

### F-07 — Spend accounted in three stores that never reconcile
- Topics: AB-15.6
- Evidence: "spend is accounted in three stores that never reconcile: the flock-guarded cost_cap ledger file (`ledger.rs:75`), the pod-held sats balance behind `GET /v1/pay/balance` (`payments.js:205`), and the marketplace token_budget carried on a grant (`llm-marketplace.js:85`). No surface sums them, so no single number answers what this container has spent."
- Failure: An operator cannot determine total spend across LLM calls, tree-search candidates, marketplace grants, and pod balance. A gradual leak in one store (e.g., an unrevoked marketplace grant, an uncleared cost_cap hold) is invisible until an external threshold is hit. Budget enforcement on one store does not constrain the others.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT)

### F-08 — Content-addressed URN crossing: lose the store, lose the identity permanently
- Topics: AB-17.4, AB-17.6
- Evidence: For `execution` and `kg` kinds, `toVisionclaw` generates a fresh sha256-12 with "no algebraic inverse" (`bc20-provenance-bridge.js:158`). Recovery depends entirely on `JsonlUrnMappingStore` at `/var/lib/agentbox/code-harness/bc20-urn-mappings.jsonl`. The material states "store loss is identity loss. No recovery receipt exists for the durable store." The `roundTrips()` proof helper uses a fresh in-memory store, proving the algebra but not the deployment.
- Failure: A crash mid-write to the JSONL file, a disk failure, or an accidental deletion of the mappings file permanently severs the link between agentbox URNs and their VisionClaw counterparts. Downstream provenance queries return `null` and dropped crossings accumulate silently. There is no backup, no reconstruction path, and no alert on store loss.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in AB-17.4 and AB-17.6)

### F-09 — Dream engine default provider sends repository content to external Z.AI unattended
- Topics: AB-23.2, AB-23.15, AB-24.7
- Evidence: `agentbox.toml:2503` sets `llm_provider = "zai"` as the default. The material states "nightly repository content leaves the LAN unless llm_provider = loom." The Loom LAN-only path at `llm.rs:205` is opt-in. This runs at 01:00-05:00 UTC unattended.
- Failure: Every night, the full content of nominated repositories (source code, configuration, potentially secrets in committed files) is sent to an external vendor API. There is no redaction step in this path (unlike the session-mirror which has `redactForEgress`). An operator who assumes the dream engine is a local-only system is leaking proprietary code nightly.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT in AB-23.15 — but the material's framing as a "deliberate operator choice" obscures that the default posture is external egress)

### F-10 — Break-glass bearer token: no expiry or scope bounds by default, full identity bypass
- Topics: AB-16.6
- Evidence: `proxy.mjs:672-706` — when `BREAK_GLASS` is configured: unset expiry permits unbounded lifetime; unset scope is unrestricted; the sentinel pubkey means "every downstream attribution for this request is a placeholder." The material states this is "a full identity bypass while enabled." ADR-2027 governing this is `proposed / none / inactive` — the implementation is partially live with no certified lifecycle.
- Failure: An operator enables break-glass for a specific incident and forgets to remove it. The token has no expiry, no scope restriction, and grants full identity bypass to anyone on the LAN who discovers it. Every action taken via this path has a placeholder attribution, making post-incident forensics impossible.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, ADR-2027 lifecycle "open")

### F-11 — payment_settlement authority class defined as zero-tolerance but never invoked in production
- Topics: AB-14.11, AB-15.6
- Evidence: `agentbox.toml:1243` classifies `payment_settlement` as zero-tolerance (irreversible). But "no production path passes that actionClass to the gate. The only call sites are tests, and `management-api/lib/authority.js:226` never sees it." The `/v1/pay/*` routes never call the authority gate.
- Failure: An actual sats payment — the most dangerous, irreversible spend the system can make — bypasses the strictest governance classification defined for it. A bug in the payment route, a compromised caller, or a mistaken amount moves real value with no human approval and no zero-tolerance gate, despite the system having a classification specifically designed to prevent this.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT in AB-14.11 and AB-15.6)

### F-12 — Audit-chain cannot detect deletion at the tail
- Topics: AB-14.9
- Evidence: `audit-chain.js:13-16` — "deletion at the tail is the ONE mode a bare chain cannot see." The hash chain (`SHA256(prev_hash || record)`) detects splices and edits but is structurally blind to truncation from the end. `readTail` (`audit-chain.js:195`) resumes from the newest file, so a truncated tail simply becomes the new starting point.
- Failure: An attacker or a disk-full condition truncates the most recent audit records. The chain verifies clean because the remaining prefix is internally consistent. The most recent — and often most forensically valuable — records are silently lost. There is no external anchor, no periodic checkpoint to a second location, and no length expectation to detect the loss.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (noted in AB-14.9 as a property of bare chains, but the lack of mitigation is not called out as a risk)

### F-13 — SONA binary accepts 384-dim learns but silently accumulates nothing
- Topics: AB-20.11, AB-21.5
- Evidence: "the prebuilt binary HARDCODES embedding_dim = 256. A 384-dim learn returns status 'learned' but ACCUMULATES NOTHING — verified live." The feeder counts `fed` on `status:"learned"` alone. Both gates stay off, but the pipeline appears to function.
- Failure: An operator enables SONA learn, observes successful "learned" responses, and believes the system is accumulating patterns. The binary silently discards everything. Resources spent on embedding and feeding are wasted. When the gates are eventually enabled (because the counts look healthy), the system has no accumulated model to apply.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE D1 — but the material notes `engine_accumulating` was added on main post-stamp, so at the verified commit there is no way to detect this)

### F-14 — bge-small embeds only the first 2000 characters; the tail is invisible to search
- Topics: AB-20.2, AB-20.3
- Evidence: "bge-small embeds only the first EMBED_PREFIX_CHARS (2000) of a value (`memory-tools.js:57,:257`) — THE TAIL IS INVISIBLE TO SEARCH." The material states this as an invariant for the caller, not a system limit — it is the agent's responsibility to "keep values under ~2,000 chars, front-load the searchable facts."
- Failure: An agent stores a detailed 5000-character memory entry (a postmortem, a configuration reference, a security policy). The critical detail is in the tail. Semantic search never surfaces it. The memory exists and can be retrieved by exact key, but no query will ever find it by meaning. The system silently loses recall for long entries with no warning to the storing agent or the operator.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (INVARIANT — but framed as agent guidance rather than a system defect; no runtime enforcement or warning exists)

### F-15 — Governance activation blocked on an unnamed actor producing an unspecified response
- Topics: AB-14.15
- Evidence: "ADR-2087 reaches `live` only on the first real governance response after the rebuild (B7, `adr-2087-check.sh:332-344`); the record does not say who is expected to produce that response, or when." The check requires `forum_auth_api` configured AND at least one `governance.receipt-posted` log line. The 2026-10-02 run exited 2 (staged) with "posted 0, queued 0."
- Failure: The governance system remains indefinitely in "staged" state because no named person or process is responsible for producing the first governance response. Months pass, the queue stays empty, and the governance guarantee remains aspirational. Meanwhile, agents operate under the assumption that governance is active (because the code exists and tests pass), when in production no decision has ever been surfaced or decided.
- Confidence: medium
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open in AB-14.15 — but the material does not flag the deeper problem: a governance system that has never processed a real decision provides zero protection regardless of code correctness)

---

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Actual relay behavior for kind-5 deletion**: AB-23.17 states "the relay and forum code are outside this topic's sources, so that half of the round trip is asserted, not verified here." Whether the cloud relay actually hard-deletes events on a kind-5, and whether the forum reads cases by subscription or by query, determines whether governance withdrawals work at all.

2. **Container network segmentation**: The material shows `server.js` binds `0.0.0.0` and mentions a "docker network" but does not describe the network topology — which containers can reach the management API, the relay, the pod, and the gateway. F-04's severity depends on whether non-loopback peers are reachable.

3. **Backup and disaster recovery procedures**: The secret-backup tool exists (AB-16.12) but the material states "the active container has not been rebuilt with this source change" and "no real-key rotation or recovery is implied." Whether any tested backup/recovery procedure exists for the RuVector Postgres, the Solid Pod, the URN mapping store, and the audit chain is not determinable.

4. **Scale and multi-instance behavior**: The material describes a single-container deployment. Whether the nostr relay, the management API, or the dream engine are designed to run as multiple replicas (with the per-process `nextEventId`, the single-writer inbox, the per-night lock) is not addressed. Failover behavior on container restart is described for individual components but not for the system as a whole.

5. **The actual composition of ROLE environment variables**: The 11-name ROLE list in `env-classes.json` is referenced but not enumerated in full. Whether `AGENTBOX_PRIVKEY_HEX` (the root operator key source for the gateway) is in the ROLE set or the DEVUSER set determines whether F-03's exposure is limited to the gateway process or extends to every child of PID 1.

## agentbox#2 / premortem

# Post-mortem — agentbox estate incident

**Basis.** Compiled only from the diagram corpus as stamped (mostly `3f05ae9e7ae13d103ad8d9fe06baed991fedb987`; AB-16 and AB-23 carry their own stamps). Every citation below is the citation the material itself gives; nothing was checked against code. Where I reason past the material I say "inferred".

---

### R-1 — Ungoverned agent side-effect execution: there is no single policy decision point, and most surfaces have no interceptor at all
- Topics: AB-14.1, AB-14.2, AB-14.14, AB-14.15, AB-23.2
- Chain of events:
  1. An agent turn or unattended job initiates a side effect through any surface other than `POST /v1/tasks` — a direct MCP tool call, a code-mode sub-call, the ACI shell, a consultant CLI, a beads background child, an alternate harness, or the dream engine's nightly actions.
  2. That surface carries no interceptor: nothing classifies the action, nothing obtains an approval, nothing runs the nine-stage pipeline. The surface's own local check (shell allowlist, spend cap, evidence gate) is the only gate.
  3. The action executes. If the surface is the dream engine, its journal write to `POST /v1/exec/record` fails open; if it is the EXP-B8 tick, its two outward effects are recorded through neither the exec journal nor the authority journal.
  4. There is no deny decision and, in the worst cases, no record; the audit trail the operator would reconstruct from (authority journal, execution journal, hash-chained JSONL) has no row for the action.
  5. The client-impacting effect — egress of their data, a spend, an external publish under the operator's identity — is discovered after the fact, and reconstruction cannot even establish which agent acted.
- Evidence:
  - AB-14.1 DIVERGENCE1: GOVERNANCE-capabilities.md:276-281 is the material's own **TOP OPEN RISK**, "no single policy decision point"; `action-plane.js` wires exactly one surface (`POST /v1/tasks`); the dream engine "now RECORDS each side effect into the same journal through POST /v1/exec/record (ADR-2071 …) but nothing there approves or denies"; "every OTHER surface above (direct tool call, code-mode, ACI shell, consultant, dream, beads, alt harness) still reaches EXEC with no interceptor — the gap is narrower, not closed".
  - AB-14.2 note: the state machine "is fully implemented and contract-tested (tests/contract/agent-action-pipeline.contract.spec.js) but is never reached by real traffic".
  - AB-23.2 note: "The night is journalled through POST /v1/exec/record, not policed"; "Every journalled side effect fails open (agentbox/services/dream-engine/src/journal.rs:30)".
  - AB-14.15 Open: the EXP-B8 tick "takes two outward side effects when its stopping rule fires, a signed forum post (scripts/experiments/exp-b8-label-log.cjs:550-563) and a pull request opened with `gh` (:573-606), and records neither through POST /v1/exec/record or the authority journal".
  - AB-14.1 DIVERGE7: skill lint is advisory — "an enabled skill with clean frontmatter is trusted at runtime with no further check" (GOVERNANCE-capabilities.md:307-308).
  - AB-14.14: even the one wired surface classifies task-spawn `side_effect_class:'local'` (FAST_PATH, no approval receipt) — "a deliberate ADR-2041 rollout choice", reclassification owed.
- Warning signs visible today: the governing doc's own TOP OPEN RISK heading; a contract suite that passes while zero production traffic reaches the pipeline; journalling that fails open; EXP-B8 already performing unrecorded outward effects; GOVERNANCE-capabilities.md:496 still describing ADR-2071 as proposed while the journal ships, i.e. the doc picture lags reality in both directions.
- Confidence: high that the enforcement gap is real and shipped; the specific triggering surface is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Same-UID custody failure: with role_isolation off (the shipped default), any process in the box can read every key
- Topics: AB-16.1, AB-16.2, AB-16.7, AB-16.9, AB-16.13, AB-16.14, AB-14.14, AB-15 (closing drift), AB-13.14
- Chain of events:
  1. Some process in the container — an agent session, an MCP server, a consultant vendor CLI, a skill script, or anything compromised through them — runs as devuser uid 1000, like every other supervised program.
  2. It reads the bridge key (`/run/secrets/nostr.key`, written 0400 devuser at boot when the flag is off), the AoE daemon token at `~/.config/agent-of-empires/serve.url`, per-agent DID key files, the mirror child secret, and 37 DEVUSER_CLASS provider credentials carried in every agent's environment.
  3. With the bridge key it can invoke `nostr-pod-bridge publish` to sign colloquy kinds under the sovereign identity, or sign directly; with the AoE bearer it drives the interaction plane; the break-glass bearer — the same value as BRIDGE_TOKEN until Q4 — is equally readable.
  4. Attribution is then forgeable in-container: a same-container process passes the loopback test and can stamp any pubkey into the journalled `agent_did` and forge `X-Agentbox-Pubkey` over loopback.
  5. External actions are signed as the operator; spend-capable keys sign — the sidestr wallet's permissive `SpendPolicy` has no caller-side authority gate, and the faucet treasury key's legacy workspace twin is never deleted. The client sees the operator's identity on actions the operator did not take.
- Evidence:
  - AB-16.1 N4: "role_isolation OFF, the shipped default — a same-uid boundary. Every supervised program, MCP server, skill and agent runs as devuser, so each can read every other's 0600 key file, the AoE daemon token and the mirror key."
  - AB-16.7: flag off — a peer process reads the key file, the AoE token and the bridge key, all SUCCEED; boot writes the bridge key 0400 devuser (entrypoint-unified.sh:1082-1091); "The token raises the bar but does not isolate same-uid peers" (GOVERNANCE-capabilities item 5).
  - AB-16.13: with the flag off, ROLE variables "stay in PID 1 and reach every child" (entrypoint-unified.sh:424, :468).
  - AB-16.9 C3: the break-glass bearer is "still the same value as BRIDGE_TOKEN until Q4".
  - AB-16.14 DEBT: "the 37 DEVUSER_CLASS credentials stay in every agent process by exception, owner disposition Q8 pending".
  - AB-14.14 Debt: "a same-container process still passes the loopback test and can stamp any pubkey into the journalled agent_did" (action-plane.js:221-231).
  - AB-15 closing drift: sidestr-wallet "ships only a permissive implementation, leaving the authority gate (ADR-2100) to the caller, which nothing in this topic yet supplies".
  - AB-16 closing tension: the faucet treasury key's workspace twin is "copied once and never deleted" (ADR-2122:212-215); with the flag off the manifest still points the faucet at the bind.
  - AB-13.14: `nostr-pod-bridge publish` signs "under the sovereign identity" (lib.rs:549-553); AB-16.2: the seccomp profile is allow-by-default and supplemental — not a sandbox.
- Warning signs visible today: every citation above is already in the corpus, marked DIVERGENCE/Debt/Tension; the role accounts and identity port exist but the flag is off pending the owner's boot rehearsal.
- Confidence: high that the boundary is as described; which process turns hostile is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Source/live divergence in the dream daemon: the running engine predates the extraction (no guard-before-apply; a restart can repeat a publish)
- Topics: AB-23 (audit qualification), AB-23.2, AB-23.3, AB-23.4, AB-23.6, AB-23.7
- Chain of events:
  1. The reviewed, registry-pinned source (`dream-core`/`dream-gate` `=0.1.0`, PR 28) is not what the supervisor runs: the image rebuild was "pending with the operator", so the loaded Nix-store binary is the pre-extraction engine.
  2. That binary applies a model-emitted dream-patch with **no protected-path guard before apply** — the exact defect the extraction's `candidate_flow` guard ordering fixes.
  3. It identifies a finished night by run id, which the engine's own ledger commit moves — so a crash after the ledger commit followed by a restart re-runs the same night and can publish a second draft PR.
  4. It resets the dry streak on REJECT, so a repo failing every night is never pruned and keeps consuming nights and vendor model spend.
  5. Its journalled side effects fail open, and agentbox CI had not run since 2026-10-08, so nothing automated flags the divergence. The operator trusts behaviour ("guard-before-apply", "finished-night rule") that the running daemon does not have.
  6. In the incident window, an unattended night applies a patch touching a protected path, or a restart duplicates a publish, and the record of it is absent or wrong.
- Evidence:
  - AB-23 audit qualification: "the loaded Nix-store binary is still the pre-extraction one, and the guard-before-apply, the finished-night rule and the corrected dry streak are **source behaviour, not live behaviour**, until the image is rebuilt (pending with the operator…) and a process-identity receipt is taken".
  - AB-23.4: "The old engine had no guard before apply (ADR-2141, Context)."
  - AB-23.7: "Before 2026-10-15 the run id was the only identity, and the engine's ledger commit moved HEAD and therefore the run id, so a restart could repeat a publish."
  - AB-23.2: "RESOLVED 2026-10-15: the old engine reset on REJECT" — i.e. the live binary still resets.
  - AB-23.3 Debt: "the agentbox repository's GitHub Actions have not run since 2026-10-08 … so PR 28 merged with no CI gate."
  - AB-23.2 note: journal fail-open (journal.rs:30); AB-23.6 Tension: "no record at these revisions shows a full night publishing a pull request through the extracted path".
- Warning signs visible today: the audit paragraph itself; the rebuild and process-identity receipt listed as pending; ADR-2141 `activation_status: staged` while `implementation_status: complete`; dead CI.
- Confidence: high that the divergence existed at the stamp (it is stated); inferred that it — or the same activation-lag pattern in another component — persisted to the incident.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — The mirror's redaction contract is described two contradictory ways
- Topics: AB-16.11 vs AB-13.9, AB-13.17, AB-13.10
- Evidence: AB-16.11 says the mirror "composes UNREDACTED selected text" before the NIP-59 wrap and concludes "Two paths, two gates, two encryption postures, one of them unredacted before wrapping". AB-13.9 draws `redactForEgress(body)` before publish with a fail-closed skip on redaction failure (nostr-live-mirror.cjs:453-457), and AB-13.17 records ADR-2026 RESOLVED with one shared policy contract (mandatory redaction, fixture `tests/fixtures/egress-redaction.v1.json`).
- Failure: whichever topic misdescribes the hook, no one can answer "is session content redacted before it leaves the box". If AB-16.11 is right, client session content egresses to the cloud relay unredacted (gift-wrapped, so exposed to anyone who obtains the child key — which R-2 shows is devuser-readable).
- Confidence: high that the contradiction exists; which side is authoritative cannot be resolved from the material.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (AB-16.11 DIVERGENCE; AB-13.17 RESOLVED)

### F-02 — Session-digest egress scope: public kind-30840 visibility plus a flattened transcript to a vendor
- Topics: AB-13.10, AB-13.17, AB-23.15
- Evidence: AB-13.17 note — "The digest is public kind-30840 visibility while this path requires a non-empty recipient enumeration. Shared redaction fixtures do not imply equal recipient semantics." AB-13.10: `summarise_via_zai` POSTs the flattened, redacted transcript to Z.AI (glm-5.3, session_summary.rs:251-268) and `publish_to_relay` is "best-effort, warn-only" (lib.rs:539-541). AB-23.15 Debt: `llm_provider = "zai"` is the default, so "nightly repository content leaves the LAN on every unattended night".
- Failure: redaction coverage rules are nowhere stated in the material; anything a flatten-then-redact pass misses in a client transcript goes to the vendor, and the resulting summary is published at public visibility.
- Confidence: high on each fact; the leak-through is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt; RESOLVED-note caveat)

### F-03 — `payment_settlement` is classified zero-tolerance but no production path enforces it
- Topics: AB-15.6, AB-14.11
- Evidence: "the authority classification table names payment_settlement zero-tolerance as an irreversible above-threshold sats spend (agentbox.toml:1176), but no production path passes that actionClass to the gate. The only call sites are tests… routes/payments.js never reaches management-api/lib/authority.js:226."
- Failure: an above-threshold, irreversible sats settlement executes with no approval, no deny-journal row and no receipt — precisely the class the table marks most dangerous.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, in both topics)

### F-04 — Break-glass bearer: unbounded lifetime by default, unrestricted scope by default, shared value
- Topics: AB-16.6, AB-16.9
- Evidence: proxy.mjs:662-706 — "unset expiry permits unbounded lifetime"; "unset scope is unrestricted"; the sentinel BREAK_GLASS_PUBKEY means "every downstream attribution for this request is a placeholder"; accepted on port 9096 and via `?access_token=`/`?bearer=` on WS upgrades, where "A single shared secret bypasses NIP-98 entirely"; AB-16.9 C3: "still the same value as BRIDGE_TOKEN until Q4".
- Failure: the bearer leaks once (readable by any same-UID process, captured at process start) and grants an unbounded, unscoped identity bypass whose uses are unattributable; because it equals BRIDGE_TOKEN, one disclosure compromises both.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE; ADR-2027 open)

### F-05 — The gateway's default identity is the ROOT operator key
- Topics: AB-16.5, AB-13.5
- Evidence: gateway.cjs:192-193 — default `AGENTBOX_GATEWAY_IDENTITY === 'operator'` means "out of the box the gateway holds and signs with the ROOT operator key"; the derived-child mode is opt-in via env.
- Failure: a gateway compromise (it is a devuser process; the key is readable per R-2) issues commands and replies as the operator identity itself — no separate channel to detect or rotate.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-06 — Revoking a compromised relay publisher requires a full nix rebuild
- Topics: AB-16.8, AB-13.3, AB-13.2
- Evidence: "The relay allowlist is baked at nix build (relayAllowedPubkeysCsv), so publisher revocation needs a full rebuild — the compromise window is one build-deploy cycle. ADR-2027 Context." AB-13.3: "no runtime mutation path — no auto-add, no fallback"; changing `allowed_pubkeys` requires `./agentbox.sh rebuild`.
- Failure: a publisher key is compromised; the operator cannot cut it off at the relay for a build-deploy cycle while its events are admitted, stored and broadcast (AB-13.2); only the second, independent inbox boundary refuses.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (flagged as NOT IMPLEMENTED in the diagram, not one of the four markers)

### F-07 — Memory has no backup, no RPO/RTO, and pod deletion never revokes the RuVector copy
- Topics: AB-20.7, AB-20.10
- Evidence: "the VisionClaw Solid Pod deleteAgentMemory() has NO REVERSE TOMBSTONE into RuVector, so deleting the pod copy does not revoke the RuVector-held agent memory. No point-in-time RuVector backup exists… no cross-store consistent restore, RPO or RTO for memory today." Plus the index law: bulk delete degrades HNSW recall silently, "no error anywhere".
- Failure: a client requests erasure; the pod copy is deleted; the RuVector copy persists and stays searchable — a privacy breach performed on request. Separately, an index-degrading maintenance or store loss destroys learned memory irrecoverably.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE D5)

### F-08 — Losing the JSONL URN-mapping store permanently orphans cross-repo identity
- Topics: AB-17.4, AB-17.6, AB-14.13
- Evidence: "for execution and kg the local is a fresh sha256-12 with no algebraic inverse. Recovery is store-dependent, so store loss is identity loss. No recovery receipt exists for the durable store" (path `/var/lib/agentbox/code-harness/bc20-urn-mappings.jsonl`); `roundTrips()` proves the algebra against a fresh in-memory store only.
- Failure: the mappings file is truncated or lost (same-UID writable per R-2); every historic execution/kg crossing becomes permanently unmappable and VisionClaw→agentbox lookups silently drop (B04 drop-and-log).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, PROTOCOL-registry)

### F-09 — Loom serves a stale generation forever, and promotion is blocked by the old baked vault
- Topics: AB-24.13, AB-24.3, AB-24.11
- Evidence: "a generation past stale_after is STILL SERVED, honestly labelled — staleness is carried, never enforced (loom-domain/src/model.rs:459-462)"; Debt: generation `visionGraph@015ca2c1f` is built, promoted and verified but "the door still serves `visionGraph@ae913f93…`, and the `vault` 0.1.0 baked into the agentbox image predates the portable-records file, so it cannot produce a promotable bundle" (ADR-141:138-147) — the source fix reaches a running vault only at the next image rebuild.
- Failure: consumers ground against a months-stale ontology and client-facing answers cite superseded classes; the already-observed failure shape (2026-09-09, a scaffold served the blockchain class `Node` to a non-ontology query with zero completion tokens) recurs with no error anywhere.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt; ADR-141 status Proposed)

### F-10 — The audit hash chain cannot see deletion at its tail
- Topics: AB-14.9
- Evidence: "deletion at the tail is the ONE mode a bare chain cannot see (audit-chain.js:13-16)"; `readTail` (audit-chain.js:195) resumes from the newest file, so a truncated tail also moves the resume point.
- Failure: anyone with write access to the JSONL (every devuser process, per R-2) deletes the last N spend/authority records; verification passes on the shortened chain and the incident's earliest actions vanish from evidence.
- Confidence: high (stated); exploitation is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — Marketplace Deny/Revoke are non-federated: peers never learn a grant died
- Topics: AB-13.16, AB-15.9
- Evidence: "38303 Deny and 38305 Revocation are deliberately NOT federated (point-to-point only), so a peer that federates the other four kinds never learns of a denial or a revocation (agentbox.toml:313)."
- Failure: a provider revokes a compromised or over-consumed grant; federated peers keep the advertisement/grant alive and consumers there keep transacting against a dead grant.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-12 — Skill routing egresses the prompt by default, and coverage of the operator's synced claude.ai skill descriptions is unanswered
- Topics: AB-22.2
- Evidence: "router jev is the default… a turn that routes at all and misses the cascade leaves the container" (DEBT); `loadLoadableExtras` adds the operator's synced claude.ai skills as candidate rubrics (lib/skill-route.cjs:276, merged at :443-450); OPEN: "whether that acceptance also covers the descriptions of the operator's synced claude.ai skills, now sent as candidate rubrics, is not recorded" (ADR-2091:25); the drift note records the shipped judge backend as `subq`, "a cloud backend" (agentbox.toml:827,850).
- Failure: a client-confidential prompt plus the operator's private skill inventory is sent to an external judge backend on ordinary turns, beyond what ADR-2090 accepted ("skill routing ONLY").
- Confidence: high on the mechanics; medium that this alone constitutes the incident.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, OPEN)

### F-13 — Colloquy knowledge units can never be retired or superseded by any wired verb
- Topics: AB-22.7, AB-22.8
- Evidence: "no code path in this crate or colloquy-mcp's six verbs (query/propose/confirm/flag/reflect/status) currently WRITES UnitStatus::Retired or Superseded; only tests set them directly. The 'signed decision' retirement door ADR-2085/ADR-2086 describe is not yet wired."
- Failure: a knowledge unit proven wrong stays servable forever (Disputed is "ranked down, never hidden"); agents keep consuming disproven guidance while governance believes a retirement lever exists.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN)

### F-14 — Three spend ledgers never reconcile: no number answers total spend
- Topics: AB-15.6, AB-15.2
- Evidence: "spend is accounted in three stores that never reconcile: the flock-guarded cost_cap ledger file (services/agentbox-ops/src/cost_cap/ledger.rs:75), the pod-held sats balance behind GET /v1/pay/balance (routes/payments.js:205), and the marketplace token_budget carried on a grant (lib/llm-marketplace.js:85). No surface sums them, so no single number answers what this container has spent."
- Failure: budget decisions are made against one store while spend accrues in the others; an over-budget condition or theft is invisible until a manual reconciliation.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT)

### F-15 — Under role_isolation the dream governance round trip stops silently, and the port can never sign the kinds it publishes
- Topics: AB-23.20, AB-23.17, AB-14.16
- Evidence: "devuser cannot open an ab-identity 0400 file, so load_key warns agent key unavailable and the round trip is SKIPPED, fail-open" (governance.rs:903-911); "the 31400 panel and 31402 cases this engine signs are exactly the kinds the port refuses to grant" (acl.rs:65-73); "As written, the port can never sign what AB-23.17 sends"; the W3b cutover is owed (ADR-2122:224-225).
- Failure: the flag is flipped at the boot rehearsal; nights keep running and every panel, case, question and veto alert silently stops being published — a veto on a dangerous night never reaches the operator, and nothing errors because the round trip is fail-open by design.
- Confidence: high on the mechanics; medium that the flag gets flipped before the cutover.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, Open)

## agentbox#3 / critical

### F-01 — Every container secret is ambient to every process, and the fix ships switched off
- Topics: AB-36 intro, AB-36.6, AB-34.6, AB-27.7
- Evidence: AB-36 states "every secret in the container reached every process … every long-running program ran as devuser (uid 1000), so a 0600 key file protected nothing from a co-resident agent", and `[security].role_isolation` "ships **false**" (`agentbox.toml:2414`). Even with the flag on, 37 DEVUSER_CLASS names stay ambient by exception Q8, including `BRIDGE_TOKEN`, `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY` and "the ssh-agent socket path that grants use of every key the agent holds" (`env-classes.json:191`, `env-classes.json:90-92`, ADR-2122:158-159). With the flag off the chain producers and faucets "run user=devuser" reading keys straight from at-rest paths (`flake.nix:3051`, `flake.nix:3086`, `run-producer.sh:70-71`).
- Failure: any one compromised or buggy MCP tool (the fleet spawns many stdio servers into the agent's environment) reads the sovereign Nostr key, the forum operator key, the break-glass bearer, the management API key and the chain signer keys, and impersonates the organisation or moves value in the same second. This is the default shipped state.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open — activation gated on owner receipt; Debt Q8 for the 37 exceptions)

### F-02 — Host Docker socket is bind-mounted and world-accessible, and the activation receipt does not gate on it
- Topics: AB-36.7
- Evidence: "the host Docker socket inode stays world-accessible until the host chmods it. The socket is a bind of the host inode that earlier boots widened, and skipping the chmod under the flag does not narrow it (`docker-compose.override.yml:116-117`)" while "the host half records the mode for the owner without gating on it (`role-isolation-rehearsal.host.sh:18-19`)"; ADR-2122:142 "counts `degraded:docker-socket` as a failure". The flag-on boot only "detect[s] and record[s] `degraded:docker-socket`" (`entrypoint-unified.sh:605`).
- Failure: a PASS rehearsal receipt (the sole activation condition for ADR-2122) can be issued while the socket is world-accessible; any process in the container that can reach the socket path controls the Docker daemon and is root on the host, past every role boundary in AB-36.
- Confidence: high (contradiction between the ADR's pass criterion and the rehearsal is stated); the exploit path via the socket is inferred from standard Docker behaviour
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open R2)

### F-03 — Shipped image bakes `NOPASSWD: ALL` sudoers for devuser while the same flake claims no runtime escalation path
- Topics: AB-36.7
- Evidence: built with `role_isolation` off — "which is how it ships" — the image "keeps devuser in `wheel` (`flake.nix:3988`) and bakes a `NOPASSWD: ALL` drop-in (`bake-devuser-privilege.sh:44-47`, called at `flake.nix:4002`)"; "The same flake says the setuid wrapper is gone and there is no runtime escalation path (`flake.nix:3756-3760`)". The grant "is inert only because `no-new-privileges` is set (`docker-compose.yml:129`) and no setuid sudo ships", and an image built off but booted on merely "records `degraded:devuser-sudo` rather than repairing it (`entrypoint-unified.sh:642`)".
- Failure: run the image through any path that drops compose's `no-new-privileges` (plain `docker run`, a scheduler) combined with any setuid sudo appearing (base-image drift, a later package), and devuser becomes root with no password — voiding every boundary in AB-36. The build-time/boot-time flag mismatch also means a flag-on boot of a flag-off build runs partially unisolated by design, logged only.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-04 — https-bridge serves wildcard CORS in front of a management-API-key-injecting proxy
- Topics: AB-30.4, AB-30.3
- Evidence: the proxy "load_mgmt_key() — tries /var/lib/agentbox/secrets/mgmt-key … then MANAGEMENT_API_KEY env" and forwards "with Authorization Bearer <key> if found" (`setup/server/src/main.rs:166-184`), and sets "Access-Control-Allow-Origin * AND Access-Control-Allow-Credentials true (`https-proxy.js:208-211`)". It is published loopback-only (`https-proxy.js:31-33`); no origin allowlist or CSRF protection is mentioned anywhere.
- Failure: the authors' DIVERGENCE note says only that the header pair "cannot both take effect" for credentialed requests — the worse consequence is the unmarked half: any webpage in the operator's browser can make a non-credentialed fetch to `localhost:<bridge-port>/api/proxy/...`; the proxy injects the real management key server-side and `ACAO: *` lets the page read the response. Any website the operator visits gets full management-API access (payments routes included).
- Confidence: medium (the fetch/CORS chain is inferred; the key injection, wildcard header and loopback publish are all stated)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partly — yes (DIVERGENCE) for the header contradiction; no for the drive-by-key-injection consequence

### F-05 — Spend-capable keys persist devuser-readable on the host bind in both modes, so the custody programme's own claim is not achieved
- Topics: AB-34.3, AB-34.6, AB-32.4
- Evidence: "the migrate step copies that legacy file once and never deletes it, so a spend-capable key stays on the host-visible bind in both modes until a manual purge (ADR-2122:212-215)"; the live manifest still sets the faucet key to the workspace path (`agentbox.toml:1825`, `run-faucet.sh:27`), the workspace files remain "legacy twins" (`role-accounts.json:78`), and the chain signer key sits at `/var/lib/agentbox/secrets/sidestr-dreamlab.key` (`sidechain/README.md:25`) readable by devuser with the flag off (`flake.nix:3051`).
- Failure: even after full role-isolation activation, the faucet treasury key (and each chain's signer key pre-activation, and the twins post-activation) is readable by every devuser process; a co-resident tool grants itself DREAM/sats or exfiltrates a spend-capable key. The business text's "only the program that needs a key can read it" overstates what is built.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, AB-34.3)

### F-06 — The P21 real-money gate is enforced as field presence only
- Topics: AB-32.3
- Evidence: "case 3 enforces only that a mainnet parent carries a `p21Receipt` field. ADR-2103 D4 asks for a CI check that the receipt *resolves*, a second check forbidding a level-1 chain on a mainnet parent, and a node that refuses to open one; none is built (`ADR-2103:229-230`)"; the test branch is at `tests/config/sidechain-genesis.test.sh:86`.
- Failure: a future chain sealed against `btc`/`xbt` with a garbage or stale receipt passes CI; the only control standing between the estate and real value — the one AB-31.9 says "cannot start until the gate exists AS CODE" — is a schema-key existence check.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-07 — The sealed chain does not commit its parent or containment, contradicting ADR-2103 D3
- Topics: AB-32.2
- Evidence: ADR-2103 D3 states parent, headerProfile, currencyPin, cashOut, pegConfirmations and refundBlocks "are committed as a `pin:` record in the genesis coinbase (`ADR-2103:73-74`)", but the seal established upstream commits only id/pegs/genesisTime/signer witness, "every containment field" is not committed (`ADR-2103:207-208`), the pin is unbuilt and the upstream proposal "has not been filed (`ADR-2103:213`)"; the binding is "the committed document plus its genesisHash" (`sidechain/README.md:77`).
- Failure: a third-party verifier cannot bind the running chain to its declared parent or currency pin from chain data alone — that binding rests on trusting the git mirror's document. The "a third party can verify" claim (AB-32 business text) holds only for the four committed fields; a dispute over parent/containment resolves to document provenance, not cryptography.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-08 — Role isolation fails open at boot when its artefacts are missing
- Topics: AB-36.2, AB-36.3
- Evidence: `ab_role_isolation_ready` checks "roles config and plan present (`role-custody.sh:59`)"; when not, "ROLE-ISOLATION-UNAVAILABLE: boot as flag off (`entrypoint-unified.sh:357`)"; delivery "fails open with a counted, grep-able marker (`role-custody.sh:11-14`)". This is presented as design rationale with no risk marker.
- Failure: with the flag on in production, a partially baked image, a pruned `/etc/agentbox/role-secrets.tsv`, or tampering boots the container fully unisolated while every manifest and dashboard says `role_isolation = true`. The only signal is a boot-log marker; nothing halts service or forces a re-rehearsal before secrets flow again.
- Confidence: high (behaviour is explicit)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — stated as deliberate design, never flagged as a risk

### F-09 — Fresh-install seed enables memory learning and SEO watch while claiming every learning gate is off
- Topics: AB-30.3
- Evidence: the seed comment claims all learning/hygiene gates off (`setup/server/src/main.rs:40-42`, `agentbox.default.toml:5-6`), but the shipped default sets `[memory_learning] enabled = true` (`agentbox.default.toml:451`) and `[seo_watch] enabled = true` (`agentbox.default.toml:823`).
- Failure: a fresh production install configured through the setup server starts collecting/learning data the operator was explicitly told was disabled — a silent data-governance divergence discovered only after data has been gathered, and the false comment propagates through every fresh-config flow.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-10 — The settlement chain is halted in production with a three-way contradiction about its state
- Topics: AB-34.5, AB-32 (For developers)
- Evidence: `[sidechain].enabled` ships `false`, "paused after an ENOSPC block-persistence failure" (`agentbox.toml:1818-1820`); the second chain's table says `enabled = true` and "live 2026-10-03" (`agentbox.toml:1836`) but is dominated and inert; the README says that table "ships `enabled = false`" (`config/sidechain/README.md:169`) and three places still describe the mirror as GitHub Pages (`flake.nix:3065`, `mirror-sync.sh:37`, `README.md:164`) though it is a plain Git checkout. Re-enable is conditioned on "state recovery and a merged upstream fix" that nobody has landed.
- Failure: the settlement chain, its faucet (forum-member DREAM grants) and both mirrors are down indefinitely; an operator or engineer reading the child table or README concludes the chain is live (or that flipping the child gate fixes it) and takes wrong incident action. Block persistence already failed once on disk exhaustion and the root cause is closed only by a pause, not a fix.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, multiple)

### F-11 — The environment-classification CI gate is failing at the verified revision
- Topics: AB-36.6
- Evidence: "`env-secret-inventory.js --check` fails here with 24 `UNCLASSIFIED` names read in the entrypoint … and the secret-shaped `FACTRAIL_API_KEY`, so the 'every name in exactly one class' claim above is not enforced by a green gate (`env-secret-inventory.js:235`, `entrypoint-unified.sh:2774`, `:2484`, `:2919`)."
- Failure: either the gate is not merge-blocking (the classification regime is decorative at HEAD) or the tree is knowingly committed red; either way, when `role_isolation` flips, at least 24 names — one visibly secret-shaped — silently stay ambient because the scrub list is incomplete, defeating the completeness premise the custody design rests on.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-12 — A TUI save silently resets operator-edited model-router settings, including the cost ceiling
- Topics: AB-28.10
- Evidence: only `enabled` round-trips through the flat TUI state (`tui_read.rs:127`); the write "emits … every OTHER key a FIXED literal (provider 'openrouter', quality_bar 0.50, cost_ceiling_usd_per_mtok 0, … `tui_sections.rs:193-200`)", and the material notes "there is no read-back-and-preserve path" — unlike the consultants' model, which ADR-2031 protects (AB-28.3).
- Failure: an operator who hand-raises the cost ceiling or changes provider/quality bar loses those values on the next unrelated TUI save; the router reverts to ceiling 0 and the OpenRouter path, silently removing a spend control on a console that executes paid calls.
- Confidence: high on the reset; medium on the spend impact (the semantics of ceiling 0 are unstated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT)

### F-13 — The Gaussian-splatting gate is a trap, and the real workload runs ungated around it
- Topics: AB-27.9
- Evidence: enabling `skills.spatial_and_3d.gaussian_splatting` makes Nix evaluation "throw … lichtfeldRev is the placeholder '0000…0' (`3dgs-stack.nix:85-97`)", while validator E006 "additionally requires gpu.backend='local-cuda' (`agentbox-config-validate.js:222-227`)" — i.e. the validator demands exactly the configuration that then breaks the build. Meanwhile "the actually-used lichtfeld-studio skill is completely independent of this gate: Claude spawns lichtfeld_mcp_bridge.py … to a manually built … LichtFeld-Studio at localhost port 45677 — never baked … never gated" (`skills/lichtfeld-studio/SKILL.md:50`).
- Failure: (a) any operator who follows the documented gate and rebuilds gets a broken evaluation; (b) production 3DGS work flows through an untracked, manually built workspace binary with no manifest gate, no provenance, no supervision and no off-state — a standing bypass of the manifest-gated dispatch this whole area claims.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE) — though the validator-demands-what-throws combination is drawn by the reader, not stated as one trap

### F-14 — Operator consoles bind 0.0.0.0 on the shared sidecar network and their credentials live in the shared devuser environment
- Topics: AB-27.5, AB-27.6
- Evidence: code-server binds "0.0.0.0:8080 (still not 127.0.0.1 — the docker-compose loopback publish only constrains host->container, not bridge->container on visionclaw_network)" (`flake.nix:2779`); jupyter-lab runs `--ip=0.0.0.0 --port=8888` (`flake.nix:2390`) with `JUPYTER_TOKEN` "export[ed] before supervisord starts" (`entrypoint-unified.sh:1029-1054`) — and AB-36 states supervisord "passes its environment to every child"; `CODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` is written to a devuser-owned file (`entrypoint-unified.sh:984-1015`).
- Failure: every co-resident sidecar on `visionclaw_network` (comfyui external, gui-tools, openmed, voice) gets a permanent attackable console surface; inside the container, every process already holds the token/password, so the consoles' authentication protects only against outsiders. A Jupyter token in the ambient environment is code-execution-as-devuser available to any tool.
- Confidence: high on the facts; medium on consequence
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partly — the code-server binding is flagged in-material; the ambient-token consequence is unmarked

### F-15 — Passwordless VNC mirrors the XR display
- Topics: AB-27.13
- Evidence: "x11vnc (priority 20) mirrors :3 on VNC 5904, **-nopw** (`xr-runtime/supervisord.conf:41-49`)". No publish/binding for 5904 is stated anywhere in the material; every other sidecar in this area publishes its ports.
- Failure: if 5904 is published or reachable on the shared network (the pattern of the other sidecars), any peer gets unauthenticated view of — and input injection into — the Godot/Monado session, with no credential to brute-force at all.
- Confidence: low, inferred — the `-nopw` is stated, the reachability is not
- Severity: medium (conditional on reachability)
- Marked by authors: no

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Port publishing and network reachability for the sidecars** — the compose files / Dockerfiles and host firewall for ports 5904 (VNC), 8443/8444 (voice), the speech stack, and the external ComfyUI 8188 are not shown; F-14 and F-15's real severity depends on them.
2. **Whether the CI gates are merge-blocking** — is `env-secret-inventory.js --check` (red at HEAD, F-11), the genesis test and the licensing check enforced by branch protection, or advisory?
3. **Secret minting strength and log hygiene** — the entropy and generation of `JUPYTER_TOKEN`, `CODE_SERVER_PASSWORD`, the mgmt-key and `BRIDGE_TOKEN`, and whether any of them or the ROLE-class values reach logs (supervisord environment capture, crash dumps, the imagemagick-mcp log).
4. **What the tests actually assert** — only summaries of `role-isolation-rehearsal.sh`, the golden tests and the supervisor drift test are given; their real coverage (e.g. the socket-dir rename race, scrub completeness, receipt-write failure paths) cannot be judged.
5. **Multi-instance behaviour** — fixed ports (9876/9877/8188/8888/8080), fixed paths (`/opt/agentbox`, `$WORKSPACE`), the uid range 960–979, and single-instance assumptions in the custody and chain state when two agentbox stacks share one host or network.

## agentbox#3 / premortem

# Post-mortem — agentbox custody/security incident (T+12 months)

**Summary (reconstructed).** A client-facing compromise ran through the agent container. The custody boundary built to prevent it (ADR-2122) was never activated: `[security].role_isolation` ships `false`, the only committed rehearsal receipt is STAGED with the flag off, and even the built form leaves spend-capable keys readable by the agent user. Everything below is drawn from the record's own citations; where the trigger of a chain is not stated in the material, it is marked inferred.

---

### R-1 — Spend-capable chain keys readable by the agent user in *both* modes: the never-deleted legacy twins and the flag-off secrets-volume chown
- Topics: AB-34.3, AB-34.6, AB-36.3, AB-36 (For developers), AB-34.2
- Chain of events:
  1. An agent tool or skill server runs as devuser (uid 1000) — the shipped mode, since `role_isolation` is `false` (`agentbox.toml:2414`) and with the flag off every chain program itself runs as devuser (`flake.nix:3051`, `flake.nix:3086`).
  2. With the flag off, the boot chowns the `agentbox-secrets` volume root to devuser (the skip at `config/entrypoint-unified.sh:548` applies only under the flag), so devuser reads the sidestr signer key (`config/sidechain/run-producer.sh:70`) and the faucet treasury key at its workspace default (`config/sidechain/run-faucet.sh:27`, `agentbox.toml:1825`) directly.
  3. The operator later flips the flag on. `ab_custody_migrate` hands the volume copies to their roles at 0400, but the legacy workspace twins are copied once and never deleted, and stay devuser-readable "by design until a manual purge" (`docs/adr/ADR-2122-...md:212-215`) — the same read still works.
  4. A holder of the signer key is indistinguishable from the chain's signer: clients accept any mirror whose document signer matches the announcement's author and hold it to the signer's tip (AB-34.2), and tips are announced as kind 33333 after every block (`config/sidechain/README.md:123`).
  5. The attacker produces blocks, runs the faucet (DREAM and test sats), and — if PRD-024's value phase had begun — moves real value through the same keys, past a p21 gate that is a field-presence stub (F-05).
- Evidence: "a spend-capable key stays on the host-visible bind in both modes until a manual purge" (`docs/adr/ADR-2122-...md:212-215`, restated in AB-36.3); "a 0600 key file protected nothing from a co-resident agent" (AB-36, For developers); flag-off custody shape AB-34.6 (`flake.nix:3051`, `run-producer.sh:70-71`, `run-faucet.sh:27`); mirror trust rule (AB-34.2, `config/sidechain/README.md`).
- Warning signs visible today: the manifest itself records the keys as still readable in both modes; the flag ships off with the only receipt STAGED flag-false (`docs/estate-closeout/x1-rehearsal-20261003T090708Z.json:4`, `:8`).
- Confidence: high that the keys are readable as described; the composite exploitation is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — The ADR-2122 boundary was never activated: every secret still reaches every process, and agents keep MANAGEMENT_API_KEY, BRIDGE_TOKEN and the ssh-agent socket by exception
- Topics: AB-36 (For developers), AB-36.4, AB-36.6, AB-36.7, AB-31 (For the business)
- Chain of events:
  1. Activation requires a flag-on PASS rehearsal receipt from the owner's rebuild (`docs/adr/ADR-2122-...md:135-143`); the only committed receipt is STAGED with the flag false, and the flag ships false (`agentbox.toml:2414`).
  2. Compose feeds `.env` to PID 1 and supervisord passes its environment to every child; every long-running program runs as devuser, so "a 0600 key file protected nothing from a co-resident agent" (AB-36, citing AB-16.1/AB-16.7).
  3. Even flag-on, W3b consumers are owed — JunkieJarvis, the live-mirror hook, the gateway, dream-engine (`docs/adr/ADR-2122-...md:224-225`) — and 37 DEVUSER_CLASS names stay ambient by Q8, including provider keys, `BRIDGE_TOKEN`, `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY` and "the ssh-agent socket path that grants use of every key the agent holds" (`config/custody/env-classes.json:191`, `:199`, `:90`, `:92`; `ADR-2122...md:158-159`).
  4. The in-container attack surface spawns third-party code: the MCP fleet runs stdio servers and `uvx blender-mcp` (`agentbox/mcp/mcp.json:152`, AB-27.2). One compromised server inherits PID 1's environment, calls the management API with the operator key (e.g. the X-API-Key routes, `management-api/routes/comfyui.js:23`, and the payments surface the estate "already refuses work it has not been paid for" on, `docs/proposals/sovereign-settlement.md:51-55`), and signs as the operator through ssh-agent.
- Evidence: as cited above, plus the classification gate currently failing with 24 UNCLASSIFIED names (F-07), so the mechanism meant to keep this list honest is red at HEAD.
- Warning signs visible today: flag false; STAGED receipt; W3b open; `env-secret-inventory.js --check` failing (`scripts/ci/env-secret-inventory.js:235`).
- Confidence: high that the state is as described; the exploitation vector is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — devuser to host root: the world-accessible Docker socket inode plus a baked NOPASSWD sudo grant held inert by two removable guards
- Topics: AB-36.1, AB-36.7
- Chain of events:
  1. The host Docker socket is bind-mounted and its inode stays world-accessible from earlier boots; the container cannot narrow it, only record `degraded:docker-socket` (`docker-compose.override.yml:116-117`, `config/entrypoint-unified.sh:605`).
  2. The shipped image — built with `role_isolation` off — keeps devuser in group `wheel` (`flake.nix:3988`) and bakes a `NOPASSWD: ALL` drop-in (`config/bake-devuser-privilege.sh:44-47`, called at `flake.nix:4002`); it is inert only because `no-new-privileges` is set (`docker-compose.yml:129`) and no setuid sudo ships (`flake.nix:3756-3760`).
  3. A devuser process reaches the socket; the Docker daemon is root on the host — the stated reason uid 965 is reserved (`config/role-accounts.json:6`) — and mounts the host root or any volume, including the secrets volume and the client-data workspace.
  4. Independently, any change that supplies a setuid sudo or drops `no-new-privileges` on a flag-off build "turns devuser back into root, past every role boundary" (`config/entrypoint-unified.sh:642`; rehearsal check (f), `scripts/activation/role-isolation-rehearsal.sh:591-592`).
- Evidence: as cited; also the visible disagreement that ADR-2122 counts `degraded:docker-socket` as a failure (`ADR-2122...md:142`) while the host rehearsal only records the mode without gating (`scripts/activation/role-isolation-rehearsal.host.sh:18-19`).
- Warning signs visible today: the reserved-uid comment; the sudo drop-in in the shipped build path; the record/recount mismatch on the socket.
- Confidence: high that both bypasses exist exactly as described; the triggering change is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Chain pause after ENOSPC leaves a recovery deadlock and a public-corruption path
- Topics: AB-34.1, AB-34.3, AB-34.5
- Evidence: `[sidechain].enabled = false`, "paused after the ENOSPC block-persistence failure; retain state and backups" (`agentbox.toml:1818-1820`), dominating all children (`flake.nix:282-284`); the producer refuses to start when the custody copy is ahead (`run-producer.sh:63-65`; faucet `run-faucet.sh:42-44`); the mirror copies `blocks.dat` every pass (`mirror-sync.sh:72`) and commits and pushes any changed file (`mirror-sync.sh:76-79`).
- Failure: a disk-full recurrence truncates `blocks.dat`; the truncated file counts as "changed", is committed and pushed to the public mirror, publishing corrupt chain data to clients; or the ahead-guard refuses start indefinitely after state divergence.
- Confidence: high (the pause is fact; truncation-publishing is inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift/Open in AB-34.5)

### F-02 — `SIDESTR_ALLOW_UNPINNED=1` runs dirty, devuser-writable consensus code with no bound
- Topics: AB-34.1
- Evidence: `run-producer.sh:85-93`; the record's own DEBT: "while the override is set, the exposure the bake closed is back, a dirty checkout runs, and nothing bounds how long it stays set" (`run-producer.sh:87-93`).
- Failure: the override is set for a test and forgotten; the workspace `siding.mjs`/schema are editable by the running user, block rules change under the chain, and clients verify the divergent blocks as signer-valid.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-03 — The `blaketestnode` pin bump bypassed the pins file's own produced-block rule
- Topics: AB-34.1
- Evidence: rule at `config/sidechain/upstream-pins:5-7`; the bump at `:10` on "operator-accepted active development head" (commit `47e187934`), baked at `lib/sidestr-upstream.nix:57-60`.
- Failure: consensus head changed on acceptance alone; if it diverges from the rev that produced blocks, the two chains' engines disagree — exactly the class of change the rule exists to prevent.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-04 — Genesis commits neither parent nor containment; `chain.json` edits change no hash and propagate to the public mirror
- Topics: AB-32.2, AB-34.2
- Evidence: ADR-2103 D3 claims on-seal binding (`ADR-2103...md:73-74`) vs the amendment that the pin is unbuilt (`:208`); the binding is "the committed document plus its genesisHash" (`config/sidechain/README.md:77`); the mirror replaces `chain.json` atomically every pass (`mirror-sync.sh:69-70`) while the chain event is never replaced (`mirror-sync.sh:40-64`).
- Failure: a signer-key holder (R-1) edits parent/containment consistently; genesisHash and the immutable chain event stay valid; signer-matching clients accept the edited document from the mirror.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, Open)

### F-05 — The mainnet regulatory gate is a field-presence stub
- Topics: AB-32.3, AB-31.9
- Evidence: the test only checks that `p21Receipt` is present (`tests/config/sidechain-genesis.test.sh:86`); resolving the receipt, forbidding a level-1 chain on a mainnet parent, and a refusing node are all recorded as not built (`ADR-2103...md:229-230`); PRD-024 says P4 cannot start until the gate exists as code (`sovereign-settlement.md:329-330`).
- Failure: a mainnet-parent document with any non-empty receipt string passes CI; the real-value phase proceeds past its own stated precondition.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-06 — The identity port hands a raw secret to devuser by grant
- Topics: AB-36.5
- Evidence: `mirror_key` with `secret true` granted to uid 1000 (`config/custody/identity-port-acl.json:42`); the record notes the tension "recorded once, in AB-16"; `NEVER_GRANTABLE_KINDS` covers kinds only (`identity_port/acl.rs:71-73`).
- Failure: even flag-on, any devuser process calls `mirror_key` and receives the live-mirror child private key, defeating the port's callers-never-hold-keys model.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (tension, recorded in AB-16)

### F-07 — The secret-classification gate is failing at HEAD
- Topics: AB-36.6
- Evidence: `env-secret-inventory.js --check` fails with 24 UNCLASSIFIED names read in the entrypoint (`scripts/ci/env-secret-inventory.js:235`; `config/entrypoint-unified.sh:2774`, `:2484`, `:2919`), including the secret-shaped `FACTRAIL_API_KEY`.
- Failure: a red gate stops binding; new secret-shaped names join the ambient devuser environment unclassified and unreviewed, widening R-2 silently.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-08 — The committed rehearsal receipt predates the rule it would now fail
- Topics: AB-36.7
- Evidence: the receipt's uid table is "derived from /etc/agentbox.toml" from an earlier commit (`docs/estate-closeout/x1-rehearsal-20261003T090708Z.json:16`, `:26`); the rehearsal now requires the baked table and plan (`scripts/activation/role-isolation-rehearsal.sh:41-43`).
- Failure: activation deliberation cites the committed receipt as evidence although it was produced under a superseded scheme.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-09 — Unauthenticated VNC in the xr-runtime sidecar
- Topics: AB-27.13
- Evidence: `x11vnc` mirrors display `:3` on VNC 5904 with `-nopw` (`xr-runtime/supervisord.conf:41-49`).
- Failure: if the port is published or reachable from the bridge/host (unstated in this material), anyone obtains the XR session's screen and input.
- Confidence: medium (inferred exposure; `-nopw` is stated)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — code-server is reachable container-wide on the shared bridge network
- Topics: AB-27.6
- Evidence: binds `0.0.0.0:8080` (`flake.nix:2779`); the record notes the compose loopback publish "only constrains host->container, not bridge->container on visionclaw_network".
- Failure: any sidecar on `visionclaw_network` reaches the VS Code UI over `/home/devuser/workspace`; a captured or brute-forced password yields the whole workspace including client data.
- Confidence: high (exposure stated; compromise inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT note)

### F-11 — Setup's shipped default enables learning gates its own header says are off
- Topics: AB-30.3
- Evidence: `[memory_learning] enabled = true` (`setup/agentbox.default.toml:451`) and `[seo_watch] enabled = true` (`:823`) against the header claim that every learning/hygiene gate ships off (`:5-6`, `setup/server/src/main.rs:40-42`).
- Failure: a fresh client deployment captures learning/SEO-watch data from day one while the operator, reading the header, believes the gates are off.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-12 — A TUI save silently rewrites model-routing configuration to literals
- Topics: AB-28.10
- Evidence: `tui_sections.rs:193-200` emits fixed literals for provider, quality_bar, cost_ceiling, trajectory and assets_dir; no read-back-and-preserve path exists, unlike `[consultants.*].model` (ADR-2031).
- Failure: an operator edits an unrelated field in the TUI; cost ceiling and provider reset to the literals, changing routing spend with no indication.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT)

### F-13 — Level-2 cosigning runs the availability-tolerant round, not the reviewed BFT shape
- Topics: AB-34.5, AB-35.1
- Evidence: "sidestr-round 0.1.0's cosign co-signs with the JS signers live" (`docs/proposals/sovereign-settlement.md:336`); the drift note that what shipped is "upstream's availability-tolerant round (not the reviewed BFT shape)" (AB-35, For developers); the 2f+1 trap the review warns against (`REVIEW-kofn-consensus-gpt6-astra.md:56`).
- Failure: the live round finalises under fewer honest signers than the reviewed thresholds require; two conflicting cosigns both complete — the safety violation the q/k split exists to prevent.
- Confidence: medium (live use stated; the round's actual thresholds are unstated — inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift in AB-35; the safety gap itself unmarked)

### F-14 — Kind codecs are unproven against upstream, and the corrected wire profile has no allocation
- Topics: AB-31.8, AB-35.3
- Evidence: "acceptance is OPEN — this allocation is not fixture-backed" (`docs/PROTOCOL-registry.md:186-189`); upstream shapes are provisional (`:180-184`); the registry still allocates the ephemeral `23510`-`23514` band to the shape the review rejected, and the corrected stored-kinds profile is unallocated (AB-35.3 Debt).
- Failure: an upstream SPEC revision changes a field; the estate's codec silently misdecodes settlement events, with no fixture to catch it.
- Confidence: medium (mechanism stated; occurrence inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open/Debt)

### F-15 — A missing roles artefact boots the insecure shape under a flag-on manifest
- Topics: AB-36.2
- Evidence: `ab_role_isolation_ready` checks for the roles config and plan (`config/lib/role-custody.sh:59`); on absence the boot proceeds "as flag off" (`config/entrypoint-unified.sh:357`) and picks the config at exec (`:1216`, `:1222`); AB-36.2: "An image that lacks either artefact boots as if the flag were off, and says so loudly."
- Failure: after a build regression or partial image, the operator's `role_isolation = true` manifest boots the every-secret mode with only a log line distinguishing it; the boundary everyone believes in is absent for that container's lifetime.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — requires a build regression; the downgrade path itself is stated)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (framed as designed fail-open)

## nostr-rust-forum / critical

## F-01 — All relay traffic through one Durable Object instance

- Topics: NF-03.1, NF-11.1
- Evidence: "The DO is a SINGLETON: every socket goes to get_by_name main" (nostr-bbs-relay-worker/src/lib.rs:207). The DO holds all sessions, all rate limits, all caches, and the ActivityLedger in one instance (relay_do/mod.rs:83-103).
- Failure: Every WebSocket connection, every EVENT publish, every REQ subscription, and every broadcast fan-out serialises through a single DO. Cloudflare DOs have hard memory, CPU, and concurrent-connection limits. A forum with more than trivial concurrent users will hit DO eviction or throughput limits. A DO eviction drops all sessions simultaneously (only per-socket state survives via hibernation recovery, NF-11.2), and while the DO restarts every client sees a dead relay.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

## F-02 — A real 64-hex admin pubkey hardcoded in the search-worker template

- Topics: NF-07.2, NF-08.7
- Evidence: "nostr-bbs-search-worker/wrangler.toml:33 ships a REAL 64-hex pubkey" and it is "the only non-placeholder value in any template" (NF-07.2 N3). The material does not name whose key it is.
- Failure: Any deployment that does not override ADMIN_PUBKEYS in the search-worker gives admin authority over the search ingest to whoever holds the hardcoded key. Because the parse rule is shared through nostr_bbs_core (NF-07.2 N1), this identity is recognised by every worker that reads the same variable. This is an undeclared backdoor or, at minimum, an operator's personal key shipped in a public template.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (the fact is stated but not flagged as a security consequence)

## F-03 — No static admin bootstrap on a by-the-book relay or auth deployment

- Topics: NF-08.7, NF-10.7 (S2)
- Evidence: "ADMIN_PUBKEYS is read by auth and relay but declared in neither template and named in no SETUP step" (SETUP.md:119). Only nostr-bbs-search-worker/wrangler.toml:33 declares it. The relay template even asserts the opposite (nostr-bbs-relay-worker/wrangler.toml:10-13).
- Failure: An operator who follows SETUP.md ends up with a relay and auth worker that have no admin authority from the static source. The only path to admin is via whitelist.is_admin (relay) or members.is_admin (auth) — but writing those rows requires an admin action, which requires having an admin. The deployment is locked out of all admin functions: no governance registration, no moderation, no channel-zone binding.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT, DD3 / NF-08.7 N4-N5)

## F-04 — Banned user with a pre-registered device key bypasses moderation gates

- Topics: NF-03.4, NF-10.11 (KF-4, KF-9)
- Evidence: "the write gate resolves a device key to its owner, but the moderation gates that follow key on the RAW signing pubkey" (docs/security/known-findings.md:27). The admission pipeline (NF-03.4) runs is_whitelisted on effective_pubkey (step 6b) but the ban/silence gate (step 8) and trust gates (step 10) check the raw signer.
- Failure: A member registers a device key, is later banned, and continues publishing through the device key. The whitelist check resolves the device key to the (banned) owner and passes because the owner was whitelisted before the ban; the ban gate sees only the device key's raw pubkey, which was never individually banned. Every ban-gated kind (NF-03.6) is publishable.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (KF-4, KF-9)

## F-05 — SSRF guard is denylist-only with no wall-clock timeout

- Topics: NF-07.6
- Evidence: "there is NO wall-clock timeout — only redirect-count and body-size limits bound a fetch" (NF-07.6 N3). "Without PREVIEW_ALLOWED_HOSTS the guard is denylist-only and DNS-rebinding-vulnerable" (nostr-bbs-preview-worker/src/ssrf.rs:13). The template does not set it (nostr-bbs-preview-worker/wrangler.toml:13).
- Failure: The preview worker fetches arbitrary URLs. An attacker who controls DNS can resolve to a private IP after the denylist check passes (DNS rebinding). Even without rebinding, a URL that accepts the connection but never responds will consume a Worker invocation indefinitely — no timeout bounds it. Both are reachable from any browser via the public /preview endpoint.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, NF-07.6 N3-N4)

## F-06 — Governance read endpoints: authenticated equals anonymous

- Topics: NF-10.11 (KF-2, KF-2a)
- Evidence: "the governance READ endpoints verify a NIP-98 signature with no membership check, so authenticated is effectively anonymous" and "the admin-gated reviewer aggregate can be reconstructed from the un-gated decisions read" (docs/security/known-findings.md:23-24).
- Failure: Any Nostr keypair holder (anyone on the internet) can mint a valid NIP-98 token and read every governance case, every decision, and every broker outcome. The reviewer telemetry (admin-gated at NF-02.10) can be reconstructed from the ungated decisions endpoint. For a system whose governance surface is supposed to be member-visible and admin-controlled, this makes the entire governance record public.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (KF-2, KF-2a)

## F-07 — Shared replay D1 is a single point of failure for all NIP-98 authentication

- Topics: NF-02.5, NF-08.4
- Evidence: "every worker binds its replay store to the SAME database (nostr-bbs-auth) so cross-worker replay is detected" (nostr-bbs-auth-worker/src/auth.rs:9-11).
