The relay, pod, and search workers all bind REPLAY_DB to nostr-bbs-auth (nostr-bbs-relay-worker/wrangler.toml:85, nostr-bbs-pod-worker/wrangler.toml:25, nostr-bbs-search-worker/wrangler.toml:25).
- Failure: The nostr-bbs-auth D1 database becomes a dependency of every authenticated REST endpoint across four workers. A D1 outage, a regional failover, or even reaching the D1 free-tier row limit on the replay table causes all NIP-98 verification to fail simultaneously — every admin action, every governance read, every pod write, every search ingest. The replay table is INSERT-only with no documented purge, so it grows unboundedly.
- Confidence: high (SPOF is certain; unbounded growth is inferred from no documented purge path)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

## F-08 — ENCRYPTION_ENABLED undeclared in every wrangler.toml: zone encryption silently off

- Topics: NF-08.2, NF-08.8
- Evidence: "ENCRYPTION_ENABLED is read from env at zone_config.rs:86 but declared in NO wrangler.toml [vars] block in any worker — a deploy that forgets it silently leaves encryption OFF" (NF-08.2 N4).
- Failure: An operator configures zones as encrypted in forum.toml, clients display the encryption affordance, but the relay never enforces the ciphertext shape check (NF-08.9) and clients never re-open gift wraps for zone-key grants (NF-05.16 N3). Plaintext posts land in what everyone believes is an encrypted zone. There is no warning, no log entry, no startup error — encryption just does not exist.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT, NF-08.2 N4)

## F-09 — RELAY_URL ships blank: NIP-42 relay-tag verification skipped by default

- Topics: NF-03.2
- Evidence: "own_relay_url unset or blank SKIPS the relay-tag check only. This fails OPEN on the defence-in-depth check" (nip42.rs:168-172). "RELAY_URL ships blank nostr-bbs-relay-worker/wrangler.toml:38".
- Failure: A kind-22242 AUTH event minted for relay A is accepted by relay B. The challenge and signature checks still hold, but the binding of the authentication to THIS relay is absent. A client that performs AUTH against one relay has that authentication accepted by a different relay deployment sharing the same challenge store, which matters when the same D1 backs multiple relay instances.
- Confidence: medium (the challenge is per-session, which limits the blast radius to deployments sharing a D1)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially (the skip is acknowledged in NF-03.2 N2, but the consequence is not called out as a production risk)

## F-10 — Schema DDL executed on every request, before authentication and CORS

- Topics: NF-02.1, NF-08.5, NF-10.11 (KF-3)
- Evidence: "ensure_schema runs its DDL on EVERY request, before the CORS short-circuit and any authentication" (docs/security/known-findings.md:26). Both workers run idempotent CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS on every cold start (schema.rs:27, lib.rs:793).
- Failure: Every unauthenticated HTTP request triggers a D1 batch containing 15+ DDL statements. This is (a) a throughput cost on the D1 free tier that scales with request volume, (b) an amplification vector — a simple GET flood costs the operator D1 writes, and (c) it runs before any rate limit in the auth worker's own sequence (the rate limit at lib.rs:174 comes AFTER the schema bootstrap at lib.rs:162).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (KF-3)

## F-11 — KV and SESSIONS are the same physical namespace: key-collision across logical uses

- Topics: NF-08.4 (O11a)
- Evidence: "KV and SESSIONS are the SAME physical namespace id — nostr-bbs-auth-worker/wrangler.toml:24 and :39" (NF-08.4 N2).
- Failure: The auth-worker uses one KV namespace for two purposes under different binding names. If the key formats overlap (rate-limit keys, session keys, or any other KV key written by one path), a write from one clobbers or corrupts the other. A rate-limit write could destroy a session; a session write could clear a rate limit. The exact key formats are not shown, so the collision is inferred from shared namespace alone.
- Confidence: medium (collision depends on key format overlap, which the material does not show)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (O11, still live)

## F-12 — Recovery sheet puts the nsec in a URL fragment: recoverable from browser history

- Topics: NF-05.8
- Evidence: "/connect magic link with the nsec in the URL FRAGMENT recovery_sheet.rs:290" and "the nsec travels in the fragment (#k=), which browsers do not send to the server" (NF-05.8).
- Failure: URL fragments are stored in browser history on Chromium and Firefox. The /connect link containing the private key sits in the history bar, recoverable by anyone with access to the machine, by browser-sync services that sync history, and by forensic tools. The material's claim of safety is true only for the HTTP request, not for the browser's local storage of the URL. A user who "shares a link" from their history bar leaks their identity key.
- Confidence: high (browser-history persistence of fragments is a known browser behaviour)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (the material explicitly frames this as safe)

## F-13 — Gift-wrap DMs: the client downloads every wrap in its inbox, then filters locally

- Topics: NF-05.9
- Evidence: "The gift-wrap half CANNOT be narrowed to one partner relay-side, so it fetches the whole wrap inbox and selects the conversation locally after unwrapping" (dm/mod.rs:636-640).
- Failure: A user with 500 correspondents downloads and NIP-44-decrypts every gift wrap ever sent to them on every sync (windowed only by GIFT_WRAP_LOOKBACK_SECS). This is an O(all-DMs) cost per page load proportional to lifetime DM volume, not to the conversation being opened. On a low-power device or a slow connection, opening one conversation triggers hundreds of cryptographic operations. The relay cannot help — it serves by p tag only.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium (performance and battery, not security — but it compounds with every new correspondent)
- Marked by authors: yes (acknowledged as "the price NIP-59 sender anonymity charges")

## F-14 — TL2 trust demotion lands directly on TL0: a trusted member loses everything in one sweep

- Topics: NF-03.8, NF-11.12
- Evidence: "ADR-2006 permits TL2 to land DIRECTLY on TL0 — one committed transition per sweep, not one rung of the ladder" (trust.rs:303-305). The sweep runs every 5 minutes.
- Failure: A member who earned TL2 (14 days, 50 reads, 10 posts, zero mod actions) goes on holiday for six months. On return, a single sweep has dropped them to TL0. They cannot create channels (TL2+), cannot edit channel metadata (TL2+), cannot delete others' events (TL3+). Re-earning TL2 requires another full 14 days. There is no warning, no intermediate step, and no way for an admin to intervene other than a manual TL3 grant (which is never auto-demoted and therefore overcorrects).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially (stated as deliberate per ADR-2006, but the operational consequence — permanent loss of earned privileges with no warning path — is not discussed)

## F-15 — Google favicon dependency: every preview embeds a google.com URL

- Topics: NF-07.7
- Evidence: "favicon via Google's s2 service, not the target site parse.rs:172" and "every preview embeds a google.com favicon URL" (NF-07.7 N2).
- Failure: Every link unfurled through the preview worker carries a favicon URL pointing at google.com/s2. Users in jurisdictions where Google is blocked see broken favicons. Users who expect the forum to be independent of third-party trackers are profiled by Google (the s2 URL encodes the target domain, leaking browsing context). If Google changes or rate-limits the s2 endpoint, every historical preview breaks simultaneously.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no (the fact is stated but not flagged as a dependency or privacy issue)

---

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **The actual key formats used in the SESSIONS and KV namespaces** — needed to confirm or dismiss F-11 (key-collision risk). If the two paths use disjoint key prefixes, the finding reduces to a maintainability concern.

2. **Cloudflare DO concurrency and memory limits in practice for this workload** — the material describes the singleton DO's in-memory state but does not profile it. Whether it saturates at 50, 500, or 5000 concurrent sessions determines whether F-01 is a day-one blocker or a scale ceiling.

3. **The replay table's growth rate and whether any purge or TTL exists** — the material describes the INSERT OR IGNORE mechanism but not the retention policy. If there is a periodic cleanup not mentioned, F-07's unbounded-growth claim weakens to a pure SPOF finding.

4. **The actual deployment's ADMIN_PUBKEYS configuration** — the templates are described, but the running production deployment may override all of them. F-02 and F-03 are template-level findings; their production severity depends on what was actually deployed.

5. **The upstream nostr crate's 0.44.7 wasm32 compatibility** — the canary tests three smokes out of five claimed features (NF-01.5). Whether nip04, nip59 and nip98 work on wasm32 at this version is a fact about the dependency, not this repo, and determines whether enabling those feature flags today would break the build.

## nostr-rust-forum / premortem

# Post-mortem — loss-of-client production incident, nostr-rust-forum estate

Basis: the twelve area documents at `verified_commit 66ca8d8c0b7d1471503f916c513089846aba27c3` (kit-compatibility record at dreamlab `ebaf16c`). No source beyond the citations in those documents. Every claim below is either stated there or explicitly flagged as inferred. Root causes are ranked by likelihood of having produced a data, custody or security failure.

---

### R-1 — A real admin pubkey ships in the only template that declares ADMIN_PUBKEYS, and no deployment guide ever sets it
- Topics: NF-07.2, NF-08.7, NF-08.8, NF-10.7 (S2), NF-06.4, NF-06.9
- Chain of events: the operator deploys per SETUP.md, which never lists `ADMIN_PUBKEYS`; the auth and relay templates do not declare it, so neither worker has a static admin bootstrap; the only template that does declare it — the search worker — ships a real 64-hex pubkey as the default; the search worker reads that var as its *only* admin source (no D1), so whoever holds that key is admin of `/ingest` on any deployment that keeps the template value; when the operator later sets `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` on relay/auth, the only example value in the repo is the shipped key, and the parse rule is shared through `nostr_bbs_core` — pasting it makes an external key static admin of the whole estate: whitelist writes and agent provisioning, 31403 decisions (an admin "admits any case"), user delete/suspend/silence, admin-only sealed-zone writes, and silent kind-42 deletion.
- Evidence: `nostr-bbs-search-worker/wrangler.toml:33` ships a real non-placeholder pubkey, "the only non-generic value in any template" (NF-08.7 N5); search-worker admin is var-only with no D1 binding (`nostr-bbs-search-worker/src/auth.rs:54`, `auth.rs:8`, NF-07.2); `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` declared in exactly one template, SETUP.md:119-123 never lists it, and the relay template asserts no reader exists while `nostr-bbs-relay-worker/src/auth.rs:185` reads it (NF-08.7 N4, DD3); admin admits any case at `nip_handlers.rs:1064` (NF-06.4); provisioning writes whitelist + registry as one batch (`governance_api.rs:420-422`, NF-06.9).
- Warning signs visible today: DD3 and S2 already file this; a live credential in a public template is the single loudest signal in the material.
- Confidence: medium — the shipped key and the plumbing are stated; that it was copied or its holder acted is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Zone encryption is silently off because ENCRYPTION_ENABLED is declared in no wrangler.toml at all
- Topics: NF-08.2, NF-08.9, NF-05.16, NF-11.8, NF-12.11, NF-10.7
- Chain of events: the deployment omits `ENCRYPTION_ENABLED` (no `[vars]` block declares it in any worker, unlike `DEVICE_KEYS_ENABLED`); relay-side `is_encrypted = gate AND zone.encrypted AND NOT is_public_read` is therefore false everywhere; the `is_zone_ciphertext` shape gate at `nip_handlers.rs:1157` never fires, so plaintext kind-42 writes are accepted into zones the config flags as encrypted (NF-08.9: "not encrypted → accept"); members post in cleartext into a "family"/"business" zone whose calendar projection and zone config promise restriction (NF-11.8), and the retro BBS masks only zk-tagged content, so the plaintext renders (NF-05.11 note); turning the gate on later gates only *new* writes — nothing purges or flags the accumulated plaintext — so the relay/operator-readable history persists while ADR-2017's sealed-original migration and the zone-key grant model present the zone as end-to-end encrypted.
- Evidence: NF-08.2 N4 — "ENCRYPTION_ENABLED is read from env at zone_config.rs:86 but declared in NO wrangler.toml [vars] block in any worker … a deploy that forgets it silently leaves encryption OFF"; NF-08.9 gate flow; NF-05.16 N3 — with the gate off, no gift wrap is re-opened and keys already held keep decrypting; NF-12.11 — the whole sealed-envelope machinery presupposes encrypted zones.
- Warning signs visible today: the doc-drift note in NF-08.2 N4 and the deliberate contrast with `DEVICE_KEYS_ENABLED`'s declared defaults (NF-08.8).
- Confidence: high — the latent condition and its stated consequence are direct; the specific exposure window is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Device-key custody split: moderation gates key on the raw subkey while admission resolves the owner, and revocation is inert when the gate is off
- Topics: NF-02.7, NF-03.4, NF-03.5, NF-10.11 (KF-4, KF-9), NF-10.2 (K6)
- Chain of events: the deployment enables `DEVICE_KEYS_ENABLED` in both workers; a member registers device subkeys (root-recoverable by design, K6); a device is stolen or a subkey leaks; at ingress the whitelist gate resolves the subkey to its owner (step 6b, `effective_pubkey`, `nip_handlers.rs:878`) so the subkey is admitted *as the member* — but the suspension/silence and ban/mute gates that follow (steps 8–9, `nip_handlers.rs:905`, `nip_handlers.rs:928`) key on the raw signing pubkey, so a ban or mute recorded against the owner does not bind the subkey; the member revokes the device (`devices.rs:477` sets `revoked = 1`), yet the only enforcement point the material shows is the relay DO's read of `device_keys` at NIP-42 AUTH, and the moderation path still uses the raw key — the revoked subkey keeps publishing. Alternatively the gate is off in either worker (both templates ship `false`): a known device-to-owner mapping is then IGNORED and revocation has no effect at AUTH at all.
- Evidence: NF-10.11 N4 (KF-4/KF-9: "the write gate resolves a device key to its owner, but the moderation gates that follow key on the RAW signing pubkey, and a gift wrap has no author to resolve at all"); NF-02.7 divergence note ("revocation has no effect at AUTH"); NF-03.4 asserts the step order 6b before 8–9; NF-10.2 K6 (subkey is root-recoverable — domain separation, not isolation).
- Warning signs visible today: both findings sit in the owned-not-silenced ledger; the divergence is invisible in any default deployment because both gates ship off.
- Confidence: medium — KF-4/KF-9 and the inert-revocation note are stated; whether relay AUTH honours `revoked` is not shown anywhere in the material, so that link is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Governance read endpoints are authenticated-but-anonymous, and the reviewer aggregate is reconstructible
- Topics: NF-10.11 (KF-2, KF-2a), NF-02.2, NF-06.9
- Evidence: "the governance READ endpoints verify a NIP-98 signature with no membership check, so authenticated is effectively anonymous, and the admin-gated reviewer aggregate can be reconstructed from the un-gated decisions read" — `docs/security/known-findings.md:23-24`, listed as an operator decision taken once for both.
- Failure: any party with any nostr keypair (no membership, no invite) harvests every decision and reconstructs the admin-gated reviewer telemetry (`governance_api.rs:1407`), deanonymizing human reviewers and exposing moderation/governance data at scale.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: high

### F-02 — The preview worker's SSRF guard is denylist-only without PREVIEW_ALLOWED_HOSTS, and there is no wall-clock timeout
- Topics: NF-07.6, NF-07.5, NF-10.7 (S3)
- Evidence: "without PREVIEW_ALLOWED_HOSTS the guard is denylist-only and DNS-rebinding-vulnerable — `ssrf.rs:13`" and "there is NO wall-clock timeout" (NF-07.6 N3/N4); the template declares only `ALLOWED_ORIGIN` (`wrangler.toml:13`).
- Failure: an attacker posts a link whose hostname re-resolves after validation; each redirect hop is re-checked but no resolve-then-pin primitive exists, so the worker fetches attacker-chosen targets and becomes an open, unauthenticated (30 req/60 s) fetch proxy with only size caps.
- Confidence: high on the stated gap; reachable impact on the Workers runtime is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence, acknowledged in code)
- Severity: medium

### F-03 — ensure_schema runs DDL on every request before auth, and ModCache fails closed on any D1 fault
- Topics: NF-10.11 (KF-3), NF-11.7, NF-02.1, NF-08.5
- Evidence: KF-3 — "ensure_schema runs its DDL on EVERY request, before the CORS short-circuit and any authentication" (`known-findings.md:26`); NF-11.7 — a D1 fault returns `Unknown`, `is_blocked` maps it to TRUE, and an `Unknown` is never cached, so every lookup re-hits D1; NF-02.1 asserts schema bootstrap precedes rate limiting on the auth worker.
- Failure: an unauthenticated flood drives per-request DDL until D1 errors; the moderation cache then blocks *every* author (fail-closed), and REST NIP-98 replay writes fail — a total publish/REST outage induced without credentials.
- Confidence: high on each stated behaviour; the combined outage is inferred from the two stated facts.
- Marked by authors: yes (KF-3 as Debt; the ModCache fail-closed behaviour is a stated invariant)
- Severity: medium

### F-04 — The events-table ALTERs discard their result, so a legacy table missing d_tag/received_at fails every save silently
- Topics: NF-08.6, NF-11.4, NF-09.5
- Evidence: "ensure_schema … discards their result (`let _ =`), so a real failure there is silent — `migrations/0009_events.sql:12-17`, `lib.rs:731`" (NF-08.6 N4); `save_event` inserts `d_tag` and `received_at` on every write (`relay_do/storage.rs:80`).
- Failure: on a D1 made from the older SETUP path, the ALTER fails silently, the columns stay missing, and every subsequent `INSERT INTO events` errors — the relay accepts nothing, with no schema error surfaced anywhere.
- Confidence: medium — the discarded result is stated; the failing INSERT is inferred from the stated column list.
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: high

### F-05 — Self-review and original-signer guards compare pubkeys case-sensitively
- Topics: NF-10.11 (KF-6), NF-06.6, NF-06.15, NF-02.10
- Evidence: "the self-review and original-signer guards compare pubkeys case-SENSITIVELY while NIP-98 returns the pubkey verbatim — `known-findings.md:29` … the same class the registry closed in NF-06.15, left open in the core aggregate" (`governance.rs:1738`, `governance.rs:1835`).
- Failure: an agent or admin presenting a mixed-case rendering of its own pubkey decides its own case (self-review forbidden, `governance.rs:1286`) or supersedes a decision it is not the original signer of — governance integrity broken by a casing trick that NIP-98 legitimately permits.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: high

### F-06 — KV and SESSIONS are the same physical namespace
- Topics: NF-08.4, NF-10.3 (O11a)
- Evidence: "KV and SESSIONS are the SAME physical namespace id — `nostr-bbs-auth-worker/wrangler.toml:24` and `:39` — key-collision risk across the two logical uses" (O11, still live).
- Failure: a key written through the `KV` alias collides with a session/challenge key (or vice versa), corrupting WebAuthn challenge state or session persistence — logins fail or, worse, a challenge row is overwritten such that verify reads attacker-shaped data.
- Confidence: medium — the shared id is stated; any actual collision is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (Anomaly, live)
- Severity: medium

### F-07 — Channel-creator lookup still falls back to the kind-40 event for pre-table and failed-write channels
- Topics: NF-03.7, NF-11.4
- Evidence: "the creator INSERT result is discarded (`let _ =`) … so a channel whose write failed has no row and is still on the deletion-exposed fallback — `storage.rs:171`, `nip_handlers.rs:2220`. Channels created before the table existed have no row either" (NF-03.7 N4); the fallback path is the exact mechanism of the closed anomaly O5.
- Failure: a kind-5 deletion of the kind-40 event on any such channel locks a TL2 creator out of their own channel metadata — the O5 lockout re-manifests for the residual population.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: medium

### F-08 — The search index has no reindex or rebuild path, and model drift fails loudly into a dead end
- Topics: NF-07.1, NF-07.3, NF-07.4
- Evidence: the five-minute cron "REINDEXES NOTHING — the handler's whole body is a `load_store` touch … There is no reindex or rebuild path anywhere in lib.rs or store.rs" (NF-07.1 N1, DD6); "an index built with one embedding model and queried with another returns 409, not silently wrong neighbours" (`lib.rs:487`, NF-07.3).
- Failure: an embedding-model change or a corrupted RVF object makes `/search` permanently 409 (or permanently empty) with no in-repo tool to rebuild the R2 store and KV mapping — search outage until manual R2 surgery.
- Confidence: high on the absence; the outage scenario follows directly.
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, DD6)
- Severity: medium

### F-09 — Every unfurled URL is disclosed to Google via the favicon dependency
- Topics: NF-07.7
- Evidence: "the favicon choice is an outbound third-party dependency in an otherwise LAN-shaped design — every preview embeds a google.com favicon URL — `parse.rs:172`" (NF-07.7 N2).
- Failure: members' link activity (which URLs are being previewed, and by implication posted) is continuously leaked to a third party from a system marketed as self-hosted — a privacy breach discoverable by the client's own network audit.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: no (observed in a note, never tagged)
- Severity: medium

### F-10 — Pod quota teardown is best-effort, so stale reservations survive deprovisioning
- Topics: NF-04.2, NF-04.7
- Evidence: "Teardown releases the owner's quota best-effort — a stale quota row must not fail the deprovision — `lib.rs:922`" (NF-04.2); the D1 quota path is authoritative and atomic (`quota.rs:110`).
- Failure: a release failure during deprovision leaves the consumed quota row behind; re-provisioning the same owner then hits the stale reservation and the user cannot write to their pod despite holding fresh quota.
- Confidence: medium — the best-effort release is stated; the lockout is inferred.
- Marked by authors: no (stated as a deliberate tradeoff, not flagged)
- Severity: medium

### F-11 — The payment ledger is a journalled D1 cache with no on-chain invalidation while the chain view is unbuilt
- Topics: NF-04.9, NF-04.11, NF-10.7 (N4)
- Evidence: "D1 demoted to a height-stamped derived view folded from sidestr UTXOs, staleness bound an error — not yet built" (`ADR-2012-...md:48-52`); production sets `PAY_ENABLED true` (amendment withdraws the dormancy assumption); credits are keyed to outpoints (`pay_ledger.rs:618`) but no reorg or spend-invalidation of an already-credited TXO is described anywhere.
- Failure: a credited outpoint is reorged away or double-spent on-chain; the journal row stands, `read_balance` (`pay_ledger.rs:412`) serves the stale number, and a debit releases paid resources against money the pod no longer holds.
- Confidence: low (inferred — the gap is stated, the reorg scenario is not)
- Marked by authors: yes (Open / proposed-not-built)
- Severity: medium

### F-12 — Calibration probe blinding does not exist on the wire the agent can read
- Topics: NF-06.14, NF-10.11 (KF-10)
- Evidence: "the tag remains on the raw signed 31402 served over REQ, because removing it would break the signature both clients verify strictly — blindness on the rendered surface is the CLIENT's responsibility" (NF-06.14 N4); recorded as a documented limitation (`known-findings.md:33-42, :45`).
- Failure: an agent reads its own raw 31402 events from the relay, learns which requests are sampled, and adapts its behaviour on oversight-visible requests only — oversight quality degrades without any gate ever failing.
- Confidence: high on the stated visibility; the behavioural adaptation is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence / documented limitation)
- Severity: medium

### F-13 — wasm_bridge exports subkey derivation and signing to JS with zero consumers, over root-recoverable subkeys
- Topics: NF-12.1, NF-10.3 (O10), NF-10.2 (K6), NF-09.3
- Evidence: wasm_bridge "exports NIP-44, subkey derivation and Schnorr signing to JS — `wasm_bridge.rs:20/88/228` … ANOMALY O10 re-verified: no JS consumer exists in this repo" (NF-12.1 N2); "a derived subkey is ROOT-RECOVERABLE — domain separation, not compromise isolation" (K6, `keys.rs:240`).
- Failure: any script running in a page hosting the bridge can invoke derivation and signing against whatever key material the bridge holds; because subkeys are root-recoverable, one exercised subkey is not contained. The surface is exported, wasm32-compiled, and exercised by no test that runs in CI (the vector suite soft-skips without fixtures, O10).
- Confidence: low (inferred — the exports and the custody model are stated; the XSS path is not)
- Marked by authors: yes (Anomaly O10)
- Severity: medium

### F-14 — Admin deletion of a kind-42 leaves no tombstone, so already-synced clients keep the content
- Topics: NF-01.3, NF-03.10, NF-05.6, NF-05.1
- Evidence: "deleting the kind-42 row outright, with no kind-5 tombstone, is deliberate — a kind-5 would tombstone the id that a sealed-original envelope restores" (`lib.rs:343-345`, NF-01.3 N3); client suppression is driven purely by the tombstone set (`channels.rs:405`, `channels.rs:446`); cached messages are evicted only after 30 days (`main.rs:195`).
- Failure: an admin "deletes" abusive content; every member who synced before the deletion retains and renders it indefinitely, with no revocation signal ever arriving — the deletion is real only for fresh clients.
- Confidence: high — each component is stated and the combination is direct.
- Marked by authors: no (presented as a deliberate ADR-2017 tradeoff, not as a risk)
- Severity: medium

### F-15 — NIP-42 AUTH skips the relay-tag binding while RELAY_URL ships blank
- Topics: NF-03.2, NF-08.8
- Evidence: "own_relay_url unset or blank SKIPS the relay-tag check only. This fails OPEN on the defence-in-depth check … RELAY_URL ships blank — `wrangler.toml:38`" (NF-03.2).
- Failure: in the shipped default, an AUTH event minted naming a different relay satisfies this relay's AUTH; the per-session challenge and signature still bind, so the exposure is a lost relay-binding check rather than open admission — but every default deployment runs without it.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (the note itself labels it fails OPEN)
- Severity: low

## ontomark / critical

### F-01 — The live legacy producer still ships five unfixed SEV-1/2 faults, including a fail-open redactor and a body-only credential scan, and it does not redact private links at all
- Topics: OM-00.2, OM-00.3, OM-05.5
- Evidence: OM-00.3 EXT states "VisionClaw's vault build job still ships S1.1, S1.2, S1.5, S2.3 and S3.1 unchanged until VC-60 retires" (ADR-OM-005…md:11). S1.1 is the fail-open redactor (`vault/src/projection.rs:229`), S1.2 the body-only credential scan while render publishes frontmatter (`vault/src/build/publish.rs:159-160,200`). OM-05.5 shows that job building in-tree (`cargo build -p vault`, ontology-publish.yml:168) and running `vault --repo vault-source build --vault knowledge` (ontology-publish.yml:172-174), whose output feeds `pack-pod-resources.py` (ontology-publish.yml:194); the same file's bullet says "the legacy `vault` build kept private links".
- Failure: A credential placed in a frontmatter value of a public knowledge page is published today into the Pod resource pack; any regex compile error silently disables redaction; private links are published unredacted by design in that pipeline. This is not a regression risk — it is the current estate until VC-60 lands.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (EXTERNAL in OM-00.3; the retirement dependency in ADR-OM-005:11)

### F-02 — One flag or one profile regression disables private-link redaction in the public scope, and the same switch also stops holding back `_misc` pages; the redaction-on guard exists only for machine scope
- Topics: OM-00.2 (S3.4), OM-00.5, OM-02.3, OM-05.6, OM-06.1
- Evidence: `--no-redact-private-links` overlays the profile as a `Some(false)` override (`ontomark-cli/src/main.rs:503`, `build/mod.rs:84`, `profile.rs:411`); redaction defaults to true only via the estate profile (`profile.rs:417`, `estate/profile.yaml:198`). With it off, the compat projection runs (`build/mod.rs:614-616`) and "never applies held_back … a public misc/ page stays public there, while stage_redacted skips it" (`ir/projection.rs:623`, `build/publish.rs:216`) — so the page API and markdown mirror carry a page whose `publish/` markdown is withheld. `ensure_machine_scope` refuses "redaction off" (`build/mod.rs:426-465`) but is invoked only for machine scope (`build/mod.rs:503-506`); no equivalent guard is stated for public builds.
- Failure: A profile file regression, or an operator copying the conformance invocation (which passes `--no-redact-private-links`, `conformance.rs:179`), produces a public site where held-back `_misc` content appears in `api/pages` and the markdown mirror while private links are not rewritten — with no refusal and no public-scope guard to catch it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partly — S3.4 is marked KEEP and the overlay is marked DIVERGENCE; the absence of a public-scope redaction guard is not marked

### F-03 — Under `--vault all` (the production command), `working/` pages are published to the public site without the census refusals and without validation; the S1.3 and S3.3 fixes bind only to the knowledge vault
- Topics: OM-03.1, OM-03.2, OM-06.1
- Evidence: The census and its refusals (non-boolean `public` → `invalid_publication_flag`, unclosed fence → `invalid_input`, `ir/projection.rs:117-142`) are invoked once for the main vault (`build/mod.rs:512-513`). Working is "loaded with its own walk and census under machine scope, else sibling_vault only if with_working_publish" (`build/mod.rs:539-549`) — `sibling_vault` is a loader (`build/publish.rs:356-367`). Validate covers "working too under machine" (`build/mod.rs:576-597`); OM-06.1 states the working census and validation as machine-scope facts (`mod.rs:539-549`, `:586-597`). Staging still scans public working pages for credentials (`build/mod.rs:574`, `publish.rs:220-243`) — that is the only gate stated for working in public scope.
- Failure: visionGraph runs `ontomark build --vault all --publish-out quartz/content --with-markdown-mirror` (publish.yml:158-163). A working page with `public: "yes"` (the exact S1.3 input) or an unclosed frontmatter fence (the exact S3.3 input) is silently treated as private and silently omitted — or, with `public: true` and failing every other rule (unknown keys, bad types, undated journals), is published to the public site unvalidated. Working journals are scanned by nobody in this path.
- Confidence: high — the material states the working census/validation only for machine scope and names `sibling_vault` (a loader) as the alternative; the absence in public scope is a direct implication, inferred only in that step
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — The promote swap has a window with no output directory, a crash there leaves the previous bundle only in a "retired" directory the next build deletes, and `publish_out` writes after promote so bundle and site stop being one generation
- Topics: OM-03.5, OM-03.1
- Evidence: "between them out does not exist" (`build/mod.rs:260-263`); `swap_in` "clear(s) a stale retired directory" and "the retired directory (is) deleted, so no obsolete export survives" (`build/mod.rs:256-267`). `write_to(publish_out)` runs after promote, and "a failure there leaves the NEW bundle beside the PREVIOUS publication tree … the two no longer one generation" (`build/publish.rs:349`, `build/mod.rs:913-915`), using the same `swap_in` on the live site directory (`publish.rs:334-349`). The flagship consumer uses exactly `--publish-out quartz/content` (publish.yml:158-163).
- Failure: Crash or power loss between the two renames: `out` (and during `write_to`, the site directory) does not exist; the only copy of the previous publication sits in `.publication-retired-PID`, which the next build deletes before rebuilding. A `write_to` failure after a successful promote leaves `.generation.json` claiming generation N in the bundle while the public site still serves N-1 — any digest-keyed consumer now sees a mismatch with no error.
- Confidence: high on the mechanics (all stated); the crash-recovery scenario is inferred from them
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partly — the post-promote divergence is marked DIVERGENCE (OM-03.5 N2) and the no-`out` window is stated as an INVARIANT (N1); the retired-deletion data-loss consequence is not marked

### F-05 — `content_digest` ignores the attribution roster (and the profile), so two builds with different Turtle/page-API content share one digest while consumers key caches on that digest
- Topics: OM-03.6, OM-06.5
- Evidence: "the attribution roster shapes the Turtle and the page API but is not an input to content_digest, which hashes pages, journals and the vocabulary only" (`build/mod.rs:621-626` vs `:861-879`, marked N1). Loom's readers key on the marker's `content_digest`/`ontology_digest` (`loom-facade/src/mirror.rs:266-285`, `:394-437`), and OM-06.5 L4 confirms "a consumer cache keyed on ontology_digest" exists as a consumption pattern.
- Failure: An attribution edit changes `ontology.ttl` and the page API; the digest does not move; the vector store and any mirror serve the old graph indefinitely with a marker that claims nothing changed. Silent, permanent staleness until a page edit happens to move the digest.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OM-03.6 N1)

### F-06 — Three wikilink resolvers with different fragment/`.md` handling mean fragment links in public pages resolve to phantom `namespace+slugify` targets that Turtle keeps but the graph tiers drop
- Topics: OM-02.1, OM-01.5, OM-04.2, OM-04.4
- Evidence: `PrivateLinks::resolve` strips `#fragment` and `.md` (`build/redact.rs:219,227`); `Corpus::build` passes the whole target to `names.get` with fallback `namespace + slugify(target)` (`ir/model.rs:338-342`), so "a fragment link … is resolved here to namespace + slugify of the whole string" (OM-02.1 n2). Turtle emits "every target kept, declared or not" (`turtle.rs:337-342`); NGG1 silently drops edges to unselected targets (`graph_tiers.rs:297-298,309-310`); curated links bypass resolution slug-verbatim (`ir/model.rs:424`, `input/slug.rs:65-75`).
- Failure: A public page containing `[[Some Page#section]]` produces a relation triple in `ontology.ttl` pointing at a nonexistent IRI (`…/some-page-section`) — a phantom node for reasoners and agents — while the same link is redacted, resolved, or dropped differently by the redactor, the page API and the tiers. The published artifacts disagree about the same link.
- Confidence: medium — the resolver divergence is stated; the phantom-triple consequence is inferred from the stated fallback and the "every target kept" rule
- Severity: medium (high if fragment-style links are common; the corpus link census would decide)
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OM-02.1 n2/n3, OM-01.5 note3)

### F-07 — ADR-2136 still tells agentbox operators the machine bundle is `--vault all`; following it silently produces the wrong artifact — the public bundle — because that command is valid and succeeds
- Topics: OM-06.5, OM-06.1, OM-00.5
- Evidence: ADR-2136:84 "names AB-P6 as the private `--vault all` machine bundle, the value the public publish passes" (OM-06.5 A2); the decided interface is `build --scope machine` (`DEPRECATION.md:462`, `ontomark-cli/src/main.rs:140-141`); machine scope refuses `--vault all` (`build/mod.rs:433-438`) but public `--vault all` is the ordinary publish command (publish.yml:158-163) and yields no `scope` field, no `api/pages/working/`, and a full `publish/` tree.
- Failure: An agentbox operator following the ADR runs `ontomark build --vault all --out …`, gets exit 0, and ships a public bundle as the pod resource pack: missing the merged page set, missing the scope stamp, and carrying a published `publish/` tree the machine pack was never supposed to include. Nothing fails; the pack is simply wrong.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT / "ADR-2136:84 still says `--vault all` and is corrected in agentbox", OM-06.5 A4; OPEN3/OPEN4 in OM-00.5)

### F-08 — Five different domain and label derivations across emitters, including alias-folding in NGG1 but not in Turtle, so the same class reports different domains per artifact
- Topics: OM-04.5, OM-04.7, OM-00.1
- Evidence: Label fallback differs — `label` else title (`indexes.rs:129`), label else title else slug (`closure.rs:109-113`), label else slug (`graph_tiers.rs:254-256`). Domain differs — verbatim (`turtle.rs:302-304`), alias-folded (`graph_tiers.rs:81-90`), exact slug (`webvowl.rs:91`), `unclassified` (`page_api.rs:147-148`), title-cased (`indexes.rs:328`). "A class in an aliased domain lands in the canonical domain tier but carries the unfolded string in `ontology.ttl`" (OM-04.7).
- Failure: A consumer joining the page API's domain index, the search index's title-cased domains and the TTL's `sourceDomain` for one class gets three different strings; domain-scoped queries and the domain tiers disagree about membership. No error anywhere.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OM-04.5 bullet, OM-04.7 bullets, OM-00.1 DOM subgraph)

### F-09 — Relation naming is forked: WebVOWL hardcodes `related-to → skos:related` from a table, Turtle derives from the vocabulary, and a hardcoded 13-name stub list misses any vocabulary-added relation
- Topics: OM-04.6, OM-04.2, OM-04.5
- Evidence: WebVOWL's `REL_MAP` (`webvowl.rs:30-42`) "agree(s) only while the vocabulary does" with Turtle's vocabulary-driven IRIs (`turtle.rs:656-673`); the stub list is "a hardcoded array of 13 property names … a relation the vocabulary adds is declared but never stubbed" (`turtle.rs:446-460`); four hand-kept orderings of the twelve relations exist (`indexes.rs:30`, `page_api.rs:18`, `ir/model.rs:36`, `webvowl.rs:30`).
- Failure: Add or rename a relation in `vocabulary.yaml`: Turtle emits it, WebVOWL labels it wrongly or not at all, the stub path stops materialising targets, and the orderings drift — the published surfaces describe different ontologies after a vocabulary edit that no build step validates against these tables.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OM-04.6 bullet, OM-04.2 bullet, OM-04.5 bullet)

### F-10 — NGG1 collapses all twelve relations to one wire byte and the dedup key merges distinct relations, while edges to unselected targets are silently dropped and `stats.json` reports declared against shipped
- Topics: OM-04.4, OM-00.2 (S3.1), OM-00.5
- Evidence: Every relation key becomes `EDGE_RELATION` (`graph_tiers.rs:307-316`), a published wire value (`ontomark-ngg1/src/lib.rs:144`); the edge set keyed `(src, tgt, type)` merges two relations between one pair (`graph_tiers.rs:283,313`); "Edge to a target that is not a selected node is silently dropped" (`graph_tiers.rs:297-298,309-310`); `declared_backbone`/`declared_relations` count what the tiers then discard (`graph_tiers.rs:285-286`). Turtle keeps every relation (`turtle.rs:337-341`).
- Failure: The explorer tiers present a `part-of` and a `related-to` between the same pair as one undifferentiated edge, and lose edges entirely for cross-boundary targets — the graph a user explores is not the graph the TTL asserts. Closing it requires an NGG2 format bump left undecided (ADR-OM-005).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (KEEP/OPEN S3.1, OM-00.5 OPEN1, OM-04.4 DIVERGENCEs)

### F-11 — The "already public" safety of several emitters rests on an unenforced pre-filtering convention, and the `gate` command already violates it by building over the raw corpus with `public_only=false`
- Topics: OM-04.5, OM-04.2, OM-02.2
- Evidence: "`backlink_index` … and `closure::compute` iterate `records` … safe today only because the corpus is pre-filtered (`projection.rs:502-506`), unenforced here" (OM-04.5); "`build_graph` with `public_only=false` is called by the CLI `gate` command on the raw, unprojected corpus (`ontomark-cli/src/main.rs:378`)" (OM-04.2); every `r.public` test in the emitters is "a second, redundant public gate" on a pre-filtered corpus (OM-02.2 n1).
- Failure: Any new caller — a future emitter, a library consumer, a refactor that hands the raw corpus where the projected one is expected — publishes private records into artifacts, because nothing in the type or the function enforces the invariant the whole redaction story depends on. The gate command demonstrates the raw-corpus call pattern already exists in production code.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium (latent security; no current leak is shown)
- Marked by authors: yes ("safe today only because … unenforced here", OM-04.5; the gate-graph divergence, OM-04.2)

### F-12 — The reasoner cap is a hard constant with no configuration: a growing corpus crosses 2000 relevant transitive pairs and every build refuses with exit 2, with no knob to raise
- Topics: OM-04.3, OM-03.1
- Evidence: "`RELEVANT_TRANSITIVE_CAP = 2000`" (`reason.rs:473`); "`WHELK_RELEVANT_CAP` is an error-code string only … the cap is the constant 2000 and `vault.toml` has no reasoner section" (`reason.rs:477`, OM-04.3 bullet); the cap error returns `ReasoningError` → exit 2 (`reason.rs:580`, `ontomark-cli/src/main.rs:536-539`).
- Failure: Ontology growth is ordinary editorial work; the day a definition-heavy edit pushes the relevant-existential count past 2000, publication halts with a refusal no operator can tune, and the only remediation is removing definitions — under release pressure.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partly — the facts are stated as an INVARIANT in OM-04.3; the unconfigurable-cliff operability risk itself is not marked

### F-13 — Two processes own the bundle: ontomark wipes `out` wholesale on every promote and treats the marker as write-once, while Loom renames its rvdb "into place" and rewrites the same marker as data
- Topics: OM-06.3, OM-03.5
- Evidence: ontomark: `Staged` write-once, "pre-existing content of out is replaced wholesale, not merged" (`build/mod.rs:260-263,270-274`), retired deleted so "no obsolete export survives" (`mod.rs:265-267`). Loom: `promote_vault_build` "reads id and artifacts and rewrites the marker as a Value" and renames "ontology-corpus.rvdb + sidecar … into place, add both to the marker … under `.promotion-in-flight`" (`loom-vector-ruvector/src/artifact.rs:168-175,198-260`).
- Failure: If the rvdb lands inside the bundle directory (the marker rewrite strongly implies it), the next ontomark build deletes it and overwrites the Loom-amended marker: the vector store vanishes and must be re-embedded (network, cost) after every build, or pipelines silently ship a bundle whose marker no longer lists the rvdb. If it lands outside, the two-writer marker remains: ontomark's write-once guarantees do not survive Loom's rewrite.
- Confidence: medium, inferred — the material states both behaviours but not the rvdb's final path relative to `out`
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — The CLI surface contradicts its own comments and contract: `--vault working` re-roots the entire public build at `working/`, refusal exit 2 is unreachable outside `build`, three verbs ignore `--profile`, and `--vault` defaults differ per verb
- Topics: OM-05.1, OM-05.2, OM-05.3, OM-05.6
- Evidence: Comments say the projections are knowledge's "by definition" (`main.rs:474-475`, `build/mod.rs:76-77`) yet `--vault working` sets `vault_root=working/` for the whole build (`main.rs:484-488`); "validate, gate and conflicts never return 2 from their own code" (OM-05.3 n2); `--profile` is resolved for every verb but "conflicts, repair bodies and repair fences never consume it" (`main.rs:437,585,629`); `--stats` "only forces the JSON report" (`main.rs:214-216,556`); `repair` defaults to `all` while the others default `knowledge` (`main.rs:100,117,130,204`).
- Failure: An operator "pre-validating" with `--vault working` then building the same way publishes the working draft tree as the site's ontology; automation keyed on exit 2 to mean "refused" cannot distinguish refusals from ordinary failures on three of the six verbs; profile-based expectations silently don't apply to conflicts and repair.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes, individually (DOC-DRIFT on the comments; DIVERGENCEs on `--profile`, `--stats`, exit codes; the default split is stated in prose)

### F-15 — The anomaly register contradicts itself: OM-00 says `context/v1.jsonld` is not in C3 while OM-03 and OM-04 say C3 lists it, and OM-03 cites an ontomark commit beyond its own verified pin
- Topics: OM-00.2 (F10), OM-03.7, OM-04.1, OM-03.3
- Evidence: OM-00 F10: "emitted but not in C3: `api/pages/_domain-index.json`, `context/v1.jsonld`, `data/.generation.json`". OM-03.7 A5 and OM-04.1 C8 both list `context/v1.jsonld` as a C3 artefact citing `sovereign-corpus-contracts.md:122-123`. Separately, OM-03.3 cites "ontomark `95208e8` (2026-10-18, estate register ER-18)" while the file's `verified_commit` is `ee9e04a` — the held-back description mixes a pinned state with a later, unpinned one.
- Failure: Anyone using this register for compliance or sign-off gets opposite answers on whether a published artefact is contractual, and cannot tell which held-back-rule description matches the deployed binary. The register is itself an artefact with divergent copies — the exact failure class it catalogs.
- Confidence:

## ontomark / premortem

# Post-mortem — ontomark estate publication failure

Basis: the twelve topic files as verified at `ee9e04a` (ontomark), with the external consumers they cite. No source beyond the citations in the material was available; every claim below carries the citation the material gives.

---

### R-1 — The legacy VisionClaw build job still ships the unfixed SEV-1 publication gates
- Topics: OM-00.2, OM-00.3, OM-05.5
- Chain of events: A credential is pasted into a frontmatter value (title, sources) of a public knowledge page → the live VisionClaw pipeline builds its own `vault` crate (`ontology-publish.yml:168-174`), whose credential scan is body-only → the rendered frontmatter carrying the secret is staged into `output/vault` publish/ (`vault/src/build/publish.rs:159-160, 200`) → `pack-pod-resources.py` ships it into Pod resources (`ontology-publish.yml:194`). Independently, a `ProseRedactor` regex compile error in that binary fails **open** (`vault/src/projection.rs:229`), so private links can also publish. Gate/conflicts see only the first vault under `--vault all` there (S1.5).
- Evidence: the register states the whole SEV-1 set was *inherited* from VisionClaw and fixed only in ontomark at 8d654d1 (OM-00.2 A1–A5); OM-00.3 EXT states plainly "VisionClaw's vault build job still ships S1.1, S1.2, S1.5, S2.3 and S3.1 unchanged until VC-60 retires under its four gates" (`ADR-OM-005-estate-alignment-one-producer-explicit-projections.md:11`); OM-05.5 VC confirms the job builds `-p vault` in-tree and never invokes ontomark.
- Warning signs visible today: the register ranks S1.1/S1.2 as SEV-1 publication-boundary failures; the retirement (VC-60) is recorded as not done; `DEPRECATION.md:145` records the records.jsonl deferral in the same legacy line.
- Confidence: high — the unfixed gates are stated as EXTERNAL; only the triggering edit is supposed, and it is the ordinary case the register's own SEV-1 reproduction tests cover.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — `write_to` runs after `promote`: the bundle and the publication tree split into two generations
- Topics: OM-03.1, OM-03.5, OM-05.5
- Chain of events: visionGraph runs `build --vault all --out site-data --publish-out quartz/content` (`publish.yml:158-163`) → `run_with` promotes `out/` first via the two-rename swap (`build/mod.rs:256-277`, `:913`), deleting the retired tree → `publish::write_to` then stages `.publish-staging-PID` beside `quartz/content` and swaps (`build/publish.rs:334-349`, `build/mod.rs:914-916`) → a disk-quota, permission or filesystem failure in `write_to` returns `Err` and exits 1 → the site (`quartz/content`) keeps generation N while the promoted bundle and its markers are N+1 — and the N+1 marker *lists sha256 hashes of the publish bytes as external artefacts* (`build/mod.rs:311-313`, `:672-678`, `:904-908`) that never landed on the site. Downstream consumers of the bundle (Loom's `promote_vault_build`, agentbox) act on N+1 while the client-facing site serves N, with no marker linking the two trees.
- Evidence: OM-03.5 N2 (DIVERGENCE): "write_to runs AFTER promote, so a failure there leaves the NEW bundle beside the PREVIOUS publication tree that swap_in kept, the two no longer one generation — `build/publish.rs:349`, `build/mod.rs:913-915`"; OM-03.5 N1: the swap is two renames, "between them out does not exist"; OM-03.7: `Report.written` counts both destinations.
- Warning signs visible today: the divergence is labelled in the register; DOC-DRIFT `publish.rs:1` still says `<out>/publish/` is the published markdown although with `publish_out` it is not in `<out>`; the module doc still says "promote atomically" (`build/mod.rs:12`) against the two-rename implementation.
- Confidence: high — the ordering and the marker behaviour are stated; only the mechanical disk failure that triggers it is supposed.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — The publish/ markdown tree is link-redacted only; marker redaction never applies to it
- Topics: OM-02.3, OM-02.4, OM-03.1, OM-03.4
- Chain of events: `stage_redacted` runs at `build/mod.rs:574` receiving only `private_links`; the `ProseRedactor` does not exist yet — it is created later inside the projection (`ir/projection.rs:561-586`) and returned to `run` at `ir/projection.rs:507` → the staging render writes "frontmatter re-rendered, links redacted, body **verbatim**" (`build/publish.rs:163-169`) → any prose mention of a private page's title, alias or name that is *not* a wikilink, and frontmatter values cleaned only at link level, pass through into `publish/` → visionGraph ships exactly that tree to the live site (`--publish-out quartz/content`, `publish.yml:158-163`) while the Turtle, indexes, page API and the opt-in `api/markdown` mirror are marker-masked.
- Evidence: the sequence diagram orders stage (574) before validate (576) and projection (602-617) (OM-03.1); REND states body verbatim (OM-03.4); OM-02.4's note says the markdown mirror "re-applies the prose redactor" as "a second body-cleaning pass" (`build/mod.rs:786`, `ir/projection.rs:653`) — distinguishing the mirror from a first pass that lacks it; OM-03.4 N2 confirms the mirror/page API come from projected records while publish/ is what the gate scans; OM-03.3 R1 counts three implementations of the same body cleaning.
- Warning signs visible today: three body-cleaning implementations with different coverage; the mirror's re-application called out as a DIVERGENCE; the redactor's fail-closed fix (S1.1) applies to its construction, not to its absence from staging.
- Confidence: medium, inferred — the material states the ordering and "body verbatim" but never says outright that publish/ prose is unmasked; the benign reading (publish/ is the author's own markdown, link-redaction is the whole boundary) is possible.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — ADR-2136 still names `--vault all` as the machine bundle; an operator following it builds the wrong artefact
- Topics: OM-00.5, OM-06.1, OM-06.5
- Evidence: AB-P6 is decided as `build --scope machine` (`DEPRECATION.md:462`, `ontomark-cli/src/main.rs:140-141`), but agentbox `ADR-2136-pin-the-shared-ontology-toolchain.md:84` still names "the private `--vault all` machine bundle", which is the public publish's value (OM-00.5 OPEN4, OM-06.5 A2 labelled DOC-DRIFT).
- Failure: an agentbox engineer follows the ADR, runs `build --vault all` (public scope), and the pod-resource pack is produced from the public publish tree — public pages only, redacted, no working records and no machine extras — so agents serve incomplete knowledge with no error anywhere.
- Confidence: high (the drift is stated); the misbuild is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (Open / Drift)
- Severity: high

### F-02 — `content_digest` ignores the attribution roster, and the roster and the dirtiness check read different ontology directories
- Topics: OM-03.6
- Evidence: OM-03.6 N1 (DIVERGENCE): the roster shapes Turtle and the page API "but is not an input to content_digest, which hashes pages, journals and the vocabulary only" (`build/mod.rs:621-626`, `:861-879`); N3: dirtiness hard-codes `repo_root/ontology` (`build/mod.rs:832`) while the roster is read from `vault_root.parent()/ontology` (`build/attribution.rs:32`) — "two roots for one directory".
- Failure: `attribution.yaml` is corrected (authorship/rights change) → rebuild → `content_digest` is unchanged, so any digest-keyed skip or cache serves the old, misattributed Turtle; when `repo_root ≠ vault_root.parent()` the dirty flag is computed over a different ontology dir than the one actually read.
- Confidence: high (both divergences stated); the digest-keyed skip is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)
- Severity: medium

### F-03 — Digests are equal across scopes and Loom ignores `scope`; a public and a machine bundle are indistinguishable to a digest-keyed consumer
- Topics: OM-06.1, OM-06.3, OM-06.5
- Evidence: the invariant test pins `ontology_digest` and `content_digest` equal across scopes (`machine_scope.rs:140`, OM-06.1 SAME); the public marker omits `scope` so its bytes do not change (`build/generation.rs:115-118`); Loom reads named fields only and never reads `scope` (`loom-facade/src/mirror.rs:266-285`, `:394-437`); OM-06.5 L4 warns "a consumer cache keyed on ontology_digest must also key on scope".
- Failure: a consumer that ingests both bundles keys them to the same digest and serves the public artefacts for the machine context (or vice versa) — silently wrong data with matching markers.
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: yes (the cache caution and the C3 reader note are author-flagged)
- Severity: medium

### F-04 — The compat projection never applies `held_back`, so held-back pages reach the machine artefacts
- Topics: OM-00.1, OM-00.2, OM-02.3, OM-03.3
- Evidence: OM-02.3 n1 (DIVERGENCE): "the compat path never forces misc/ pages private … a public misc/ page stays public there, while stage_redacted skips it (`build/publish.rs:216`)"; S3.4 is KEEP, pinned by the byte-parity test that passes `--no-redact-private-links` (`conformance.rs:164`, `:179`); OM-03.3 records that after ER-18 `is_unpublished` still applies `_misc` at top level only — the divergence that remains.
- Failure: the compat flag is used (parity run, or an operator "debugging" redaction) → a deliberately held `misc/` page appears in the search index, page API, prose index and Turtle while `publish/` omits it — internal content on the site or in the API with inconsistent surfaces.
- Confidence: high (stated); firing requires the flag.
- Marked by authors: yes (Open / Keep — S3.4)
- Severity: high

### F-05 — Three wikilink resolvers and two target-to-IRI derivations disagree
- Topics: OM-00.1, OM-01.5, OM-02.1
- Evidence: OM-00.1 LINK: `Corpus::build` keeps fragment and `.md` (`ir/model.rs:336-342`), `PrivateLinks::resolve` strips both (`build/redact.rs:210-229`), the shacl `Resolver::page` has no slug key and a case-sensitive id match (`shacl.rs:1201-1207`), curated links are slug-verbatim (`ir/model.rs:424`); OM-02.1 n2: "a fragment link redacted as private is resolved here to namespace + slugify of the whole string"; OM-01.5 note3: link refs use `target_slug` at `ir/model.rs:424` but the resolve fallback uses `slugify` at `ir/model.rs:342`, differing on kebab input with leading/trailing/doubled dashes.
- Failure: on the compat path a `[[Private Topic#x]]` link fabricates the IRI `private-topic-x`, embedding the private page's slugified title as a public edge target that marker redaction does not match; in both paths the `target_slug`/`slugify` mismatch breaks backlink keys for kebab targets, silently dropping or mis-attributing edges.
- Confidence: high (the divergences are stated); the leak chain needs the compat flag — medium for that part.
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)
- Severity: high

### F-06 — `backlink_index` and `closure::compute` iterate all records; the pre-filter assumption is unenforced
- Topics: OM-02.2, OM-04.5
- Evidence: OM-04.5 (DIVERGENCE): both iterate `records` while consumers iterate `public()`/`public_classes()`; "safe today only because the corpus is pre-filtered (`projection.rs:502-506`), unenforced here".
- Failure: any future caller hands a raw corpus (the compat path already hands the raw corpus to `project_with_redactor`) and private pages enter the backlink index and closure feeding public artefacts.
- Confidence: high (latent, stated).
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)
- Severity: medium

### F-07 — `--vault working` re-roots the whole projection at working/, and a missing working/ silently narrows the build
- Topics: OM-03.2, OM-05.2, OM-05.5
- Evidence: OM-03.2 invariant: "`--vault working` alone makes the WHOLE projection come from `working/` (`main.rs:484-488`)" against comments that the projections are "knowledge/'s by definition" (DOC-DRIFT, OM-05.2); `sibling_vault` loads working "only if its pages dir exists" (`build/publish.rs:356-367`), and OM-05.5 n3: "ontomark silently accepts a knowledge-only build — `publish.yml:180-186`" (visionGraph guards it; nothing in ontomark does).
- Failure: an operator runs `--vault working` believing it is a filter and publishes a draft ontology; or `working/` is absent/renamed under `--vault all` and the site silently publishes knowledge-only content.
- Confidence: high (behaviours stated); the operator error is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)
- Severity: high

### F-08 — Census and load read each file twice: a time-of-check gap
- Topics: OM-03.3
- Evidence: OM-03.3 N2 (DIVERGENCE): "each page is still read twice, census then load, so a file edited between the two is classified on one text and parsed from another, a time-of-check gap the digest no longer widens (`ir/projection.rs:117`, `input/page.rs:452`)."
- Failure: a page edited mid-build is classified on one text and parsed from another; the census can pass a text whose loaded version differs, producing counts and a published page that disagree with the census record.
- Confidence: medium (the race is stated; the window is narrow).
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)
- Severity: medium

- Topics: OM-01.2, OM-02.5
- Evidence: OM-01.2: "non-md → continue, not reported (`input/page.rs:542-544`)"; against OM-02.5: "the census exists so the parser cannot silently drop a file (`ir/projection.rs:6-9`)".
- Failure: a page misnamed (`.markdown`, `.MD`, a stray character) vanishes from the corpus with no skip record and no census count — content silently absent from every artefact.
- Confidence: high (stated).
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)

Also noted while investigating, below the cut for severity: the promote swap is two renames with a window in which `out` does not exist (`build/mod.rs:260-263`), while the module doc still says "atomic" (`build/mod.rs:12`, OM-00.2 F5); `sibling_vault` never compares kinds, so a library caller can stage one vault twice (`build/publish.rs:363`, OM-03.2 N3); `Census.rejected` is the constant 0 with a dead `conflicting_` counter (`ir/projection.rs:147,171`, OM-02.5 n4) and `Report.unsatisfiable` is always empty on success (`build/mod.rs:110-114`, OM-03.7 N3) — both published fields that cannot carry their documented meaning; `--stats` only forces the JSON report (OM-05.1); the global `--profile` is resolved but never consumed by `conflicts` and both `repair` subcommands (`ontomark-cli/src/main.rs:437,585,629`, OM-05.1); and `ontomark-parse` carries a second frontmatter reader not shared with production (`ontomark-parse/src/frontmatter.rs:1-9`, OM-01.1). Each is stated in the material with the citations given there.

## sidestr-rs / critical

### F-01 — Published 0.1 kernel lets a party cheat via its own penalty path, and 0.2 cannot restore those channels
- Topics: SR-04 (header), SR-04.1, SR-04.3
- Evidence: "the 0.1 key let a commitment's owner spend its own revocation leaf, which is the flaw this release fixes" and "a 0.1 channel cannot be restored by 0.2" (SR-04 header); "Version 0.2.0 closes a hole in which a party could cheat with its own penalty path" (SR-04 business); "only version 2 snapshots restore; a 0.1 (version 1) snapshot is refused … such a channel must be closed with 0.1" (`sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:29`, `:182`); hitch is `publish = true` (`../sidestr-rs/Cargo.toml:43`) and 0.2.0 is a breaking release over 0.1.
- Failure: any 0.1 channel in the wild is permanently exposed — the counterparty's punishment path is not exclusive, so broadcasting an old state is decided by fee race rather than by possession of the revocation secret — and the only sanctioned exit is to keep running the flawed 0.1 to close it, which keeps the cheat live through the close.
- Confidence: high (flaw, cheat characterisation and non-restore are stated; exploit mechanics inferred from the construction)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (stated in prose; no marker)

### F-02 — No host exists: every custody duty around the channel kernel is unowned
- Topics: SR-04.5, SR-04 (header), SR-04.3
- Evidence: "**Open:** no estate host supplies the complete service boundary, which 0.2.0 widened by the scheduler and chain-lookup duties" (SR-04.5); host must supply wallet funding, relay I/O, atomic snapshot storage, chain watches, broadcasting and the scheduler (`sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:49`, `:70`, `:105`); "The estate has no host for any of it (VisionClaw ADR-2111 is proposed and unimplemented)" (SR-04 header; ADR-2111:95).
- Failure: operating channels in production requires writing from scratch the code that holds keys, persists snapshots atomically, watches the chain and fires timeouts — the entire money-bearing error surface the kernel deliberately excluded — with no reference implementation to validate against.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-03 — The live chain is produced by a test-only JavaScript engine; the CI-validated Rust producer has never been deployed
- Topics: SR-01.4, SR-01.2, SR-03 (business)
- Evidence: "sidestr:dreamlab is still a test-only chain produced by the upstream JavaScript engine" (SR-01 business); "the producer runs the upstream JavaScript engine baked at the upstream pins" (`config/sidechain/README.md:111`, `:117`); "**Open:** deploy and prove a native producer before describing the Rust engine as the running estate chain" (SR-01.4); the oracle ladder (`ci.yml:14`–`207`) validates the Rust workspace, not the deployed engine.
- Failure: taking over production means carrying a value-bearing chain (ADR-2096 calls sidechains the sole value instrument) whose block production runs on an upstream test node at frozen pins; its crash-recovery, persistence and exhaustion behaviour are untouched by this estate's validation, and none of the Rust parity work transfers to it.
- Confidence: high (deployment facts stated; production-readiness of the JS engine inferred from its "test-only" designation)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-04 — Penalty and claim windows depend on host chain lookups whose freshness and source are unspecified
- Topics: SR-04.4, SR-04.5, SR-01.4
- Evidence: reactions start from "host sees funding spent" (`sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/chain.rs:176`), claims are "republished after six blocks" (`chain.rs:578`), and the machine's deadline is height-based (`machine.rs:1306`); the host supplies "chain lookups for on_spend" (`protocol/mod.rs:49`, `:105`); the only described chain-data path in the estate is the agentbox mirror (`config/sidechain/README.md:111`), with no latency, completeness or reorg-depth guarantee stated anywhere.
- Failure: a counterparty broadcasts a revoked commitment while the mirror lags or the supervisor's mirror is down; `on_spend` is never called inside the window; the penalty confirms after the cheating outputs are already spent — the punishment mechanism that makes unilateral closes safe silently fails.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (dependency on the feed is stated; the feed's weakness is inferred from absence)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — Forward records are keyed by (downstream channel, payment hash); two payments with the same hash collide
- Topics: SR-04.3
- Evidence: "router keeps ForwardRecord before the add, keyed by downstream channel and payment hash, so a restart or a second payment in flight cannot lose them" (`sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:108`, `:173`, `:7`). The stated key cannot distinguish two forwards carrying the same hash over the same channel, and the safety claim covers restart and "a second payment in flight", not same-hash duplication.
- Failure: a payee reuses an invoice and two forwards with the same hash arrive over one channel; the records collide — one is lost or a settle/fail is applied to the wrong record — leaving an upstream HTLC to time out on-chain or crediting a preimage to the wrong payment.
- Confidence: low, inferred (key is stated; collision handling is unstated; authors assert second-payment safety)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — Timeout, retry and broadcast-retry state lives only in the host scheduler; nothing survives a restart unless the host invents persistence
- Topics: SR-04.5, SR-04.4
- Evidence: "Hitch keeps failed broadcasts, retry timers and the proposal, funding and unfunded timeouts on its channel document; here they are the host's scheduler, with the constants exported" (`sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:70`, `:121`); "the router expects the host to retry a retryable action (Hitch tries twenty times)" (`route.rs:13`). The stated host storage duties cover only snapshots and forward records; no persistence home for timers or retry counters is named.
- Failure: host process crashes; on restart the proposal, funding and unfunded timers and retry counters are gone — a channel whose funding never confirmed is never abandoned, failed broadcasts are not retried, and channels hang until an operator force-closes.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (delegation is stated; non-persistence inferred from the duty list)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — Refunding markets promise "half a sat each", a value an integer-satoshi ledger cannot represent
- Topics: SR-02.3
- Evidence: "Refunding --> Refunding: redeem paired outcomes at half a sat each" (SR-02.3 diagram); the ledger elsewhere is integer satoshis (asset tallies, integer constant-product boundary, 1 sat = 1 gwei map).
- Failure: a market whose collateral is an odd number of sats per pair (e.g. 1 sat) enters refunding; each outcome is owed 0.5 sats, which no output amount can carry — redemption either rounds with no stated consensus rule for the extra sat or produces dust-unspendable outputs, splitting the pair holders' claims.
- Confidence: medium (the half-sat rule is verbatim; non-representability and the missing rounding rule are inferred)
- Severity: medium (fires only if rules activate; today no live chain has a `rules` field, `chain.json:28`)
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — The equal-tip-hash consensus assertion only runs "when the oracle environment is set"
- Topics: SR-04 (header)
- Evidence: "Since 0.2.1 a chain-consensus suite opens five channels … with siding replaying to the same tip hash when the oracle environment is set" (SR-04 header). With the environment unset, the replay-parity comparison is skipped while the suite still passes.
- Failure: a run (developer machine, forked CI job, cache-restored step) with the variable unset reports green without the only check that Rust block construction reaches the reference tip; a consensus divergence ships.
- Confidence: medium (the conditional is stated; whether CI always sets it is not)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — The compatibility-contract ADR no longer describes the compatibility contract, in two independent ways
- Topics: SR-01.2, SR-03.2
- Evidence: "**Drift (ADR-0001 vs CI):** the record says CI pins 'all four reference repositories', but the workflow now pins six" (`ADR-0001:27` vs `ci.yml:14`); separately, "The crate deliberately commits only an applied block, unlike the reference's `prepare`" (`sidestr-evm/src/lib.rs:84`) — a stated behavioural divergence from the engine ADR-0001 declares to be the contract, recorded only in a crate doc.
- Failure: an engineer implementing a new rule reads ADR-0001 as the map of oracles, misses Reef and the teller entirely, or copies the reference's prepare-commit behaviour; the result agrees on fixtures but diverges on restart and state handling, and the contract document never told them.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift) for the pin count; no for the commit-semantics divergence

### F-10 — Three published crates (nostr, round, agent) have no reference oracle at all
- Topics: SR-01.1, SR-01.2, SR-04.5
- Evidence: the ladder pins spec, schema, blaketestnode, Hitch, Reef and teller (`ci.yml:14`–`30`); nothing covers `sidestr-nostr` (the kind-23600 transport the host design depends on, `protocol/mod.rs:105`), `sidestr-round` or `sidestr-agent`, all `publish = true` (`Cargo.toml:43`); ADR-0001's own premise is that "a green Rust-only test suite cannot prove wire or consensus parity" (`ADR-0001:27`).
- Failure: a wire-encoding or round-consensus change in these crates passes every Rust test and publishes; peers built on other implementations reject or misread kind-23600 events or federation messages — exactly the failure class the ladder exists to catch, absent for these surfaces.
- Confidence: medium (oracle absence from the stated list; impact follows ADR-0001's stated premise)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — Malformed points are dropped silently where the reference replies `reject`, with no port-side timeout
- Topics: SR-04.2, SR-04.5
- Evidence: "**Tension (Hitch vs port):** a point off the curve is refused at deserialisation and dropped without a `reject`, where Hitch replies with a reject at the proof-of-possession check" (`protocol/mod.rs:63`); every other message kind passes Hitch's validator (`wire_oracle.rs:185`); timeouts are host scheduler duties (`protocol/mod.rs:70`).
- Failure: a Rust peer receives an update containing one bad point, consumes it and says nothing; the counterparty waits indefinitely for a `reject` that never comes; the channel stalls until host-side timeouts — themselves unimplemented — tear it down, converting a protocol error into a force close.
- Confidence: medium (divergence stated; the hang consequence is inferred, since no port-side timeout is described)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-12 — One supervisor instance fronts producer, mirror and faucet
- Topics: SR-01.4
- Evidence: "Agentbox supervises the producer, mirror and faucet" (`config/sidechain/README.md:111`); the diagram shows a single AB node feeding the JS producer, mirror and Rust faucet, with the mirror the only described chain-data reader. No second instance, failover or HA appears anywhere in the material, for either chain.
- Failure: the supervisor or its host dies: block production halts and the mirror stops simultaneously, so every chain-watching consumer — including any future Hitch host's `on_spend` feed — goes blind at the same moment; liveness and custody observation share one failure point.
- Confidence: low, inferred (single instance is what the material shows; HA is simply never mentioned)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-13 — The deployed engine and released crates are frozen at baked pins; CI repins carry no redeploy or re-release obligation
- Topics: SR-01.2, SR-01.4
- Evidence: the producer "runs the upstream JavaScript engine baked at the upstream pins" (`config/sidechain/README.md:117`); pins are static in the record (Hitch "still `62f8e39`", blaketestnode `f1da4a6`, `ci.yml:19`–`22`) while at least one oracle was already repinned (Reef `648487a`→`2bd3cb8`, `ci.yml:25`) and crates were released around those pins (wallet 0.5.1, hitch 0.2.0/0.2.1, `TODO-unified.md:114`). No process ties a repin to redeploying the producer or re-releasing a crate.
- Failure: an upstream security or consensus fix lands; CI repins and goes green; the producer keeps serving the old baked revision and released crates were validated against the previous oracle — production and the compatibility contract silently diverge.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (baking and repinning stated; absence of a reconciliation policy inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — Estate policy makes sidechains the sole value instrument; both live sidechains are test-only and rules-free
- Topics: SR-01.4, SR-02 (business), SR-03 (business), SR-03.4
- Evidence: ADR-2096 "sidestr-sidechains-are-the-sole-value-instrument" is cited as governing (SR-03); the only live chains are the "test-only" `sidestr:dreamlab` and the sealed research chain `dreamlab-txbt4` (SR-01 business), whose documents carry no `rules` field (`chain.json:5`, `:28`); "the live estate chain has no rules field and therefore offers none of them" (SR-02 business); estate adoption is still open under N-10 (`TODO-unified.md:101`).
- Failure: accepting production responsibility under current policy means moving value onto chains the estate itself designates test-only, produced by a test engine, with no asset, market, EVM or bridge capability activated — there is no compliant production configuration to run.
- Confidence: high (facts stated; ADR-2096's acceptance status is not given)

- Marked by authors: no (the activation gap is Open in SR-02.4/SR-03.4; its contradiction with the sole-value-instrument policy is not marked)

### F-15 — The topics are verified against different commits, so their citations cannot all describe one system
- Topics: SR-01 (front matter), SR-02–SR-05 (front matter)
- Evidence: SR-01 records `verified_commit: {sidestr-rs: f77856dd…, visionflow: 62d16e02…}`; SR-02–05 record `sidestr-rs: dc8e1ca8…`, and SR-02 a third visionflow `e49fe424…`; SR-04's prose sits in the 0.2.1 era while SR-01's README narrative anchors on `f77856dd` — at least one topic's path:line set is stale relative to the others.
- Failure: an engineer verifying a finding checks out the wrong revision, confirms a stale claim or dismisses a real one because the cited lines do not match; the distillation's core warrant — every citation checked at a single pinned revision — does not hold uniformly.
- Confidence: high (stated in front matter; which topic is current is not)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Reorg semantics of applied sidechain blocks.** Hitch has `un_spend` for reorganised spends (`chain.rs:333`), implying reorgs occur, but no rewind path is described for EVM state committed "by block hash" (`sidestr-evm/src/state.rs:468` region) or for other committed rule state. I need the core's reorg/finality model before judging SR-03's commit design.
2. **Escrow of deposited sats.** Only the same-block withdrawal↔coinbase invariant is stated (SR-03.1); how deposits lock or burn sats, and whether supply conservation across deposit/withdrawal is enforced anywhere, is unstated.
3. **Mempool persistence and resubmission ownership.** Whether the producer's mempool survives restart, and who resubmits transactions admission refused as immature (`state.rs:628`, `:721`), is unstated.
4. **Mirror freshness and multi-chain supervision.** The latency, completeness and reorg depth of the mirror feed (which F-04 turns on), and whether one agentbox instance serves both `dreamlab` and `dreamlab-txbt4`, are unstated.
5. **Full text and status of ADR-2096 and ADR-2111.** Only titles and single line references appear; whether ADR-2096 is accepted, and whether ADR-2111's deleted "host payment store" overlaps the persistence duties SR-04.5 imposes on the host, cannot be determined here.

## sidestr-rs / premortem

# Post-mortem: client custody loss in a payment channel the operator stood up on the estate

**Scope and method.** The reconstructed incident is a loss of a client's channelled funds during live operation of a Rust `sidestr-hitch` peer against a reference (JavaScript) Hitch counterparty. The material available is the topic register only; the three root causes below are ranked by how directly this register already documents the enabling defect, and each chain of events is the mechanism an engineer can check against the cited lines. Steps that depend on facts the material does not show — that a host was built, that a 0.1-era channel existed — are labelled inferred. All citations are to the state recorded in this material.

---

### R-1 — A client channel was operated without the host service boundary the Hitch kernel requires
- Topics: SR-04.5, SR-04.4, SR-04.3, SR-01.3
- Chain of events:
  1. `sidestr-hitch` 0.2.1 ships with a chain-consensus suite that drives five channels through close, sweep, penalty and claim with `sidestr-core`'s `Chain` validating every spend (SR-04 developer summary); source parity is closed on the board's N-9 row.
  2. Adoption stays open under N-10 and no estate host exists (inferred: a minimal host was nevertheless assembled for the client).
  3. The host omits one or more duties the kernel explicitly outsources: chain watching for `on_spend`, broadcasting, the retry/timeout scheduler, or atomic snapshot persistence.
  4. The channel funding is spent on chain; with no host feeding the spend to `on_spend`, the kernel never classifies it and never produces the penalty or claim transactions.
  5. A revoked or expiring state is broadcast by the counterparty; the penalty window (`chain.rs:182`) and the claim margins lapse; the counterparty sweeps; the client's funds are gone.
