  6. Alternative sub-path: the host persists snapshots non-atomically; after a crash it restores a pre-revocation snapshot and broadcasts its own revoked commitment, which the counterparty punishes — total channel loss.
- Evidence: the host-duty enumeration with wallet funding, relay I/O, atomic snapshot storage, chain lookups, broadcasting and the scheduler (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:49`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:70`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:105`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:121`); chain handling requires the host to see the spend and broadcast the returned transactions (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/chain.rs:176`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/chain.rs:364`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/chain.rs:578`); the atomic-persistence assumption (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:30`); the explicit Open that no estate host supplies the boundary, with ADR-2111 proposed and unimplemented (`../project/docs/adr/ADR-2111-re-sequence-rgb-for-bridged-assets-and-delete-the-host-payment-store.md:95`, `../project/docs/TODO-unified.md:101`, `../project/docs/TODO-unified.md:114`); the invariant that source evidence cannot promote activation status (`docs/adr/ADR-2012-sidestr-settlement-is-ecosystem-canon.md:115`, SR-01.3); "Nobody in the estate runs one" (SR-04 business summary).
- Warning signs visible today: the Open marker itself; the 0.2.0 release widening the host's scheduler and chain-lookup duties; retry constants exported to a host that does not exist (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:13`); an eight-crate published surface whose glowing oracle suites address the kernel, not the service around it.
- Confidence: high that the boundary is absent and custody-critical; the incident sequence (an incomplete host was built) is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — A recorded port departure — the off-curve point silently dropped instead of rejected — stalled the peer protocol with the reference implementation
- Topics: SR-04.5, SR-04.2, SR-01.2
- Chain of events:
  1. A Rust Hitch peer interoperates with a reference JavaScript peer at the pinned revision `62f8e39` (the reference engine is what the estate ecosystem runs; CI validates fixtures and suites, not live inter-session conversations — inferred for live operation).
  2. A malformed point off the curve arrives in a peer message, from a bug or an attacker.
  3. The Rust port refuses it at deserialisation and drops it with no reply; the reference replies with `reject` at the proof-of-possession check — a deliberate, tested departure.
  4. The sender's state machine, expecting a `reject` (or an ack) to advance, waits; the Rust side has discarded the message and emits nothing.
  5. With an update pending, the machine accepts and proposes no further updates and forwards and settles nothing; the channel is frozen while payment timeouts run.
  6. The stall resolves only by unilateral close (inferred for the peer's timeout behaviour); in-flight payments fail, and any on-chain claim the Rust side must make depends on the same absent host duties as R-1 — funds at expiry are lost.
- Evidence: the Tension as recorded: "a point off the curve is refused at deserialisation and dropped without a `reject`, where Hitch replies with a reject at the proof-of-possession check" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:63`); the shape-check invariant that nothing is written until the message is verified in full (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:43`); the pending-update binding — nothing forwarded, settled or announced and no further update accepted or proposed (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:4`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:27`); the `reject` message's origin in Hitch's peer (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/CHANGELOG.md:45`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/CHANGELOG.md:66`); ADR-0001 making the reference the compatibility contract (`../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0001-reference-oracle-is-the-compatibility-contract.md:27`); the separate wire-oracle pin at `62f8e39` (`../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:20`, `../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:22`, `../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:126`) — the wire oracle passes "every message kind, `reject` included" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/tests/wire_oracle.rs:185`), which cannot exercise a reply the port never sends.
- Warning signs visible today: the port knowingly carries a documented behavioural divergence from the declared compatibility contract, recorded only as a code comment; the divergence sits exactly on the malformed-input path an attacker or a version-skew bug will hit.
- Confidence: high that the divergence exists as described; medium and inferred that this divergence, rather than another, produced the stall.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Recovery hit the version-1 snapshot trap and forced the channel through the flawed hitch 0.1
- Topics: SR-04.3, SR-04.4
- Chain of events:
  1. During the twelve months a channel exists that was opened under 0.1, or a 0.1-era backup is restored during the incident (inferred: today "nobody in the estate runs one", SR-04 business summary).
  2. Recovery is attempted under the current kernel; snapshot restore refuses version 1 because 0.1's single-party revocation keys let the owner spend its own revocation leaf.
  3. The operator is forced to downgrade to 0.1 to close the channel, because 0.2 cannot restore it — "such a channel must be closed with 0.1".
  4. Under 0.1, a commitment's owner can spend its own revocation leaf; the counterparty — aware of the forced downgrade, or routinely — broadcasts an old commitment and sweeps it through its own immediate revocation path before any penalty can engage, or the operator's own 0.1 broadcast exposes the flaw.
  5. Client funds are taken; alternatively, while the operator hunts for 0.1 binaries, the claim deadlines (`delay + CLAIM_MARGIN`) and HTLC expiries pass and the counterparty claims the expiring outputs.
- Evidence: the invariant that only version-2 snapshots restore and a version-1 snapshot is refused (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:29`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:182`); the 0.1 flaw and the closure requirement ("the 0.1 key let a commitment's owner spend its own revocation leaf, which is the flaw this release fixes"; "Any channel opened with the earlier version has to be closed with that version", SR-04 developer summary; `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/CHANGELOG.md:45`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/CHANGELOG.md:52`); the claim-margin deadline (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/machine.rs:1306`).
- Warning signs visible today: a published crate line whose predecessor contains a theft-class transaction-construction flaw and whose only migration story is "close it with the flawed version".
- Confidence: medium — the mechanism is fully documented; the existence of any 0.1-era channel or backup after today is inferred, and the material states none exists now.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — `sidestr-evm` deliberately diverges from the reference's prepare-time state commit, against its own compatibility contract
- Topics: SR-03.2, SR-01.2
- Evidence: "The crate deliberately commits only an applied block, unlike the reference's `prepare`" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/lib.rs:84`), while ADR-0001 makes the reference the compatibility contract (`../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0001-reference-oracle-is-the-compatibility-contract.md:27`).
- Failure: if the `evm` rule is ever activated, a Rust follower and the reference disagree about durable state between block proposal and application; after a refused or reorganised block the two resume from different retained-height snapshots (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/state.rs:161`), splitting followers and allowing conflicting balances to be served.
- Confidence: high that the divergence is as stated; the failure is conditional on activation.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — Recovery correctness rests on an unverifiable host property: atomic snapshot persistence
- Topics: SR-04.3
- Evidence: "the host persists the full recovery boundary atomically before sending anything the call returned, and restores the previous snapshot if the save fails" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:30`) — an assumption about code that does not exist (SR-04.5 Open).
- Failure: a host that saves after transmitting, or non-atomically, crashes mid-exchange; on restart it reloads a pre-update snapshot after the revocation secret was revealed, broadcasts its own revoked commitment as if current, and the counterparty takes the entire channel through the penalty path.
- Confidence: high that the kernel depends on the property; the violating host is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — Channel snapshots concentrate signing keys, revocation secrets and preimages in one blob whose protection is a host duty
- Topics: SR-04.3
- Evidence: the snapshot contains "the signing key, the revocation basepoint and per-state secrets, and preimages" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:22`); "Snapshot storage must be protected as wallet key material" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:24`); no host exists to enforce this (SR-04.5 Open).
- Failure: a stand-in host persists snapshots in an ordinary database; a single read compromise yields every channel's keys and every outstanding preimage — total channel loss plus theft of in-flight payments.
- Confidence: medium — contents documented; storage practice inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — hitch 0.1, published with an owner-spendable revocation leaf, remains installable
- Topics: SR-04.3, SR-01.1
- Evidence: 0.1 "let a commitment's owner spend its own revocation leaf" (SR-04 developer summary; `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/CHANGELOG.md:52`); hitch is one of the eight published crates (`../sidestr-rs/Cargo.toml:43`, SR-01.1).
- Failure: any integrator resolving `sidestr-hitch` 0.1 — or an operator forced to downgrade as in R-3 — runs theft-enabling transaction construction with no in-band warning beyond the changelog.
- Confidence: high that 0.1 is published and flawed; downstream exposure inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — Snapshot restore may accept self-consistent state without a stated cross-check against the chain's committed root
- Topics: SR-03.3
- Evidence: restore "verifies version, configuration, root and time" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/state.rs:305`); the material never states the root is compared with the coinbase-committed root at that height rather than with the snapshot's own recorded root (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/state.rs:161`).
- Failure: a substituted snapshot whose worlds and recorded root agree internally resumes a follower at a forged balance; the stated aim of not treating "unverified serialised state as consensus truth" holds only if the root is externally checked. Verify the provenance of the compared root.
- Confidence: low, inferred — the comparison's provenance is unstated.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — Canonical JSON is described as sorted by "byte order" but RFC 8785 sorts by UTF-16 code units
- Topics: SR-05.2
- Evidence: "keys ascending by byte order, no whitespace" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-reserve/src/lib.rs:577`), in the same sentence that fixes RFC 8785 (JCS) as the canonical form; the cross-checks are `serde_jcs` and the teller's `jcs` (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-reserve/src/lib.rs:60`).
- Failure: an attestation whose credit ids or keys contain characters where UTF-8 byte order and UTF-16 code-unit order differ canonicalises differently from the teller; the digest a reserve key signed no longer matches the consumer's recomputation and the consuming bridge or mint refuses the statement — signed reserves become unverifiable.
- Confidence: medium — rests on the material's own wording conflicting with the standard it cites.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — Reference-set documentation is behind the workflow: ADR-0001 says four repositories, CI pins six, and one pin has already rotated
- Topics: SR-01.2, SR-01.1
- Evidence: the authors' Drift marker — "the record says CI pins 'all four reference repositories', but the workflow now pins six" (`../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0001-reference-oracle-is-the-compatibility-contract.md:27`, `../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:14`); Reef repinned `648487a`→`2bd3cb8` (`../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:25`); the README crate table was likewise stale until 2026-10-18 (SR-01.1).
- Failure: an operator or auditor working from the ADR is unaware the teller and Reef references define validated behaviour; a future pin rotation lands as a CI diff with no matching decision record, and what a production build was validated against silently diverges from what anyone believes is deployed.
- Confidence: high — stated.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-08 — Market refunds pay "half a sat each" in a ledger whose conservation is integral
- Topics: SR-02.3, SR-02.2
- Evidence: refunding redeems "paired outcomes at half a sat each" (SR-02.3 diagram); the asset rule enforces integer conservation with checked arithmetic (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/assets.rs:160`) and pools use an integer constant-product boundary (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/pool.rs:213`); no even-collateral constraint on market creation is stated (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/markets.rs:138`).
- Failure: a market with odd total collateral cannot refund exactly — the transition either rounds (creating or burning half a sat against conservation) or refuses (holders of complete pairs stranded past expiry). Check whether opening constrains collateral parity.
- Confidence: low–medium — the rule is stated; odd-collateral creatability is unstated.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — Client-facing value sits on a chain documented as test-only, level 1, with no rules field
- Topics: SR-01.4, SR-02.4, SR-03.4
- Evidence: "`sidestr:dreamlab` is still a test-only chain produced by the upstream JavaScript engine" (SR-01 business summary); it is level 1 and its document reaches `containmentDigest` without a `rules` field (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/dreamlab/chain.json:5`, `../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/dreamlab/chain.json:28`); SR-02.4 and SR-03.4 carry Open markers on exactly this.
- Failure: a client's funds operate under a sealed document whose containment is a digest only, with no rules overlay to arbitrate disputes and a producer that is the upstream JavaScript engine rather than any estate-validated component.
- Confidence: high that the status is as stated; client-value exposure is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, SR-02.4 and SR-03.4; SR-01.4 Open on the producer)

### F-10 — A reorganised close is undone without reopening the channel
- Topics: SR-04.4
- Evidence: `un_spend` "undoes a reorganised spend without reopening the channel" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/chain.rs:333`).
- Failure: after a one-block reorg the funding output is live again but the channel is finished; the only exit is a fresh unilateral close — fees plus the delay — and if the host stops watching the funding after `un_spend`, even that is not triggered.
- Confidence: medium — the behaviour is stated; the consequences are partly inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — Unconfirmed claims are republished on a fixed six-block cadence with no stated fee escalation
- Topics: SR-04.4
- Evidence: `watch_outputs` "claims republished after six blocks" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/chain.rs:578`); the material states no fee-bumping.
- Failure: a claim that underpays sits below the fee floor; rebroadcasting identical bytes cannot fix that; an expiring HTLC output is claimed by the counterparty at timeout.
- Confidence: low, inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — The fixed 1 sat = 1 gwei mapping makes gwei-fractional value unrepresentable on the chain side
- Topics: SR-03.1
- Evidence: "One sat maps to one gwei inside the overlay" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/lib.rs:5`); the coinbase must pay withdrawals exactly (SR-03.1 invariant; preparation "checks both the root and withdrawal payments", `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/state.rs:468`).
- Failure: a withdrawal of a sub-gwei residual (left by gas accounting) cannot be paid in integer sats; either the withdrawal is refused and value is stranded in the overlay, or rounding breaks the exact coinbase payment check. Verify the rounding policy.
- Confidence: low, inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-13 — Canonical attestation bytes are defined by a sixth external repository added without a decision-record update
- Topics: SR-01.2, SR-05.2
- Evidence: the teller at `7c00cea` is "the oracle for `webledgers-teller` and the RFC 8785 JCS of `sidestr-reserve`'s canonical attestation bytes" (`../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:30`); ADR-0001 still describes four repositories (`../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0001-reference-oracle-is-the-compatibility-contract.md:27`).
- Failure: the bytes a reserve key signs are fixed by an upstream repository's revision; a repin of the teller changes the signed format, and an estate that has not recorded the teller in its decision records learns of the change only as CI fixture drift.
- Confidence: medium — facts stated; risk inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (the pin-count drift is marked; the teller's authority role is not flagged as a risk)

### F-14 — The live Rust faucet depends on wallet releases whose payment-request oracle was repinned after publication
- Topics: SR-01.4, SR-01.2, SR-01.3
- Evidence: the Rust faucet is a live agentbox component beside the producer and mirror (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:111`, SR-01.4 diagram); `sidestr-wallet` 0.5.1 shipped "BIP 21 with the Reef oracle" (SR-01.3) and Reef was repinned `648487a`→`2bd3cb8` (`../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:25`); adoption remains open under N-10 (`../project/docs/TODO-unified.md:101`, `../project/docs/TODO-unified.md:114`).
- Failure: the deployed faucet was built and reviewed against the old Reef pin; if it parses BIP 21 requests, it accepts or rejects request forms differently from the current oracle, and a malformed request either halts payout or misdirects it. Whether the faucet uses BIP 21 at all is unstated — verify.
- Confidence: low, inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — Refused immature transactions rely on someone resubmitting them; no requeue is stated
- Topics: SR-02.4
- Evidence: admission refuses immature BIP 68 or `nLockTime` transactions so that "they can be submitted again once their height arrives" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/state.rs:631`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/state.rs:721`); the scheduler class that would resubmit is a host duty in the channel world (SR-04.5).
- Failure: a channel sweep or refund refused at admission is dropped by a submitting host with no retry logic; the refund is late, and on the channel side a missed window becomes a counterparty claim.
- Confidence: low–medium — admission behaviour is stated; the absence of a requeue is inferred from the material's silence.
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

## solid-pod-rs / critical

Findings ranked by production damage — security and custody first. Author markers are Tension / Debt / Drift(DOC-DRIFT) / Open / DIVERGENCE as used in the material.

### F-01 — COPY authorises the destination only; acl:Read on the source is never checked
- Topics: SP-03.8, SP-02.11, SP-10.4
- Evidence: SP-03.8 states "COPY authorises Write on the destination only — it never checks acl:Read on the SOURCE" (solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:2565), and handle_copy calls storage.get(source) directly (lib.rs:2585) with no read gate. SP-10.4 lists it as window W6 among items "recorded, not closed", with no closure note even at SP-10's newer verified commit.
- Failure: On a path-multitenant server, any principal with Write anywhere runs `COPY` with `Source: /victimPod/private.ttl` into their own pod and reads the bytes — cross-tenant exfiltration through a route registered in the default table (SP-02.11, lib.rs:4778).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in SP-03.8; W6 in SP-10.4) — but classified as a "window", not an open confidentiality bypass.

### F-02 — The estate pin (alpha.12) predates the alpha.13 fixes for two confidentiality bugs, and the record set itself is split across two commits
- Topics: SP-09.7, SP-10.4, SP-10.8, SP-03.9, SP-08.14
- Evidence: agentbox's Nix pin is v0.5.0-alpha.12, rev d64131b, "equal to this topic's verified alpha.12 HEAD" (SP-09.7). The glob child-ACL leak is stated live at that commit (SP-03.9 N2: the merged body includes children with stricter sidecars) and is "CLOSED in alpha.13" only per SP-10.4 W7 (CHANGELOG.md:22, lib.rs:2797-2824); the "anonymous MCP reads / WAC sidecar bypass" critical is likewise "CLOSED in 0.5.0-alpha.13" (SP-10.8 F2, CHANGELOG.md:26). SP-01–SP-09 carry verified_commit d64131b; SP-10 carries 193f4962 — contradicting the baseline's own same-commit rule (SP-10.11).
- Failure: you deploy the pinned alpha.12 believing the later docs; the default-on LDP glob route leaks private child resources (no feature flag needed), and any pod with MCP enabled leaks anonymously. No alpha.13 row exists in the pin matrix, so nothing tells you which artefact carries the fixes.
- Confidence: high on the textual facts; medium (inferred) that alpha.12 is what would actually be run.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: the leaks and closures are individually marked; the pin-sits-below-the-fixes consequence is not.

### F-03 — MRC20 deposits credit on mempool-visible anchors, with no confirmation and no un-credit path
- Topics: SP-07.9, SP-07.17, SP-07.19, SP-07.13
- Evidence: verify_mrc20_anchor accepts "a head still in the mempool… (intact, not verified)" (SP-07.9 N4, solid-pod-rs/src/mrc20.rs:1004); mempool-side verify answers with is_intact, "so an unconfirmed head passes" (SP-07.19, mempool.rs:752). The deposit path credits immediately after that verify plus an outpoint replay guard (SP-07.17, handlers/pay.rs:652). The ledger is a stored number whose only reversal is reverse_payout for payouts (SP-07.13, payments.rs:536) — no reversal for credits.
- Failure: an anchoring/funding tx that is RBF-replaced or evicted from the mempool after the credit leaves a permanently credited balance with no chain backing, spendable through /pay/.swap or /pay/.withdraw; equally, a deposit can be spent before a single confirmation.
- Confidence: medium — the mempool acceptance and instant credit are stated; the RBF/eviction exploitation is inferred from standard Bitcoin behaviour.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (stated as invariant/notes, not flagged as a problem)

### F-04 — The whole value-bearing /pay/* surface is always-on with no feature flag, on a stored-number ledger the ADR says must be replaced
- Topics: SP-07.14, SP-07.15, SP-07.18, SP-10.8
- Evidence: "the whole /pay/* surface is registered with the SAME gating as /pay/.info — always on, with no payments feature flag" (SP-07.14, lib.rs:4625) — while git/provenance is default-off (SP-01.7). The lock is "PROCESS-wide… Two replicas serving the same pod storage share no lock" and the README audit records "non-atomic payment state" as a reproduced critical (SP-07.15, lib.rs:200); alpha.13 holds the lock across anchor charges (SP-10.8 F4) but the replica gap stands. ADR-2008 D4/D5/D7 are "not built" — the balance remains a stored number (SP-07.18), and the record's own Tension notes D3 "retires" MRC20 as a token rail while /pay/.buy and /pay/.withdraw still execute trades (pay.rs:1362, :1451).
- Failure: the most dangerous surface is the only unconditionally mounted one. Concurrent or crash-interrupted mutations to the ledger, order book or pool lose or duplicate value; recovery exists only for externally broadcast intents (SP-07.15 RC). The README itself says do not carry value through these routes.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE SP-07.14; audit F4; ADR-2008 Tension/Open) — the "always-on while everything else is default-off" inversion is what they under-play.

### F-05 — Every correctness mechanism assumes exactly one process
- Topics: SP-05.4, SP-05.5, SP-07.15, SP-06.8, SP-10.4
- Evidence: the NIP-98 replay store is process-local — "a restart reopens the window for one TTL" and the seam has exactly one implementor (SP-05.5, replay_store.rs:90); PAYMENT_STATE_LOCK is process-wide (SP-07.15, lib.rs:200); the pod-creation limiter "one POST /.pods per IP per day" hangs off AppState (SP-06.8, lib.rs:465) and the notification fan-out is an in-process broadcast channel (SP-08.2). W3/W4 in SP-10.4 record the first two as open.
- Failure: scaling to two replicas silently accepts replayed NIP-98 credentials (auth bypass), double-applies payment mutations (custody), multiplies the creation-flood limit, and splits notification streams — with nothing signalling the assumption broke.
- Confidence: high for replay and payment (stated); medium (inferred) for the limiter/notifications extension.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — W3/W4 and the replay DIVERGENCE are marked; the single-instance assumption as a system-wide property is not.

### F-06 — Default CORS echoes any origin with credentials while the WAC origin gate is compiled out
- Topics: SP-06.10, SP-04.8, SP-01.6, SP-02.3
- Evidence: "an empty allowed_origins list means every Origin is echoed back — a local-dev default, not a production one" (SP-06.10 N2, the CorsHeaders middleware at lib.rs:3087), and wildcard+credentials deliberately degrades to echoing the concrete Origin with Access-Control-Allow-Credentials (SP-06.10 invariant). acl-origin "is NOT in any default feature set… a stock build ignores the Origin header entirely" (SP-01.6 O1; SP-04.8 Ignored branch).
- Failure: a default deployment approves credentialed cross-origin reads and writes from every website. Any bearer credential reachable from browser JS — the forge f1.* push token (SP-08.11), the dev bearer, a client-side OIDC access token — becomes usable cross-site, with no origin check at either the CORS or the WAC layer.
- Confidence: high that both defaults behave as stated; the composite bypass is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: each half is marked; the combination is not.

### F-07 — Solid-OIDC accepts tokens regardless of audience; discovery and the crypto path disagree
- Topics: SP-05.8, SP-05.7, SP-10.1, SP-10.6
- Evidence: "aud is NOT validated — only the claim's PRESENCE is enforced" (SP-05.8 D1, oidc/mod.rs:812); extract_webid has no cnf.webid branch, so the LWS10 delta is unshipped (D2, :864); discovery advertises only ES256/RS256 while the verifier also dispatches EdDSA (D3, :184) — SP-10.1 calls invariant 6 "the one currently BROKEN".
- Failure: once OIDC is enabled, a token minted for a different resource server verifies here — cross-audience token replay — and DPoP binding semantics fall short of what callers assume from the IdP's cnf jkt output (SP-05.16).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (three DIVERGENCEs, pinned by tests per SP-10.6)

### F-08 — The only shipped IdP user store is in-memory, while the server mounts password routes and the audit's mitigation is "don't expose the IdP router"
- Topics: SP-05.18, SP-02.9, SP-10.8
- Evidence: SP-05.18 lists exactly one UserStore implementor, InMemoryUserStore (user_store.rs:70). The server's always-on route table registers POST /login/password, /account/password/reset and /account/password/change (SP-02.9 D4–D6, lib.rs:4634–4658) — no feature gate is shown for these, unlike the gated discovery routes. Audit F3: "forged IdP identity — mitigated by not exposing the optional IdP router" (SP-10.8).
- Failure: a restart deletes every account, password hash and session — password login and reset break silently. And the F3 mitigation is contradicted by the server binary itself registering account routes.
- Confidence: medium — the sole implementor and route registration are stated; which store backs those server routes is not.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (F3 is marked; the persistence loss and the route contradiction are not)

### F-09 — The Bitcoin-anchoring pipeline fails silently and cannot even be started
- Topics: SP-07.3, SP-07.12, SP-07.20, SP-07.7
- Evidence: load_trail maps every storage read error to Ok(None) (trail_store.rs:94-100), so a transient fault is indistinguishable from "no trail" and build_anchorer returning None is reported as "not configured"; the handler's own error text still claims a pay-token is required (SP-07.3 N4, DRIFT-marked). GitmarkAnchorer::anchor "holds no lock across load, advance, broadcast and save" — two concurrent anchors spend the same head (mempool.rs:994). "Nothing outside the tests calls genesis… no route, CLI verb or boot step writes a git-mark trail file" (SP-07.3 N5; SP-07.20 N2). A failed epoch batch anchor "is swallowed" (SP-07.7).
- Failure: an operator who believes writes are Bitcoin-anchored gets silent non-anchoring — a storage hiccup reads as "not configured", concurrency corrupts trail state, and on a fresh pod the git-mark branch is unreachable from this repository at all.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt ×2, Open, DRIFT on the message) — marked individually, but the composite "the headline feature cannot run" is not drawn.

### F-10 — Anchoring trail secrets are persisted under a servable /.well-known/ path
- Topics: SP-07.20, SP-07.12, SP-02.11
- Evidence: save_gitmark_trail writes "/.well-known/gitmark/name.json with the base secret" (trail_store.rs:133) beside a secret-free blocktrails.json (:140). Trail files live in pod storage, and /.well-known/ paths not explicitly routed fall through to the LDP catch-all GET (SP-02.11) under whatever ACL governs them. The MRC20 store needs to_public to strip keys "before a trail is served" (SP-07.12) — showing such files are treated as servable content.
- Failure: any ACL granting read on that subtree (a broad owner-everywhere or public default rule) publishes the Bitcoin key that controls the trail; an attacker advances or redirects the chain of marks.
- Confidence: low (inferred) — the material never states the ACL covering /.well-known/gitmark/**.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — "Atomic" storage is two renames; a crash between them corrupts the body/metadata pair
- Topics: SP-06.4, SP-10.4, SP-10.3, SP-10.8
- Evidence: "the body and its sidecar are two renames — a crash between them leaves a mismatched pair", recorded as the 2026-08-19 audit finding that filesystem writes "violate the advertised atomic-storage contract" (SP-06.4, fs.rs:111); W2 in SP-10.4 "recorded, not closed"; SP-10.8 F5 carries no closure note. The write-then-mark window (W1) is likewise non-atomic and only made observable by the receipt (SP-10.3).
- Failure: a crash mid-write serves a resource with another revision's ETag/content-type — conditional PUTs on stale ETags clobber content; crashes after the storage put leave writes unmarked and unprovable.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE SP-06.4; audit F5; W1/W2)

### F-12 — Chain verification and deposits depend on one public third-party explorer, defaulting to testnet
- Topics: SP-07.11, SP-06.9, SP-07.19
- Evidence: "ONE URL, no fallback chain" and "DEFAULT_MEMPOOL_URL is the PUBLIC mempool.space testnet4 explorer" (mempool.rs:60), with a startup log line the only signal (SP-07.11). Deposit verification, anchor verification and the proxy all resolve through it (SP-06.9 USE1–4; SP-07.19's walk via MempoolLookup). The walk checks keys and spend order but "the amount is not checked" (SP-07.19).
- Failure: explorer outage or rate-limiting halts deposits and verification; a lying or compromised explorer can vouch for UTXOs that do not exist (IP pinning defeats DNS rebinding, not dishonesty); and a default-config pod silently anchors to testnet while operators assume mainnet.
- Confidence: high on the defaults and single-URL; medium (inferred) on the dishonest-explorer credit path.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — the D7 single-URL DIVERGENCE is marked; the trust and testnet-default angles are not.

### F-13 — A default server build cannot verify NIP-98 at all; the working credential is a "dev bearer"
- Topics: SP-01.5, SP-01.7, SP-01.8, SP-05.1
- Evidence: "without nip98-schnorr the verifier is a fail-closed stub" (nip98.rs:376); nip98-schnorr is in no default feature set and reaches the server only through the install feature (server Cargo.toml:163); the server default is empty (SP-01.7, server Cargo.toml:123). The unconditional credential path left standing is verify_dev_bearer, an HMAC dev token (lib.rs:573).
- Failure: an operator running the default binary finds every NIP-98 agent treated as anonymous — total auth outage on any non-public pod — and the workaround the code nudges toward is a shared-secret dev token in production.
- Confidence: medium — assembled from the feature graph; whether any shipped binary forces nip98-schnorr on is not stated.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (the stub is marked as a fail-closed INVARIANT; the operational consequence is not)

### F-14 — The bare binary's production posture: plaintext, no rate limit, 50 MiB bodies, silent port wandering
- Topics: SP-10.5, SP-10.10, SP-02.7, SP-02.3
- Evidence: tls and rate-limit are both default-off (SP-10.5, server Cargo.toml:126/:129); the request body cap is 50 MiB (SP-10.10 B1, lib.rs:351) with no throttling by default; on AddrInUse the server binds a shifted port across up to 11 attempts and warns (SP-02.7, main.rs:162), re-reading base_url from the shifted listener.
- Failure: a default deploy is cheaply exhaustible by unauthenticated 50 MiB POSTs; behind an orchestrator a silently shifted port splits health checks from the real listener and lets a stray second instance coexist undetected; TLS is implicitly the operator's job.
- Confidence: high on the behaviours; medium on the incident framing.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — SP-10.5 states the posture as design; the port-shift hazard is unmarked.

### F-15 — Notification delivery is lossy by design and the pod emits almost no telemetry
- Topics: SP-08.2, SP-08.17, SP-10.12, SP-08.16
- Evidence: the WebSocket pump's broadcast channel "drops messages for a slow receiver" (SP-08.2); the legacy driver's outbound mpsc is capacity 256 and "lossy by design" under back-pressure (SP-08.17). Prometheus export is out of scope — "a bare solid-pod-rs-server exposes NO metrics endpoint"; only two opt-in counters exist, and WAC denials, replay rejections, quota refusals and provenance failures are "logged but never counted" (SP-10.12).
- Failure: estate consumers streaming agent events over the WebSocket channel (SP-08.16, agentbox AB-13) silently miss updates under load with no counter to detect the loss; operators cannot see denial, replay or payment-failure rates without log scraping.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — the metrics DIVERGENCE is marked; the lossy channels are stated as deliberate design, not as a problem.

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Which commit is the production artefact.** SP-01–SP-09 verify d64131b (alpha.12); SP-10 verifies 193f4962 and cites alpha.13 CHANGELOG entries. Whether an alpha.13 tag/publish exists (releases are tag-driven, SP-09.6) and what it contains beyond the three named fixes.
2. **ACL coverage of storage-internal files**: /.well-known/gitmark/** (F-10), .quota.json, agent.did.json, and the nsec private-key documents provision_pod_keys writes (SP-05.19) — whether the LDP catch-all can serve any of them under default or provisioned ACLs.
3. **How the deposit credit amount is derived** — from trail state strings, transfer ops, or UTXO value — given verification never checks amounts (SP-07.19), and whether anything downstream imposes a confirmation threshold.
4. **What backs /login/password and /account/password/*** on the server — the UserStore implementation, its persistence, and whether audit F3's "don't expose the IdP router" mitigation was ever valid for these routes.
5. **The proxy trust model for X-Forwarded-Proto/Host** in the NIP-98 signed-URL reconstruction (SP-05.1 N1) — whether forwarding headers are honoured from any peer or only a trusted proxy, and whether a configured base_url pins the comparison.

## solid-pod-rs / premortem

# Post-mortem — solid-pod-rs production incident (custody/data/security class)

Basis: the diagrams-as-code corpus only; no source was read. Root causes below are ranked candidates drawn from defects the material itself records as open at its latest verified commit (SP-01–SP-09 at `d64131b`; SP-10 at `193f496`, the alpha.13-era register). Where a deployment fact is assumed rather than stated (e.g., two replicas), it is flagged. SP-10.8 records the MCP bypass (F2) and the intra-process payment race (F4) as closed in alpha.13 — the incident is therefore modelled on the gaps that register still lists as open.

---

### R-1 — Payment-ledger split-brain: the process-wide lock and the local stored balance let two replicas diverge
- Topics: SP-07.15, SP-07.18, SP-09.7, SP-10.4 (W4), SP-10.8 (F4)
- Chain of events: The operator scales the pod to two replicas over one pod storage — a normal HA step. Each replica holds its own in-process `PAYMENT_STATE_LOCK` and persists the ledger through `write_ledger`. Two concurrent `/pay` mutations land on different replicas; both read the same state, both mutate, both persist — last writer wins. A debit recorded on one replica is lost or a credit double-applied; `recover_payment_intents` only compensates where an external broadcast was involved, and "an intent with no payout receipt is RETAINED with a warning — the ledger cannot restore a debit it has no receipt for". The client's balance is now wrong in a direction the operator cannot reconcile from either replica.
- Evidence: SP-07.15: "the lock is PROCESS-wide. Two replicas serving the same pod storage share no lock, and the README's audit line records 'non-atomic payment state' as a reproduced critical finding" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:200`); "The storage API has NO cross-resource CAS primitive, so the guard must be held across the replay check, the ledger/order/pool/trail mutation and the persist" (`handlers/pay.rs:302, :169, :198, :306`); the recovery gap at `handlers/pay.rs:246`. SP-07.18: D4/D5/D7 "not built" (adr/ADR-2008…:97-98), `get_balance` reads the stored map (`payments.rs:412`) and `write_ledger` is still the authority (`payments.rs:913`). SP-09.7 Open: "nothing states what replaces it" (`lib.rs:200`). SP-10.4 W4 — in the **later** verified register — still lists the lock as process-wide, not storage-wide. SP-10.8 F4's alpha.13 fix (`CHANGELOG.md:56`) only widens the lock's coverage inside one process.
- Warning signs visible today: the README's own audit reproduces this as critical; ADR-2008 makes fixing it (D7) a precondition of the port that has not happened; the README says not to carry value through `/pay` routes (SP-07.14, SP-10.8).
- Confidence: high — the divergence is stated verbatim; only the multi-replica deployment is assumed.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Credit granted against an unconfirmed anchor head: deposits verify while still in the mempool
- Topics: SP-07.17, SP-07.19, SP-07.9, SP-07.13
- Chain of events: A client (or attacker) submits an MRC20 deposit. `verify_mrc20_anchor` walks the trail and accepts a head still in the mempool; the anchorer's `verify` answers with `is_intact`, "so an unconfirmed head passes". The handler then applies `credit_by_outpoint` — the outpoint is the one-time receipt — and the caller's ticker balance is credited. The deposit transaction is never confirmed (minimal fee, evicted, replaced). The credit stands: credits have no reversal primitive (only `reverse_payout`, for payouts), and the credited balance is spent via `/pay/.swap` or `/pay/.withdraw` before anything settles. Unbacked tokens have left the pod.
- Evidence: SP-07.19: "is_verified needs every mark confirmed (`blocktrail.rs:841`), is_intact also accepts a head still in the mempool (`blocktrail.rs:849`). verify answers with is_intact, so an unconfirmed head passes" (`mempool.rs:752`); SP-07.9: "A head still in the mempool is still accepted (intact, not verified)" (`mrc20.rs:1004`); SP-07.17: credit happens "after verify_mrc20_anchor … and a replay guard on the anchor outpoint, credit_by_outpoint credits the caller's TICKER balance" (`handlers/pay.rs:652`); SP-07.13: credits are receipt-once (`payments.rs:450`), the only undo is `reverse_payout` (`payments.rs:536`).
- Warning signs visible today: the `is_verified`/`is_intact` distinction exists precisely because confirmation matters, yet the answering predicate is the weaker one; the README bars carrying value on these routes.
- Confidence: medium — the mempool acceptance and the credit sequence are stated; the absence of any confirmation gate at credit time is inferred from the material not showing one.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — COPY reads the source without acl:Read: write access to any destination exfiltrates private resources
- Topics: SP-03.8, SP-10.4 (W6)
- Chain of events: A principal holds acl:Write on some container it controls. It issues `COPY` with a `Source` header naming a private resource elsewhere on the pod. `handle_copy` authorises Write on the destination only, fetches the source body through storage, writes it into the destination — and copies the source's acl sidecar alongside. The attacker then reads its own copy. Every resource on the pod that storage can read but the principal could not is exfiltrable this way.
- Evidence: SP-03.8: "COPY authorises Write on the destination only — it never checks acl:Read on the SOURCE, so a principal with write rights on the destination can copy a resource it could not have read directly" — Write enforced at `lib.rs:2565`, `get(source)` at `:2585`, `put(dest)` at `:2591`, sidecar copied at `:2598`. SP-10.4 W6 restates the open window (`lib.rs:2563`); unlike W7 (glob), no closure is recorded anywhere.
- Warning signs visible today: it is a standing DIVERGENCE in both the alpha.12-era topic and the alpha.13-era failure register.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Trail reads collapse every storage fault into "no trail exists"
- Topics: SP-07.12, SP-07.3, SP-07.20
- Evidence: "`load_trail` maps every storage read error to `Ok(None)` (`trail_store.rs:94-100`) … `load_gitmark_trail` copies the fault (`trail_store.rs:186`), so a transient storage fault makes `build_anchorer` return `None`, which the anchor handler reports as 'not configured'" (SP-07.12, Debt); `build_anchorer` at `handlers/prov.rs:128`, the misleading message at `prov.rs:383` (SP-07.3 N4).
- Failure: A transient FS/permissions error during an Epoch/Always anchor makes the pod skip anchoring and answer "not configured" — the write is git-marked but never notarised, and the storage fault is recorded nowhere. Worse direction (inferred): `GitmarkAnchorer::genesis`'s "trail already stored?" check (`mempool.rs:959`) reads through the same fault-masking loader, so a transient error during genesis can report "not stored" and overwrite the existing secret-bearing trail file, orphaning every prior on-chain anchor.
- Confidence: high for the fault-masking and the skipped anchor (stated); the genesis-overwrite direction is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-02 — Concurrent anchors double-spend the trail head
- Topics: SP-07.20
- Evidence: "anchor holds no lock across load, advance, broadcast and save. Two concurrent anchors on one trail would both spend the same head, and the second broadcast fails as a double spend after the first has saved" (`mempool.rs:994`); `broadcast_and_save` at `mempool.rs:890`, `save_gitmark_trail` at `trail_store.rs:191` (SP-07.20, Debt).
- Failure: Two epoch-filling writes (or a write and an operator-driven anchor) race; both derive from the same head; one transaction confirms, the saved trail and the published `blocktrails.json` now describe one branch while the other broadcast is a permanent double-spend — the second caller's commit SHA is silently never anchored, or the walked chain diverges from what `verify_anchor_report` can follow.
- Confidence: high (stated).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-03 — Body and metadata are two renames; a crash splits the pair
- Topics: SP-06.4, SP-10.4 (W2), SP-10.8 (F5)
- Evidence: `put` writes the `.meta.json` sidecar **first**, then the body (`fs.rs:111` sequence); "the rename is atomic per file, but the body and its sidecar are two renames — a crash between them leaves a mismatched pair" (SP-06.4 DIVERGENCE); SP-10.8 F5 records it as an open audit finding (`fs.rs:111`); ETag is a SHA-256 over the body (`fs.rs:107`).
- Failure: A crash after the meta rename and before the body rename leaves **new** metadata (ETag, content-type) over the **old** bytes. A subsequent conditional PUT with `If-Match` on the new ETag succeeds believing the store holds the new body; caches keyed on the ETag serve stale bytes as current. Silent corruption with a self-consistent-looking ETag.
- Confidence: high (order and window stated; the conditional-write consequence follows from the stated ETag semantics).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in SP-06.4; audit finding F5 in SP-10.8)

### F-04 — The replay store is process-local: replicas and restarts reopen the window
- Topics: SP-05.4, SP-05.5, SP-05.1, SP-10.4 (W3)
- Evidence: "A process-local store loses every entry on restart, so each restart reopens the window for one TTL" (SP-05.5 contract note); SP-10.4 W3 (`lib.rs:192`); the freshness window is two-sided 60 s (`nip98.rs:28`, SP-05.2); the REST path's replay check sits at `lib.rs:547` (SP-05.1).
- Failure: A captured NIP-98 event is replayed against a second replica, or within one TTL after a restart/redeploy, before `created_at` leaves the 60 s window; signature, URL, method and payload hash all verify; the write or pay action executes twice.
- Confidence: high that the store is process-local and the window 60 s (both stated); the interception precondition is a deployment assumption.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (stated as contract property and an open window; only the adjacent single-implementor gap carries a DIVERGENCE label)

### F-05 — OIDC access tokens verify regardless of audience
- Topics: SP-05.8, SP-05.7, SP-10.6 (D1)
- Evidence: "`validate_aud = false`" (`oidc/mod.rs:812`); only the claim's **presence** is enforced because `SolidOidcClaims::aud` has no serde default (`mod.rs:670`); "A token minted for another audience verifies here; a deployment needing audience restriction must enforce it above this API" (SP-05.8 D1); SP-10.6: "documented AND PINNED by tests, not fixed".
- Failure: A token issued by the shared IdP for a different resource server in the estate is presented to the pod; signature, DPoP binding and expiry pass and the token's `webid`/`sub` is authorised against WAC — cross-service token confusion becomes pod access whenever two services share an issuer.
- Confidence: high (stated).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-06 — Discovery does not advertise EdDSA that the verifier accepts (invariant 6 broken)
- Topics: SP-05.8, SP-10.1
- Evidence: discovery advertises ES256/RS256 only (`oidc/mod.rs:184`, `:186`); the verifier dispatches EdDSA (`mod.rs:549` per SP-10.1); SP-10.1: invariant 6 is "currently BROKEN in the shipped direction: the verifier accepts EdDSA … and discovery does not advertise it".
- Failure: The metadata a relying party uses to decide which issuers/algos to trust disagrees with what the gate enforces: conforming clients reject EdDSA tokens the pod would have accepted, and the pod accepts EdDSA tokens from issuers clients believe unsupported.
- Confidence: high (stated).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-07 — `extract_webid` ignores cnf.webid: the WebID claim carries no key binding
- Topics: SP-05.8, SP-05.16
- Evidence: `extract_webid` reads only the top-level `webid` claim and a URL-shaped `sub`; "There is NO cnf.webid branch"; `CnfClaim` exists (`mod.rs:685`) but is not consulted (`mod.rs:864`); the LWS10 C.1 delta is unshipped (SP-05.8 D2).
- Failure: A bearer access token stolen off the wire fully impersonates the WebID on the pod — the IdP mints a `cnf jkt` claim (`tokens.rs:56`), but the consuming side never checks the token is bound to the presenter's key.
- Confidence: high (stated absence; theft scenario generic).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-08 — The acl:origin gate is compiled out of every default build
- Topics: SP-01.6, SP-04.8, SP-10.5
- Evidence: "acl-origin is NOT in the default set, so a default build ignores the Origin header entirely" (SP-01.6 O1, `crates/solid-pod-rs/Cargo.toml:168`); SP-04.8 Ignored state: "a stock build ignores the Origin header entirely and a cross-origin write is gated only by the agent's credential"; SP-10.5 lists ORG among the default-off switches.
- Failure: An ACL that says "origin-restricted" is silently unevaluated because the evaluation path is `cfg`-ed out; any holder of a non-origin-bound credential drives writes from any origin, and the operator's policy and the enforcement disagree without any signal.
- Confidence: high (stated).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-09 — A failed Epoch batch anchor is swallowed
- Topics: SP-07.7, SP-07.3, SP-10.6 (D7), SP-10.12
- Evidence: "A failed batch anchor is swallowed — it never fails the write nor the git-mark" (`lib.rs:3626`); Epoch defers to the accumulator (SP-07.3); the mempool seam is one URL with no fallback, public testnet4 default (`mempool.rs:60`); no counters exist for provenance failures (SP-10.12).
- Failure: The public explorer is down or rate-limiting for a day; every Epoch-policy write reports success, the accumulator keeps filling, no anchor is broadcast — the operator's "commits notarised on Bitcoin" audit story is silently false for that window and discoverable only by log scraping.
- Confidence: high (stated swallow + stated single endpoint).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — The git-mark anchorer is unreachable from this repository, and its error message points the wrong way
- Topics: SP-07.3, SP-07.20, SP-01.7
- Evidence: "nothing outside the tests calls `GitmarkAnchorer::genesis` (`mempool.rs:943`), and no route, CLI verb or boot step writes a git-mark trail file (`trail_store.rs:191`)" (SP-07.3 N5, OPEN); the handler still answers "no pay-token / trail not minted" while `build_anchorer` no longer requires one (`prov.rs:383` vs `:128`, N4 DRIFT); with the server's empty default the entire mark path is a no-op shim (`lib.rs:3650`, SP-01.7).
- Failure: An operator sold on Bitcoin-anchored provenance ships a build that records zero marks by default, and even with `--features git` a fresh pod cannot start a trail from this repository — "anchored" and "anchoring nothing" are indistinguishable until someone audits the chain.
- Confidence: high (stated).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open and Drift)

### F-11 — The anchoring base secret lives in pod storage under a predictable well-known path
- Topics: SP-07.20, SP-02.11, SP-04.3
- Evidence: `save_gitmark_trail` writes "/.well-known/gitmark/name.json **with the base secret**" (`trail_store.rs:133`) beside a deliberately secret-free `blocktrails.json` (`:140`); the LDP catch-all GET serves stored resources (`lib.rs:4771`); access is governed only by WAC resolution, deny-by-default on missing ACLs (SP-04.3); no `/.well-known/gitmark/` route or guard appears anywhere in the route tables (SP-02.9–SP-02.11).
- Failure: Any inherited or permissive ACL at or above the pod root makes the trail's private key readable to whoever holds Read there; the holder derives every mark key — the trail **is** the chained derivation (SP-07.20) — and can spend the head UTXO, forging the trail's continuation. The path is guessable, so scanning is trivial once any read path exists.
- Confidence: medium — the storage location is stated; the exposure depends on ACL configuration (inferred).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — The host's deposit proxy dead-ends in 501, possibly already
- Topics: SP-09.7, SP-07.17
- Evidence: "agentbox's payments route still proxies `{txo_uri, amount_sats}` to this server's `/pay/.deposit` and will surface the new 501 once its build exercises an alpha.11-or-newer binary" (ADR-2008…:151-152, SP-09.7 Debt); non-MRC20 bodies get 501 unconditionally (`handlers/pay.rs:467`); the same topic pins agentbox's Nix build at `v0.5.0-alpha.12` (`rev d64131b`) — a version that already carries the 501.
- Failure: The ADR words the breakage as future, but the recorded pin means the client's deposit flow may already be returning 501; value the client intended to move is not credited and the operator learns of it from the client.
- Confidence: high for the route mismatch; the "already live" reading rests on the pin being current at incident time.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-13 — The git credential is prefix-bound, method-wildcarded, body-unbound, and shown with no replay check
- Topics: SP-05.3, SP-05.1, SP-06.12
- Evidence: GitLenient = "* method plus URL-prefix" (`nip98.rs:93`), so "one token signed over the repo base URL … covers every smart-protocol sub-request" (`solid-pod-rs-git/src/auth.rs:154`); "the git bridge also passes body = None, so a push body is NOT hash-bound" (`auth.rs:148`); the single-use replay check is shown only in the REST extractor (`lib.rs:547`, SP-05.1) — `BasicNostrExtractor::authorise` (`auth.rs:115`) is never shown consulting `NIP98_REPLAY` in SP-05 or SP-06.12.
- Failure: A captured git Authorization header replayed inside the 60 s window (`nip98.rs:28`) re-executes a push against the same repo and every sub-path under the signed base; nothing in the described path records the event id as used.
- Confidence: low — the missing replay check is inferred from absence in the material; the code may check it.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — The value-carrying /pay surface cannot be compiled out
- Topics: SP-07.14, SP-10.4 (W4), SP-10.8
- Evidence: "the whole /pay/* surface is registered with the SAME gating as /pay/.info — always on, with no payments feature flag" (`lib.rs:4625`, SP-07.14 N2); the README's status section says not to carry value through these routes until the audit findings are fixed; the critical payment finding (non-atomic state) remains open in the window register (SP-10.4 W4).
- Failure: An operator who wants only the pod's storage features cannot ship a binary with the payment routes removed — every deployment, including ones that never configured payments, exposes balance, swap, buy and withdraw handlers sitting on the state layer from R-1.
- Confidence: high (stated).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-15 — The corpus contradicts itself on glob GET child-sidecar enforcement
- Topics: SP-03.9, SP-10.4 (W7)
- Evidence: SP-03.9 (verified `d64131b`): "the merge gates on the FOLDER only. A child carrying its own, stricter acl sidecar is still included in the merged body" (DIVERGENCE note). SP-10.4 W7 (verified `193f496`, later): "glob GET gates on the folder acl:Read, then enforce_read_inner per child, a refused child is skipped not merged — CLOSED in alpha.13" (`lib.rs:2797-2803`, `:2818-2824`, `CHANGELOG.md:22`).
- Failure: One of the two statements misleads. If W7's closure is narrower than claimed (e.g., only the trailing `/*` shape admitted at `lib.rs:2625`), a glob under a public folder still merges private children — an ACL bypass. If SP-03.9 is simply stale, any auditor or downstream consumer working from the older topic misjudges the pod's exposure. The check must be against the deployed version, not either diagram.
- Confidence: high that the contradiction exists; medium that the current code is safe.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence in SP-03.9)

## visionclaw#2 / critical

### F-01 — Agent-event ingest records "Attributed" provenance from an asserted pubkey with no signature verification
- Topics: VC-27.13
- Evidence: "SECURITY: ProvenanceStatus::Attributed means a well-formed pubkey was asserted, NOT that a signature was verified - the wire carries no sig field (provenance.rs:29-38)". The WebSocket itself authenticates the connection (Bearer/session), but nothing binds the event's claimed agent pubkey to the authenticated session identity.
- Failure: Any authenticated agentbox process or bridge connection can push events claiming any agent's pubkey. KPI trust-variance, HITL precision, provenance traces, and the ACSP governance record all attribute actions to an identity that was never cryptographically proven. A compromised or misbehaving internal service can silently falsify the audit trail.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (SECURITY)

### F-02 — deleteAgentMemory leaves RuVector embeddings persistent and searchable
- Topics: VC-22.6, VC-26.6
- Evidence: "DIVERGENCE: no reverse tombstone to RuVector - embedding row persists and stays searchable (docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md:87)". The Pod-side delete never calls any RuVector deletion endpoint; "RuVector is external Postgres reached only via MCP, no delete-propagation hook exists in this path".
- Failure: A user requests erasure of their agent memory. The Pod resource is deleted, but the vector embeddings remain in Postgres and continue to surface in similarity searches. This is a regulatory/privacy violation (GDPR-style erasure failure) with no detection signal.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-03 — Shipped compose inverts the fail-closed RBAC posture to open-by-default
- Topics: VC-22.9, VC-33.6
- Evidence: Code is fail-closed: "public_reads_enabled() ends .unwrap_or(false) (rbac_gate.rs:126-133) and main.rs:807-813 refuses to start owner-less unless RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS is set". The shipped compose sets RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=1 (docker-compose.unified.yml:111) and RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS=1 (:112), so "an unassigned pubkey resolves to Editor (role_store.rs:359)".
- Failure: A deployment using the shipped compose exposes all reads publicly and grants Editor (write) to any authenticated but unassigned pubkey. Combined with F-01 (no per-event signature verification), an anonymous or low-privilege actor can read the full knowledge graph and write to it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (CORRECTED ADR-2070, DIVERGENCE)

### F-04 — Full-sync CLEAR GRAPH silently destroys all runtime governed-write ontology additions
- Topics: VC-22.10, VC-20.4
- Evidence: "this CLEAR wipes the ENTIRE assert graph including runtime OWL classes/axioms added via the governed write door (github_sync_service.rs:930-932)". And: "a class added via add_owl_class/add_axiom between full-syncs is NOT itself in GRAPH_PROVENANCE-protected history for the assert graph - only re-derivation from the corpus...restores it after the next rebuild".
- Failure: An operator triggers FORCE_FULL_SYNC=1 (or sets force_full_sync). Every ontology class and axiom that was added through the governed API since the last sync—decisions that went through the full ACSP approval pipeline with signed provenance—is silently deleted. There is no warning, no backup of the deleted state, and no reconciliation.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (stated as a design consequence, not flagged as a defect)

### F-05 — ElevationActor loses in-flight governance cases on restart (no boot reconciliation)
- Topics: VC-24.4, VC-24.5
- Evidence: "STILL OPEN on this path: the ElevationActor poll shown here persists set_status but has no boot reconciliation, so a case lost between publish and persist is not reloaded on restart. ADR-2101 adds that to DecisionElevationActor only - see VC-24.6". The actor's in-memory `pending` HashMap (elevation_actor.rs:140) holds cases between the 31402 publish and the 31403 decision; ADR-2110 added a Reconcile at boot for DecisionElevationActor but not for this one.
- Failure: The process restarts (deploy, crash, OOM) while a human is considering an elevation case. The signed kind-31403 decision arrives after restart, hits an empty pending map, and the human's governance decision is dropped. The case is stuck in limbo with no terminal status.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (STILL OPEN)

### F-06 — Wire node-id overflow silently aliases two different nodes
- Topics: VC-23.7, VC-32.6
- Evidence: "remap_wire_id masks the id into 26 bits and an error! log names the class and both ids (:194-204, ADR-2024). The masked id still aliases another node, so an over-range id is loud, not harmless." Also: "an id that ALREADY carries a recognised class pattern is forwarded unchanged by the encoder's last branch" — bypassing the bound check entirely.
- Failure: When the node counter exceeds 67,108,863 (26 bits), a new node's wire ID is masked to the same value as an existing node. Position updates, analytics (cluster/anomaly/community/centrality), and drag operations are applied to the wrong node. The error log fires but the corruption is already on the wire and in the client's render state.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (the consequence is stated but not flagged as a defect; it is presented as an accepted tradeoff)

### F-07 — `public: false` plus a valid `owl-class` marker still ingests the page into the knowledge graph
- Topics: VC-21.2, VC-21.12
- Evidence: "DIVERGENCE that same closeout also records that an explicit public false plus a class marker remains included by policy - is_kg_included is an OR, so a valid owl-class overrides public false (vault/mod.rs:162-163)".
- Failure: An author explicitly marks a page `public: false` intending it to be private, but the page also carries an `owl-class` frontmatter key (perhaps as a draft or annotation). The page is ingested as a knowledge-graph node, its content is queryable via SPARQL, and its wikilinks create edges to other nodes. The author's explicit privacy setting is silently overridden.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-08 — Three concurrent ACSP relay connections under different panel identities share one bridge secret; both actors consume all kind-31403 events
- Topics: VC-24.11, VC-24.9
- Evidence: "three separate relay connections under three different panel identities can exist concurrently, all using the same bridge secret". The kind map shows kind-31403 consumers include both "ElevationActor.Decision handler (elevation_actor.rs:1196)" and "DecisionElevationActor Decision handler (decision_elevation_actor.rs:721)", both subscribing via `run_decision_subscription` filtering on kind 31403 since Timestamp::now.
- Failure: A kind-31403 decision event published for an enrichment-proposal case is received by both actors' relay subscriptions. If handler filtering by case-ID prefix is incomplete or racy, the wrong actor processes the decision, potentially opening a duplicate PR or recording a terminal status on the wrong case. The shared bridge secret means relay-side identity checks cannot distinguish the three producers.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — the material shows both subscribe to 31403 but does not show the handler-level case-ID filtering)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — No retry mechanism for Oxigraph writeback after SQLite decision commit
- Topics: VC-22.8, VC-24.3
- Evidence: "no cross-store 2PC - the durable decision commits in SQLite first (writeback_triggered=true) - the Oxigraph write is best-effort, writeback_committed flips only on Ok (enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:556, :574)". No reconciliation job reads `writeback_committed=false` rows to retry.
- Failure: An enrichment decision is approved, SQLite commits, but the Oxigraph INSERT fails (transient network issue, lock contention). The decision exists in SQLite as authoritative but the knowledge graph never receives the derived summary. `writeback_committed` stays false forever with no process to detect and retry. The insight loop shows the case as decided but the KG has no evidence of it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (the "best-effort" nature is stated but no one marks the absence of retry as a defect)

### F-10 — Oxigraph has no point-in-time backup; GRAPH_PROVENANCE is restore-only because it cannot be re-derived
- Topics: VC-22.11, VC-22.7
- Evidence: "Oxigraph still has NO point-in-time backup - that bullet stands" (VC-22.11). "GRAPH_PROVENANCE is restore-only because it cannot be re-derived" (VC-22.7, PROPOSED ADR-2103).
- Failure: The Oxigraph data directory is corrupted or accidentally deleted. GRAPH_KNOWLEDGE and the assert graph can be rebuilt from the corpus via a full sync, but GRAPH_PROVENANCE—the append-only audit trail of every governed mutation—is permanently lost. There is no secondary copy and no way to reconstruct signed provenance events.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (PROPOSED ADR-2103 — proposed but not implemented)

### F-11 — Feature flags are process-global, shared across all users, and updated by wholesale replace
- Topics: VC-18.2
- Evidence: "OPEN ADR-2059 - FEATURE_FLAGS remains process-global, unpersisted and shared across all users, and update does a wholesale replace." A partial POST "silently resets omitted flags to the body's values."
- Failure: Two operators concurrently update flags. Operator A disables `ontology_validation`; operator B updates `sssp_integration`. B's wholesale replace silently re-enables ontology_validation because it wasn't in B's request body. Additionally, a process restart resets all flags to defaults with no persistence, silently re-enabling validation that was deliberately disabled.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN ADR-2059)

### F-12 — Whelk reasoning has no timeout or axiom-count cap; can block the sync cycle indefinitely
- Topics: VC-20.3
- Evidence: "DOC-DRIFT - module doc claims complete EL reasoning (whelk_inference_engine.rs:5) but infer() lines 412-467 has no tokio timeout or axiom-count cap". The reasoning runs in the post-sync path and is called unconditionally.
- Failure: A corpus update introduces a large or pathological ontology (many classes, deep hierarchies). The Whelk reasoner runs for an unbounded time, blocking `run_post_sync_reasoning` and preventing all subsequent sync cycles from completing. The system appears hung with no error and no way to intervene short of a restart.
- Confidence: medium
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT — but the marker addresses the doc claim, not the missing timeout itself)

### F-13 — isPowerUser is always false from normal login; power users see all gated settings locked in production
- Topics: VC-33.6, VC-33.1
- Evidence: "isPowerUser is a single boolean, always false from login()/loginWithPasskey() and true only from devLogin()/dev-mode auto-login. NEVER the four-tier Owner/Admin/Editor/Viewer role". "The client never fetches its own resolved role." "isPowerUser is a client-side placeholder. server determines it per-request from POWER_USER_PUBKEYS."
- Failure: A legitimate power user (listed in POWER_USER_PUBKEYS on the server) logs in via NIP-07 or passkey in production. The client sets isPowerUser=false. Every SettingRow with `isPowerUserOnly` renders disabled with a Lock icon. The user cannot access settings the server would allow them to change, and there is no UI path to discover or correct this.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-14 — Ontology boot pull is fail-open with no staleness signal to consumers
- Topics: VC-26.13, VC-26.14
- Evidence: "INVARIANT fail-open (module doc ontology_pull.rs:12-17): any network or verification failure is logged and the pod keeps whatever it already held". No staleness indicator is served to clients; they cannot distinguish current from stale ontology.
- Failure: The GitHub release endpoint is unreachable or a hash mismatch occurs for an extended period. The pod continues serving the old ontology generation indefinitely. Clients build class hierarchies, run SPARQL queries, and make governance decisions against stale data with no indication that the ontology is out of date.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (presented as an invariant/design choice, not a defect)

### F-15 — Client seeds 3 synthetic nodes after a 5000ms timeout if no real graph data has arrived
- Topics: VC-30.10
- Evidence: "5000ms fallback timer seeds 3 synthetic nodes if no real data arrived by then (lastProcessedGraphRef still null)" (useGraphDataSubscription.ts:141-155).
- Failure: The backend is slow to respond (cold start, Oxigraph query slowness, network latency slightly over 5 seconds). The client renders 3 fake nodes. A user sees a graph with three nodes that don't exist, may click on them (getting errors or confusing behaviour), and may make decisions based on data that isn't real. The synthetic nodes are eventually replaced but there is no visual indication they were placeholders.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

---

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Actual handler-level filtering in ElevationActor vs DecisionElevationActor for kind-31403**: the material shows both subscribe to the same relay and consume the same event kind, but does not show whether case-ID prefix matching prevents cross-actor processing. I would need to read the `Decision` handler in both `elevation_actor.rs` and `decision_elevation_actor.rs`.

2. **Whether the Oxigraph writeback failure path has any out-of-band reconciliation**: I would need to search for any background job, cron, or operator command that reads `writeback_committed=false` rows and retries the Oxigraph write.

3. **The actual node-count trajectory relative to the 26-bit wire ID limit**: I would need production data or capacity projections to know whether the 67M node limit is a realistic concern or theoretical.

4. **Whether `public: false` + `owl-class` co-occurrence happens in the real corpus**: I would need to scan the actual vault content to determine how many pages carry both keys and whether authors are aware of the OR semantics.

5. **The ACSP relay's filtering behaviour for kind-31403 delivery**: the material says the relay enforces sender registration for kinds 31400-31402 but says nothing about whether 31403 delivery is filtered by panel ID or broadcast to all subscribers. I would need the relay-side configuration or source.

## visionclaw#2 / premortem

Post-incident review. The estate is documented as diagrams-as-code; every claim below is anchored to the path:line citations the material itself gives, so each can be verified against the code before anyone acts. The three R-items are the failure chains most likely to have produced a data, custody or security loss; the F-items are latent defects found while tracing them. Where a step is my inference rather than something the material states, the confidence line says so.

### R-1 — Erasure is structurally incomplete: Pod deletes leave RuVector embeddings live, and provenance can never be erased at all
- Topics: VC-22.6, VC-22.4, VC-22.1, VC-26.6, VC-20.4
- Chain of events: A data subject invokes `deleteAgentMemory` → the Pod-side LDP DELETE succeeds (404 counts as success, VC-26.5/VC-26.6) and the client is told `deleted=true` → the path "never calls mcp__claude-flow__memory_delete or any RuVector endpoint — no delete-propagation hook exists" → the embedding row persists in ruvector-postgres and "stays searchable" → the server's own direct read-only RuVector client is a second consumer that can surface the deleted memory → separately, any erasure demand touching `GRAPH_PROVENANCE` is unsatisfiable: the graph is append-only, retraction only adds `dl:validTo`, and the ADR-2102 choice (crypto-shred vs declare out of scope) is still only PROPOSED → the operator certifies deletion; the subject's data resurfaces in search or audit; the client leaves.
- Evidence: VC-22.6 — "DIVERGENCE: no reverse tombstone to RuVector - embedding row persists and stays searchable (docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md:87)"; "DRIFT: the server now also holds a direct read-only RuVector client … the embedding row this path leaves behind has a second consumer Cargo.toml:133-138". VC-22.4 — "append-only - no DELETE, DROP or CLEAR issued against this graph (ADR-2016, provenance_emitter.rs:32-33)"; "PROPOSED ADR-2102 … ADR-2102 exists to force that choice rather than leave it to omission". VC-22.1 — vector/agent-memory write-master is RuVector external Postgres, a distinct store from the Pod. VC-26.6 — deleteAgentMemory returns true on 204-or-404.
- Warning signs visible today: The divergence is written into the erasure authority document itself; the direct-read drift note explicitly widens the exposure; VC-20.4 shows a further vector copy (class index into RuVector namespace `ontology-classes`) with no described erasure path.
- Confidence: high — the gap is documented, and the incident is its direct consequence.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — A forced full sync destroys governed ontology writes, and Oxigraph has no backup to recover them
- Topics: VC-22.10, VC-22.11, VC-22.7, VC-21.1, VC-20.7
- Chain of events: A full sync runs (operator action, `FORCE_FULL_SYNC=1` env, or `?force_full=true` — VC-21.1) → `clear_graph()` wipes GRAPH_KNOWLEDGE (github_sync_service.rs:384-389) → `rebuild_assert_graph` filters to ontology-typed nodes and does CLEAR GRAPH assert + INSERT (github_sync_service.rs:603-604, :963, :1004) → "this CLEAR wipes the ENTIRE assert graph including runtime OWL classes/axioms added via the governed write door (github_sync_service.rs:930-932)" → classes added through POST/PUT `/api/ontology/classes|properties|axioms` (VC-20.7, power-user gated, :1645-1667) that never round-tripped through a merged vault PR are gone → `rematerialise_decisions` re-derives decision quads but its inserts are "non-fatal on failure" (:1082, :1020-1026) → recovery is impossible: "Oxigraph still has NO point-in-time backup" (VC-22.11) and "GRAPH_PROVENANCE is restore-only because it cannot be re-derived" (VC-22.7) → permanent, unrecoverable loss of client-contributed ontology and of the decision read-half.
- Evidence: VC-22.10 — "a class added via add_owl_class/add_axiom between full-syncs is NOT itself in GRAPH_PROVENANCE-protected history for the assert graph - only re-derivation from the corpus … restores it after the next rebuild". VC-22.11 — backup manifest covers only settings/enrichment/kpi (+optional liveness); backup-sqlite.sh:75, :242-245. VC-22.7 — ADR-2103 snapshot is PROPOSED, not implemented.
- Warning signs visible today: The wipe of runtime governed writes is stated on the diagram itself; the backup gap is a standing bullet the authors re-affirm; the trigger is a single env var or query parameter, and who may call `POST /api/admin/sync` is nowhere stated in this material (checkable in the route registration).
- Confidence: high — behaviour and absence of backup are both documented.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — One plaintext .env concentrates every trust boundary: the shared ACSP panel/bridge secret, the agent key, and the GitHub PAT
- Topics: VC-22.1, VC-24.11, VC-24.8, VC-24.3, VC-28.5, VC-28.6, VC-24.9
- Chain of events: Credentials live in a plaintext `.env` ("Credentials → DotEnv", VC-22.1); the SOPS rollout was never executed — a 43MB gitignored `scripts/sops` binary exists with "no .sops.yaml, secrets.enc.yaml or sops-env.sh" (DATA-authority-erasure.md:112) → any read of that file yields: (a) `VISIONCLAW_AGENT_KEY`, which authenticates `POST /api/enrichment-proposals/{id}/decide` (VC-24.3, :442) — the holder can create a stub case if unknown (:548), approve with a self-chosen attribution hex pubkey, and land triples in GRAPH_ONTOLOGY_SUMMARY via `append_derived_summary` (:587), with the rationale gate OPEN for first-contact cases (:332, :393); (b) the panel/bridge Nostr secret, which is shared by NostrBeadPublisher and NostrBridge (VC-24.8) and is "the same bridge secret" behind three concurrent AcspClient relay connections (VC-24.11) — a holder can sign kind-31402 requests and, as the panel identity, drive ElevationActor approvals that create real PRs; (c) `PRIVATE_REPO_GITHUB_PAT`, which can push branches with force on collision (github_pr_service.rs:489-497) → forged governed decisions and KG poisoning attributable to real-looking principals, with "NIP-26 delegation not wired, service-signing only" (VC-24.8) making forged provenance indistinguishable from genuine.
- Evidence: As cited inline above; plus VC-28.5 — the same `VISIONCLAW_AGENT_KEY` also gates `/image-gen/agent-submit`, and there is deliberately no default value (:82-88), i.e. one shared secret across two intake surfaces. VC-24.9 — relay-side enforcement accepts 31400-31402 from registered pubkeys; 31403/31404/31405 are consumer/admin-only.
- Warning signs visible today: ADR-2104 is PROPOSED with the rollout unexecuted; the three-connections-one-secret note; the shared bridge keypair trust-boundary note; the decide route's ungated first-contact rationale hole.
- Confidence: medium — the concentration and sharing are documented facts; the exfiltration and forging steps are inferred beyond the material.
- Severity: high

### F-01 — Shipped compose inverts the code's fail-closed RBAC defaults: public reads on, owner-less allowed, every authenticated key an Editor
- Topics: VC-22.9, VC-33.6, VC-33.1
- Evidence: Code fails closed — `public_reads_enabled() … .unwrap_or(false)` (rbac_gate.rs:126-133) and main.rs:807-813 refuses owner-less start — while docker-compose.unified.yml:111 sets `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=1` and :112 allows owner-less (VC-33.6 re-anchors the same block at :106/:107 after two moves); an unassigned authenticated pubkey resolves to Editor (role_store.rs:359; `UserRole::default_authenticated()` rbac.rs:68-70). Login itself is "client-asserted, no server verify round-trip" (VC-33.1).
- Failure: Deployed with the shipped compose, any Nostr key that can sign NIP-98 authenticates and is an Editor; every mutation gated only by "authenticated" (e.g. all `/analytics` POSTs under `mutations_only()`, VC-18.1) becomes reachable by an arbitrary stranger.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE / "CORRECTED ADR-2070" note; VC-33.6 DIVERGENCE; ADR-2027 demo default acknowledged)

### F-02 — Analytics feature flags are process-global, unpersisted, and replaced wholesale by any partial POST
- Topics: VC-18.2
- Evidence: "OPEN ADR-2059 - FEATURE_FLAGS remains process-global, unpersisted and shared across all users, and update does a wholesale replace"; "WHOLESALE REPLACE - no field merge, a partial POST silently resets omitted flags"; `ontology_validation` "is the ONLY flag read as a real GATE" (ontology/mod.rs:335).
