- Failure: One authenticated client posts `{"sssp_integration":true}`; `ontology_validation` silently reverts to the body's value, disabling the validation gate for every user; a restart silently resets all flags mid-case.

### F-03 — NIP-98 replay cache is an in-process Mutex<HashMap>: restarts (and any second instance) reopen the single-use window
- Topics: VC-26.4, VC-32.1
- Evidence: REPLAY_CACHE at nip98.rs:215 (`Mutex<HashMap>`); claim window 2× TOKEN_MAX_AGE_SECONDS with the age window 60s (nip98.rs:169, :178, :362); the same single-use verification guards the Solid proxy and the `/wss` signed-subprotocol upgrade (http_handler.rs:160-173).
- Failure: A captured `Authorization: Nostr` token replayed within 60s after a server restart — or against a replica with its own cache — passes the single-use check and authenticates as the victim's pubkey.
- Confidence: medium (in-process scope documented; multi-instance deployment inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — Agent-event attribution is asserted, never signature-verified; the intent ledger and HITL/Trust KPIs are forgeable
- Topics: VC-27.13, VC-25.3, VC-22.5
- Evidence: "SECURITY: ProvenanceStatus::Attributed means a well-formed pubkey was asserted, NOT that a signature was verified - the wire carries no sig field (provenance.rs:29-38)"; events publish "always … regardless of provenance status"; the declared intent is persisted verbatim on kpi_agent_events "rather than re-derived at read time, so a later reading cannot soften it" (0006_kpi_agent_event_intent.sql:1; sqlite_kpi_repository.rs:245); trust_variance/hitl_precision consume these rows (kpi_compute.rs:140-143).
- Failure: A rogue or compromised agentbox pushes agent_action events attributing actions to a human pubkey with a fabricated declared intent; the governance dashboards report fabricated human judgement durably — the `system:whelk-gate` exclusion does not cover a forged human DID.

- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (explicit SECURITY note)

Contrast: the sibling decide route is X-Agent-Key gated (VC-24.3).

### F-07 — Decision fan-out has no cross-store 2PC and no drawn retry: SQLite commits, the Oxigraph write is best-effort

- Evidence: "no cross-store 2PC - the durable decision commits in SQLite first (writeback_triggered=true) - the Oxigraph write is best-effort, writeback_committed flips only on Ok" (enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:556, :574); neither VC-22.3 nor VC-22.8 draws any retry or reconciliation of a failed writeback.
- Failure: A transient Oxigraph failure leaves an approved, attributed decision with `writeback_triggered=true, writeback_committed=false` indefinitely; the summary graph silently lacks facts the SQLite ledger says were written, and nothing revisits them.

ADR-2101 adds that to DecisionElevationActor only" (set_status at elevation_actor.rs:1429); contrast DecisionElevationActor `plan_reconciliation` (decision_elevation_actor.rs:819).

### F-10 — Over-range wire node ids are masked into 26 bits and alias another node
- Topics: VC-23.7, VC-32.6, VC-32.10
- Evidence: "remap_wire_id masks to 26 bits … An over-range id is still masked and therefore aliases, but never silently" (binary_protocol.rs:199-204; NODE_ID_MASK 0x03FFFFFF, :26); pinning is server-authoritative keyed on the wire id (nodeDragStart, VC-32.10).
- Failure: Once the NEXT_NODE_ID counter passes 67,108,863, node 67108864 renders, pins, and receives agent beams/heat as node 0; an operator drags the node they see and the server pins a different one.

### F-11 — OntologyPipelineService's inferred-edge path hangs off an entry point the server never calls, while its doc comment claims sync calls it
- Topics: VC-20.3, VC-20.4
- Evidence: "Debt: … trigger_reasoning (ontology_pipeline_service.rs:244), reached only through on_ontology_modified (:157), and a search … finds no caller of that method outside tests/integration/pipeline_test.rs:94; PATH B … hangs off an entry point the running server never invokes"; "Drift (doc comment vs callers): the doc comment on on_ontology_modified says it is called automatically by GitHubSyncService (:150), while the sync cycle … calls the Whelk engine and select_inferred_edges_for_sync directly (:1272)"; PATH B also runs the surviving `CustomReasoner`, not Whelk.
- Failure: An operator enables `materialise_inferred_edges` expecting PATH B; nothing ever runs, and any belief about which reasoner produced the inferred graph is wrong.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt + Drift)

### F-12 — TriggerReasoning forwards to a ReasoningActor that no longer exists — the governance trigger is a silent no-op
- Topics: VC-20.8

- Failure: A client triggers reasoning through OntologyActor; the handler returns without error while no reasoning occurs — inferred materialisation silently never happens from this path.
- Confidence: medium (comment-vs-code mismatch documented; whether the send fails silently is inferred)

### F-13 — rematerialise_decisions' graph writes are non-fatal: decision records can vanish from the assert graph after a full sync without any error

- Confidence: medium
- Severity: medium

### F-14 — Push-to-talk binds to any canonical agent DID from graph selection; no authorization of the binder appears anywhere in the path
- Topics: VC-35.2, VC-34.21, VC-35.7
- Evidence: `resolveSelectedAgentDid` accepts the node id or metadata DID (pttAgentBinding.ts:36-53); `setSelectedAgentDid` checks only canonical form ("a non-canonical DID binds to null, never to a raw string", PushToTalkService.ts:111); `set_ptt_with_target`/`bind_selected_agent` thread the DID (audio_router.rs:202, :227); `shouldDispatchGoverned` gates on state plus canonical DID only (VC-34.21); the governed dispatch then signs a 31402 to /v1/voice-intent (VC-35.7). No step checks that the speaker may command that agent.

### F-15 — Shared global mutation surface: any authenticated key can rewrite constraints, kernel mode and params for every user
- Topics: VC-18.1, VC-18.3
- Evidence: The `/analytics` scope wrap is `RequireAuth::authenticated().mutations_only()` — "every POST needs an authenticated pubkey" (analytics/mod.rs:170 and note N1); POST `/params`, `/constraints`, `/focus`, `/kernel-mode` dispatch UpdateVisualAnalyticsParams/SetComputeMode to the single GPU compute actor (params_handlers.rs:63, :383; force_compute_actor.rs:3650, :3231); no per-user namespacing of this state is described anywhere.
- Failure: With the shipped compose (F-01) every key authenticates, so one client's constraint update or kernel-mode switch mutates the shared simulation for all connected users — a griefing and correctness vector for shared sessions, including server-authoritative pinning downstream.
- Confidence: medium (single shared actor and global state are documented; per-user isolation is nowhere described)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

## visionclaw#3 / critical

### F-01 — Dev-mode full auth bypass is armed by default and enforces no peer check
- Topics: VC-36.15, VC-36.3
- Evidence: `docker-compose.unified.yml:95` sets `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE: "${VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE:-1}"` — ADR-2108 flipped the default from `:-0` to `:-1`, so `launch.sh up dev` boots an ARMED backend unless the operator opts out. When armed, every request is granted as `DEV_MODE_PUBKEY = "dev-mode-local-admin"` (`src/utils/auth.rs:116`, `:96`, `project/src/main.rs:299`) and the REST chokepoint is described as "LAN-local FULL bypass, peer-agnostic: no NIP-98, no token, no peer check" (`src/utils/auth.rs:203-218`). Further bypass sites exist at `src/settings/auth_extractor.rs:25`, `src/handlers/memory_cloud_handler.rs:66`, `src/handlers/speech_socket_handler.rs:165`, `src/handlers/socket_flow_handler/filter_auth.rs:28`. The backend is directly reachable on the network: nginx :3001 deliberately does not proxy `/ws/presence` and clients connect to the backend port directly (VC-36.10 note). The authors' own caveat: both services load `env_file .env`, "so the var must never sit in a .env shared with prod — sharper now that the compose default supplies it unasked" (`docker-compose.unified.yml:92-94`). The removed `?token=` fallback also "survives only dev-auth-gated" (VC-36.3), i.e. it lives in this same default-armed service.
- Failure: any peer who can reach the dev backend port — which headsets must reach directly — performs full-admin writes (layout, physics settings, drag/pin) with no credential. "LAN-local" is a deployment assumption, not an enforcement; nothing in the material shows firewalling of that port, and the bypass is on by default.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (presented as an ADR-2108 decision; only the .env caveat is noted)

### F-02 — Signed credentials cross the wire in cleartext ws:// and http://
- Topics: VC-36.3, VC-36.10, VC-36.11, VC-36.16
- Evidence: `XR_BACKEND_WS` defaults to `ws://localhost:4000` (`graph_scene.gd:72` area) and the documented deploy run uses `XR_BACKEND_WS=ws://192.168.2.132` (VC-36.16). `_http_base` (`xr-client/scripts/graph_scene.gd:1311`) maps a `ws://` base to plain `http://` for REST. Over these transports the client sends the NIP-98 `authenticate` text frame (`xr-client/rust/src/transport.rs:108-115`) and `Authorization: Nostr <b64 kind-27235>` headers (`xr-client/rust/src/signer.rs:128`, `graph_scene.gd:1206-1207`). The presence socket bypasses nginx entirely (VC-36.10), so no TLS termination point exists for it anywhere in the material; the nostr secret itself is passed in the Godot process environment on the headset host.
- Failure: a listener on the LAN path captures the kind-27235 event (replayable within its validity window) or the dev bearer and writes as the headset user; no wss/https production URL appears anywhere in the material.
- Confidence: medium (schemes, endpoints and headers are stated; the replay window and network position are inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — NIP-98 acceptance is never read back; a rejected signature is indistinguishable from success
- Topics: VC-36.3, VC-36.4
- Evidence: "the client sends the NIP-98 `authenticate` message and never reads an acknowledgement, so a rejected signature leaves the session anonymous with no signal" (`xr-client/rust/src/transport.rs:113`, VC-36.3 Open); the state machine "has no Authenticated state" (VC-36.4 note).
- Failure: a headset with a rotated or mistyped secret connects, streams the graph normally, then every drag/pin is refused server-side; on a dev-bypass backend the same misconfiguration is completely invisible, so it surfaces only at the prod cut-over.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-04 — Relay and client route validators disagree; the relay can emit frames the client discards
- Topics: VC-38.7, VC-38.9
- Evidence: VC-38.7 states the relay "rejects a negative seq (`src/handlers/socket_flow_handler/session_relay.rs:195`) and a non-array sidecar (`session_relay.rs:188`) while accepting `null`; the client defaults a bad seq to 0 (`xr-client/rust/src/memory_route.rs:554`) and rejects any non-array sidecar, `null` included, as a JSON error (`memory_route.rs:325`)". A parse failure "returns error and sets last_error" and the frame never reaches the gate (`memory_route.rs:516`, VC-38.9).
- Failure: a desktop route with `sidecar: null` passes relay validation, is relayed at up to 4 Hz, and is dropped as a Json error on every headset — the desktop user sees a route, the headset never does, with only `last_error` as a trace. A malformed seq silently becomes 0, which can collide with the previous frame's stamp and read as Stale.
- Confidence: medium (the divergent code paths are stated; how often null or odd seq occurs in practice is not)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (the divergence is described matter-of-factly, not marked as a defect)

### F-05 — Route ordering rides on unrebased wall clocks; skew permanently hides routes
- Topics: VC-38.8, VC-38.12
- Evidence: `sent_at` is Unix milliseconds on both paths — the relay's `sentAt` "passed through unrebased" and the headset's own `Time.get_unix_time_from_system()` (`xr-client/scripts/memory_cloud_layer.gd:491`, `xr-client/rust/src/memory_route.rs:1596`). VC-38.8 Open: "a headset clock ahead of the desktop's makes desktop frames `Stale` until the desktop catches up and a clock behind does the reverse; nothing states the unit or bounds the skew." A Stale verdict is silent — `last` does not move and no signal fires.
- Failure: a headset a minute fast runs its own query (route stamped with its inflated now); every later desktop relay for that user is judged Stale and dropped, so the room and the desktop permanently disagree about the current route until someone notices and fixes clocks.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-06 — Client/server constants are hand-copied, and one copy has already caused an incident
- Topics: VC-36.11, VC-36.8, VC-38.12
- Evidence: "REPEL_K_MAX is 500 and must not exceed the server validator bound (0..500): a Spread press that clamped above it was rejected with HTTP 400 and the whole PUT discarded... The bound lives server-side, so the two constants move together" (`graph_scene.gd:299-303`, clamp at `:1168`). `K := 50` in `memory_search.gd:25` is "a hand copy of HEADSET_K (`memory_query.rs:51`)... and nothing enforces it" (VC-38.12 Drift). The HUD Key swatches are "duplicated from their sources by design... a palette change must be made twice" (`hud.gd:230`, `:1027`); `SWARM_STATUS_COLORS` mirrors `render_store::agent_status_color` (`hud.gd:230`).
- Failure: a server-side bound or palette change ships without its client twin and the documented failure repeats: user presses Spread, the whole physics PUT is discarded by a 400, and settings silently "don't apply".
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially (Drift on K, DEBT on swatches; the REPEL_K_MAX sync hazard is stated as an invariant with the incident history but not tracked as open risk)

### F-07 — NodePositionContext is a module singleton with no invalidation; stale readers survive remounts
- Topics: VC-37.7
- Evidence: "NodePositionContext is named a context but is a module-level mutable singleton, not a React context. GraphManager publishes the live buffer on every frame (`GraphManager.tsx:431`) and the id map on every node-set change (`GraphManager.tsx:75`), and the agent overlay reads it directly. Nothing invalidates a stale reader after a remount. (`NodePositionContext.tsx:13`)" (VC-37.7 NOTE3, DEBT).
- Failure: after a graph reload or remount, BotsVisualization (`BotsVisualization.tsx:29`) keeps an old id→index map against a re-allocated positions buffer and renders agents at the wrong nodes or out-of-range indices — and the wrong answer looks like ordinary rendering, not an error.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT)

### F-08 — Broken credentials degrade silently across three surfaces at once
- Topics: VC-36.10, VC-36.11, VC-38.2, VC-38.5
- Evidence: an empty XR_NOSTR_SECRET still yields an ephemeral signer (`graph_scene.gd:455-457`) but "an ephemeral key cannot satisfy the presence challenge or gate drag/pin" (VC-36.10, INVARIANT 6). The fallback bearer is "still constructed by the client" while "the server refuses it for any non-loopback peer, so the HP headset never passes it" (VC-36.11 DIVERGENCE, `graph_scene.gd:94`, `:470`, `:1214`). The memory cloud treats 401/403 as "hide points, clear route, state forbidden... quiet state not a toast" (`memory_cloud_layer.gd:362`, VC-38.5). With F-03's missing auth ack, no surface names the root cause.
- Failure: a prod headset with an unset or expired secret boots, streams the graph, then presence never joins, every write 401s, and the memory cloud silently vanishes — three symptoms, one cause, and the only visible clue is the write-denied strip.
- Confidence: high (each leg is stated; the compound diagnosis cost is inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially (the bearer DIVERGENCE and the quiet-403 design are marked; the compound silent-degradation mode is not)

### F-09 — project.godot declares a renderer/version combination that has never produced a working headset build
- Topics: VC-36.2
- Evidence: `config/features = PackedStringArray('4.3','Forward Mobile')` (`project.godot:16`) while "the only build that has ever rendered on a headset is 4.6.1-stable on Compatibility" — left "OPEN by design" because the array is editor-managed. ADR-2032 records that only gl_compatibility submits both eyes on SteamVR/Linux/NVIDIA (VC-36.16); the correction lives in a text header (`project.godot:3-6`), not in the field tools read.
- Failure: anyone who rebuilds or opens the project honouring the declared features gets the one-eye/black-composite client the ADR forbids; the working configuration is carried by prose and tribal knowledge.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT, open by design)

### F-10 — Headset deploy is an unsupervised manual ceremony whose runbook contains a documented race
- Topics: VC-36.16
- Evidence: the verified deploy path is manual ssh kill-then-launch; "SEPARATE kill then launch over TWO ssh calls — a single chained call races the compositor"; the launch must carry XAUTHORITY or "Godot cannot open the X11 display"; the headset run loads the debug library (`xr-client/rust/Cargo.toml:65-66`, mitigated by opt-level 2). No supervisor, restart, or watchdog appears anywhere in the material.
- Failure: any client crash, headset reboot or SteamVR restart leaves a dead or stale Godot until an operator notices and repeats the fragile sequence by hand; the documented two-call workaround is itself a race being managed by humans.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (documented as ceremony, not flagged as risk)

### F-11 — The memory cloud, route and beat layers have never been judged in a headset
- Topics: VC-36.25, VC-36.20
- Evidence: "the memory cloud, its relayed route and the beat-clock relay have been exercised by GUT and desktop screenshots, not in a headset — stereo agreement, sprite legibility and route brightness are unmeasured" (`docs/XR-client.md:856`, VC-36.25 N2 OPEN); VC-36.20's boundary: "No fresh headset, live authenticated graph or Quest frame-budget certification".
- Failure: the feature reaches wearers with unmeasured stereo disagreement and brightness — the class of defect that only manifests in-headset and is most expensive to discover post-deployment; desktop screenshots certify nothing about the medium.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN)

### F-12 — Governing documents contradict the code on a security control and on merge-gate numbers
- Topics: VC-36.3, VC-36.16, VC-36.18, VC-38.3, VC-36.19
- Evidence: "BASELINE-architecture.md's 'Known divergences' bullet (:248) recording it as still-open was not updated when ADR-2058 landed" — and the token fallback in fact survives dev-auth-gated (VC-36.3), so the governing doc is wrong in both directions. Test counts are quoted as 141, then 218 (VC-36.18), then "479 headless tests... The count is the governing document's, not re-run for this stamp" (VC-36.16). The same class: a "Stride-16 instance buffer" comment over a stride-20 constant and test (`memory_cloud.rs:1016` vs `:67`, VC-38.3 Drift) and the 45 Hz fade comment over a 90 Hz loop (`render_store.rs:109-111`, VC-36.19 Drift).
- Failure: a security auditor follows BASELINE-architecture.md and audits a token path whose status the doc misstates; merge decisions are made against test counts nobody re-ran; an engineer implementing to the stride-16 comment packs the wrong buffer and misrenders every cloud sprite.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially (individual drifts are noted in passing; the BASELINE security staleness is untagged and there is no tracked defect list)

### F-13 — The declared ship target (Quest 3 APK) does not exist and its renderer path is unexercised
- Topics: VC-36.2
- Evidence: "the .mobile override targets the Quest 3 APK, which is UNBUILT — no Android NDK is provisioned. 90fps was measured only on VIVE Pro + dual RTX 6000" (`project.godot:57`, `docs/BASELINE-architecture.md:209-210`) while "project.godot:2 header still calls the Quest 3 APK the ship target" against `graph_scene.gd:25-26` naming the Vive/SteamVR rig as currently deployed (VC-36.2 D2/D3).
- Failure: capacity, budget and ship-date decisions are made against a target that has never built; any eventual APK attempt lands on the `mobile` rendering override that no test or measurement has ever exercised.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium

### F-14 — RouteGate's reload latch silently drops every later snapshot mismatch for an id
- Topics: VC-38.8
- Evidence: at the verified revision (`a053adcef`) "`reloaded_for` is set at the first Reload and never cleared, not on Apply and not on Clear (`memory_route.rs:621`), so a second mismatch for an id once reloaded for is dropped instead of reloaded"; the fix exists only in a later commit, `6ca5ff4b2`.
- Failure: a relayed route names a snapshot the headset doesn't hold → one reload; if the reload lands on a third id or the mismatch recurs, every subsequent route for that id is silently Dropped and the headset shows a stale or absent route indefinitely.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (described as an at-revision defect with the later fix named)

### F-15 — Query Execute's failure states are shipped unverified and untested
- Topics: VC-36.18
- Evidence: "server correctness and the user-visible denied/error states are UNVERIFIED. Query Execute is implemented but runtime acceptance remains open" and "no headset/scene/shader test covers this path" (VC-36.18 DIVERGENCE); the GUT inventory in VC-36.16 lists no query-execute scene test.
- Failure: a user runs a pattern query against a slow or refusing server; the 4xx/5xx branch has never been exercised, so the failure mode — silence, stuck result planes, an unnoticed denial — is whatever the code happens to do on the day.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

## Not judgeable from this material

- **Server-side auth enforcement detail**: NIP-98 tag/`created_at` validation windows and replay protection, and whether any wss/TLS termination exists upstream — needed to weigh F-02 and F-03 before acting.
- **Network exposure of the dev backend**: firewalling of ports 4000 and the presence socket, whether nginx :3001 proxies `/api` to the armed dev service, and what else shares the 25G/LAN segment — decisive for F-01's blast radius.
- **Server-side persistence and custody**: whether layout/physics/settings writes are durable, backed up, and consistent across users — these topics cover clients only; nothing shows the server's data-at-rest or backup story.
- **Multi-user concurrency**: behaviour with two or more simultaneous headsets/users in one room and graph — roster scale, per-user relay isolation, and `broadcast_seq` behaviour under concurrent writers are not drawn.
- **Release reality**: what actually runs in production — the material shows a dev-default compose file and a debug-library headset run; I cannot tell whether a release build of server and client is deployed anywhere, which F-01, F-02 and F-10 all hinge on.

## visionclaw#3 / premortem

# Post-mortem — VC-INC-2027-10: unattributed destruction of a client estate over the LAN

**Incident summary.** A client's persisted graph estate (layout mode, physics settings, pins) was rewritten by a party that presented no credential. Every action in the server log is attributed to a single shared identity, so no per-user attribution or rollback was possible. The client withdrew. This post-mortem is written against the recorded material only (VC-36, VC-37, VC-38; XR client verified at `a053adcef72f3262f57a4b32d902475a1bdc9048`, browser client at `af3dff3f25300cf12bceda5650688ec223270eca`). Every claim below carries its citation for verification; the three root causes are ranked candidates, and the latent-defect list includes everything else suspected while investigating.

---

## Root causes, ranked

### R-1 — The VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE default was flipped to armed, and the bypass is peer-agnostic on the LAN
- Topics: VC-36.15, VC-36.11, VC-36.3
- Chain of events:
  1. The dev compose service ships `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE: "${VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE:-1}"` — since ADR-2108 (2026-09-08) the default is **armed**, and the operator must opt *out* with `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE=0` (`project/docker-compose.unified.yml:95`).
  2. Both services load `env_file .env`; the material itself warns "the var must never sit in a .env shared with prod — sharper now that the compose default supplies it unasked" (`project/docker-compose.unified.yml:92-94`). A shared `.env`, or a dev/dev-auth image promoted internally, carries the armed default without anyone setting it.
  3. The release-binary refusal only triggers if the variable is *present* to that process (`project/src/main.rs:136`, `:161`); in dev and dev-auth builds the hygiene hook is an empty stub (`project/src/main.rs:169`), so an armed backend boots with only a warning banner (`project/src/main.rs:296`).
  4. With the bypass active, every request is granted as `DEV_MODE_PUBKEY = "dev-mode-local-admin"`, a power user, on the authenticate frame (`src/utils/auth.rs:96`, `project/src/main.rs:299`, `src/handlers/socket_flow_handler/filter_auth.rs:28`, `:43`); the REST chokepoint returns `Ok(DEV_MODE_PUBKEY)` with "no NIP-98, no token, no peer check" (`src/utils/auth.rs:203-218`).
  5. Any LAN peer (the headset network is `10.10.10.0/30` and `192.168.2.x`, VC-36.16) rewrites layout, physics and pin state. All of it logs as one shared identity — attribution and custody are unrecoverable.
- Evidence: `docker-compose.unified.yml:88-91`, `:92-94`, `:95`; `project/src/main.rs:109`, `:136`, `:161`, `:169`, `:296`, `:299`; `src/utils/auth.rs:96`, `:116-121`, `:127-130`, `:203-218`; `src/handlers/socket_flow_handler/filter_auth.rs:28`, `:43`.
- Warning signs visible today: an opt-out (not opt-in) default; detection is a log banner only; the bypass is explicitly peer-agnostic on the LAN; the `.env` caveat is already written down; and the same bypass exists at further sites (`src/settings/auth_extractor.rs:25`, `src/handlers/memory_cloud_handler.rs:66`, `src/handlers/speech_socket_handler.rs:165`).
- Confidence: high — the material states every link except the exact transport of the variable into the failing deployment.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — The legacy PHYSICS_BEARER credential is accepted for loopback peers, and the client still sends it
- Topics: VC-36.11
- Chain of events:
  1. When no real Nostr secret is present, the client constructs `Authorization: PHYSICS_BEARER` plus `X-Nostr-Pubkey: <pubkey_hex>` (`xr-client/scripts/graph_scene.gd:94`, `:470`, `:1214`; `docs/XR-client.md:838`). The pubkey header is client-chosen and unsigned.
  2. The server "refuses it for any non-loopback peer" — which directly implies it **accepts** it from loopback. The deploy ceremony is built on SSH into the host (`ssh john@10.10.10.1`, VC-36.16), and any local forward, co-located sidecar, monitoring scraper, or SSRF from a same-host service reaches the API as a loopback peer.
  3. Such a peer presents the bearer with an arbitrary pubkey and obtains privileged writes attributed to an identity it picked.
- Evidence: `xr-client/scripts/graph_scene.gd:1212-1214` (fallback construction); the stated refusal rule ("refuses it for any non-loopback peer, so the HP headset never passes it"); `docs/XR-client.md:838`.
- Warning signs visible today: the path "still exists and is still constructed by the client" (labelled DIVERGENCE in VC-36.11); no stated expiry date for the loopback acceptance.
- Confidence: medium — the loopback acceptance and the exploitation path are inferred from the wording "refuses it for any non-loopback peer"; the client-side behaviour is stated.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — NIP-98 is the only credential, the authenticate acknowledgement is never read, and a bad secret fails silently
- Topics: VC-36.3, VC-36.4, VC-36.10, VC-36.11
- Chain of events:
  1. Query-token auth was deleted from the client (ADR-2076); NIP-98 is the only graph-socket credential (VC-36.3).
  2. If `XR_NOSTR_SECRET` is empty or mistyped, `NostrAuth.create()` returns an **ephemeral** signer (`xr-client/scripts/graph_scene.gd:455-457`), which "cannot satisfy the presence challenge or gate drag/pin" (`docs/XR-client.md` INVARIANT 6, VC-36.10).
  3. The client sends the `authenticate` message and never reads an acknowledgement — "a rejected signature leaves the session anonymous with no signal" (`xr-client/rust/src/transport.rs:113`); the state machine has no Authenticated state at all (VC-36.4 note).
  4. In a production deployment (dev-mode off), the client nevertheless connects and receives the full read stream; every privileged write — drag/pin (`transport.rs:108-115`), layout posts, physics PUTs — is refused. The user works for a session believing state is being saved; nothing is persisted. This is a custody failure from the client's perspective even though no bytes were destroyed server-side.
  5. Conversely, in any mixed environment where dev-mode is on for one service and off for another, the same user's actions succeed under `dev-mode-local-admin` on one path and fail invisibly on the other — a split identity in the audit trail.
- Evidence: `transport.rs:113`, `transport.rs:108-115`; `graph_scene.gd:455-457`, `:466-471`; `graph_scene.gd:1253`, `:1257`, `:1198` and `hud.gd:1436` (the write-denied strip, ADR-2108 — which covers HUD-routed writes and only after that ADR landed); VC-36.4 "The client has no Authenticated state".
- Warning signs visible today: the "Open" note at VC-36.3 stating the ack is never read; ADR-2108's own history — before it landed, every server-routed HUD write 401'd "into an invisible push_warning" — showing this class of failure was already real once.
- Confidence: medium — the mechanics are fully stated; the "believed saved but not persisted" outcome is inferred from them.
- Severity: high

---

## Further latent defects

### F-01 — Multiple independent dev-bypass sites must each individually route through the release stub
- Topics: VC-36.15
- Evidence: release builds compile `dev_full_bypass_active` to a stub returning false (`src/utils/auth.rs:127-130`), but three further bypass sites are listed — `src/settings/auth_extractor.rs:25`, `src/handlers/memory_cloud_handler.rs:66`, `src/handlers/speech_socket_handler.rs:165` — and the material does not state that all of them call the stubbed function rather than reading the variable directly.
- Failure: one site reads the env var itself; a promoted dev-auth build (or any build where the release stub does not cover that site) leaves the memory-cloud or speech surface fully bypassed while the main chokepoint appears closed.
- Confidence: medium — inferred from the absence of a stated guarantee.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-02 — Route ordering compares wall clocks across machines with no stated unit bound or skew bound
- Topics: VC-38.8, VC-38.12
- Evidence: `sent_at` is Unix milliseconds on both paths — the relay's `sentAt` passed through unrebased and the headset's own `Time.get_unix_time_from_system()` (`xr-client/scripts/memory_cloud_layer.gd:491`) — and "nothing states the unit or bounds the skew" (VC-38.8, Open).
- Failure: a headset clock ahead of the desktop's makes every relayed route `Stale` until the desktop catches up (and the reverse starves headset query routes); the user is shown no route, or the wrong route held as current, during a live session.
- Confidence: high — stated as Open.
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)
- Severity: medium

### F-03 — Relay and client disagree on route-frame parsing at the edges
- Topics: VC-38.7
- Evidence: the relay rejects a negative `seq` (`src/handlers/socket_flow_handler/session_relay.rs:195`) and a non-array `sidecar` while accepting `null` (`:188`); the client defaults a bad `seq` to 0 (`xr-client/rust/src/memory_route.rs:554`) and rejects any non-array `sidecar`, **`null` included**, as a JSON error (`memory_route.rs:325`).
- Failure: a desktop emits `sidecar: null` (or the equivalent edge shape); the relay passes it, the headset parse fails on every delivery, and the route silently never applies.
- Confidence: high — both sides stated.
- Marked by authors: no (documented asymmetry, no label)
- Severity: medium

### F-04 — `reloaded_for` is never cleared, so one snapshot mismatch permanently drops routes for that id
- Topics: VC-38.8
- Evidence: at this revision `reloaded_for` is set at the first Reload and never cleared — not on Apply, not on Clear (`xr-client/rust/src/memory_route.rs:621`) — so a second mismatch for a once-reloaded id is dropped instead of reloaded. A fix exists as VisionClaw `6ca5ff4b2` (2026-10-18, ER-26), i.e. after the verified commit `a053adcef` in this topic's header.
- Failure: any revision without the fix: a desktop that has moved to a newer snapshot than the headset holds gets exactly one reload, then permanent silence for that snapshot id.
- Confidence: medium — the defect is stated; whether the fix commit reached every deployment is not.
- Marked by authors: no (narrated with fix commit; no label)
- Severity: medium

### F-05 — Beat offset's provisional seed carries rtt 0 and poisons the minimum-rtt filter for ~16 s
- Topics: VC-38.14
- Evidence: the first relayed frame with `serverTime` seeds a provisional offset with `rtt 0` (`xr-client/rust/src/beat.rs:410`); the NTP-style filter picks the least-delayed of the last eight samples (`beat.rs:311`), so the seed wins until eight real pongs push it out — roughly sixteen seconds of `rtt_ms 0` with a one-way downlink bias. Fix in `6ca5ff4b2`.
- Failure: graph and cloud pulses are mistimed against the room for the first ~16 s after a beat relay; any consumer treating `rtt_ms` as a quality signal is misled.
- Confidence: high — stated.
- Marked by authors: no (narrated with fix commit; no label)
- Severity: low

### F-06 — `memory_flash` timestamps are parsed but never used, and no freshness check is visible on the flash-to-drift path
- Topics: VC-38.18, VC-36.26
- Evidence: "`MemoryFlash.timestamp` is parsed and carried but never read downstream" (`xr-client/rust/src/semantic.rs:333`, Debt). The agent drift credit is applied before the bursts-enabled check (`xr-client/scripts/beat_pulse.gd:113`), feeding `agent_drift_offset` (`xr-client/rust/src/binary_protocol.rs:1486`). The freshness machinery (`ts_is_newer`, `render_store.rs:480`) is documented for 0x23 actions and agent states, not for flashes.
- Failure: relayed, buffered or replayed `memory_flash` frames draw bursts and move agent rest slots as if live; the drift field's history is distorted by stale events. (Freshness-check absence is inferred, not stated.)
- Confidence: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, for the unused timestamp)
- Severity: medium

### F-07 — Demo evidence is stamped on the local tick clock before any server clock anchor exists
- Topics: VC-36.23
- Evidence: "before any action has anchored the clock server_clock_ms is -1 and the first frames fall back to the local tick clock, an unrelated time base" (`xr-client/rust/src/binary_protocol.rs:1573`, `xr-client/scripts/agent_demo_director.gd:215-216`) — stated as Open.
- Failure: a demo started on a freshly connected client stamps early evidence in tick-clock time; when a real anchor arrives, ordering and the 30 s evidence TTL (`render_store.rs:926`) judge those records against an unrelated time base — retiring or immortalising them wrongly.
- Confidence: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)
- Severity: medium

### F-08 — The demo's reserved id range 0xD001–0xD0FF is convention only, and Stop retires ids outright
- Topics: VC-36.23, VC-36.5, VC-36.9
- Evidence: "Provenance is the reserved id range 0xD001-0xD0FF alone" (`agent_demo_director.gd:25`, `:87`); `retire_agents(demo_wire_ids())` removes records and anchors outright (`binary_protocol.rs:1567`, `render_store.rs:1049`). Agent wire ids are `u32` with the `AGENT_NODE_FLAG 0x8000_0000` (VC-36.5) and no server-side exclusion of the reserved range is stated.
- Failure: the server assigns a live agent a wire id whose low bits land in the reserved range; the operator runs and stops a demo; `Stop` deletes the live agent's record and anchor — the roster and work beams lose a real agent mid-session.
- Confidence: medium — inferred; the collision possibility follows from the stated convention-only provenance.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-09 — Client clamp and server validator bound are manually coupled constants with a data-loss precedent
- Topics: VC-36.11
- Evidence: "REPEL_K_MAX is 500 and must not exceed the server validator bound (0..500): a Spread press that clamped above it was rejected with HTTP 400 and the whole PUT discarded. The bound lives server-side, so the two constants move together" (`graph_scene.gd:299-303`, clamp at `:1168`).
- Failure: the server bound is tightened independently; the client clamps to the old ceiling; the next physics PUT is 400'd and the **entire** payload — including the user's other pending settings in that PUT — is discarded, exactly as it did before.
- Confidence: high — the failure mode already occurred once per the material.
- Marked by authors: no (stated as an invariant to maintain)
- Severity: medium

### F-10 — NIP-98 signs the exact request URL; nothing structural prevents signing/sending divergence
- Topics: VC-36.11
- Evidence: "the signed URL must be the EXACT request URL including query, or the server tag check fails" (`graph_scene.gd:1206-1207`).
- Failure: any refactor that re-orders query parameters (e.g. the fold GET at `graph_scene.gd:976` or relations at `:3326`) or re-encodes between signing and sending produces mass 401s that present as an authentication outage rather than a URL bug.
- Confidence: low — inferred fragility; no current divergence is stated.
- Marked by authors: no (recorded as an invariant)
- Severity: low

### F-11 — NodePositionContext is a module singleton with no invalidation after remount
- Topics: VC-37.7
- Evidence: "Nothing invalidates a stale reader after a remount" (`client/src/features/graph/contexts/NodePositionContext.tsx:13`, Debt); `GraphManager` publishes the live buffer every frame (`GraphManager.tsx:431`) and the id map on node-set change (`:75`); `BotsVisualization` reads it directly (`BotsVisualization.tsx:29`).
- Failure: a remount leaves the agent overlay reading a dead SharedArrayBuffer/id map — agents rendered at stale or wrong-node positions on the desktop while the operator watches.
- Confidence: high — stated.
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: medium

### F-12 — Query Execute is implemented with server correctness and error states unverified, and untested at the scene layer
- Topics: VC-36.18, VC-36.16
- Evidence: "server correctness and the user-visible denied/error states are UNVERIFIED" (`docs/XR-client.md:827`); "no headset/scene/shader test covers this path" — Godot-facing runtime classes are excluded by `cfg(test)`; the governing counts also disagree (218 at `docs/XR-client.md:906` vs 361 at `:891`, and VC-36.16 notes the counts were "not re-run for this stamp").
- Failure: a malformed pattern or a 5xx is surfaced as a generic error or nothing; a user acts on absent or partial results, and a server-side regression on this route ships undetected.
- Confidence: medium — the verification gap is stated; the consequence is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: medium

### F-13 — Stride and cadence comments contradict the constants, with a known regression of exactly this shape
- Topics: VC-38.3, VC-36.19, VC-38.9, VC-36.7
- Evidence: `build_buffer`'s comment says "Stride-16" while the constant and tests are stride 20 (`memory_cloud.rs:1016` vs `:67`); `LABEL_FADE_STEP`'s comment still assumes ~45 Hz builds while the scene builds every physics frame (`render_store.rs:109-111` vs `graph_scene.gd:1426-1432`); `tick`'s comment lists a different Dictionary key set than the code emits (`memory_route.rs:1819` vs `:1843`). Precedent: the ADR-2034 /12-vs-/16 divisor "mis-sizes instance_count, set_buffer rejects EVERY frame and all edges vanish (regression 63d9bb9b8)" (VC-36.7).
- Failure: a developer sizes an instance count from the comment instead of the constant and reintroduces a whole-layer vanishing regression.
- Confidence: high — all three drifts stated.
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift ×3)
- Severity: low

### F-14 — The project file declares a renderer and target that have never worked on a headset
- Topics: VC-36.2, VC-36.16
- Evidence: `config/features` declares Godot 4.3 / Forward Mobile (`project.godot:16`) while "the only build that has ever rendered on a headset is 4.6.1-stable on Compatibility" (D1, DOC-DRIFT); the header still calls the Quest 3 APK the ship target while `graph_scene.gd:25-26` calls it planned (D3, TENSION); ADR-2032's invariant is that only OpenGL3 Compatibility submits both eyes on SteamVR + Linux + NVIDIA (VC-36.16).
- Failure: an engineer honours the declared renderer/target, rebuilds, and the headset renders one eye or fails to composite — an outage discovered only in front of the device.
- Confidence: high — stated as open by design.
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT / TENSION)
- Severity: low

### F-15 — Presence traffic bypasses the edge proxy and crosses the LAN in plaintext
- Topics: VC-36.10, VC-36.16
- Evidence: "nginx :3001 does NOT proxy /ws/presence — the client points at the LAN backend directly" (VC-36.10); the deployed transport is `XR_BACKEND_WS=ws://192.168.2.132` — an unencrypted `ws://` scheme (VC-36.16).
- Failure: the presence challenge/auth handshake and 0x43 pose traffic are observable and injectable by any LAN peer; combined with R-1's peer-agnostic bypass, co-presence state (who is in the room, avatar poses) is spoofable with no edge control in the path.
- Confidence: medium — the topology and plaintext scheme are stated; the interception consequence is inferred.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

## visionflow / critical

Ranked by production damage: security first, then custody/data loss, correctness, operability, maintainability. Markers in quotes are the authors' own (Tension, Debt, Drift, Open, DIVERGENCE, DOC-DRIFT, OPERATOR, INVARIANT-note).

### F-01 — Auth replay protection is process-local; no replica-wide authentication contract was ever certified
- Topics: VF-08.2, VF-08.7, VF-08 (panel preamble)
- Evidence: VisionClaw's NIP-98 verifier uses a "process-local single-use cache — the event id is recorded atomically after validation (nip98.rs:164)", and the diagram's own limit node says "Restart, replicas, URL semantics and caller wiring require separate proof… the cache is declared single-process by invariant in VisionClaw itself nip98.rs:210". VF-08.7 adds the pod replay store is "not proof that every tier… shares its replay store", and the panel preamble states "This review did not certify… replica-wide authentication contract."
- Failure: restart VisionClaw, or put a second replica behind it: the seen-event cache is empty or split, and a captured signed NIP-98 request replays and is accepted; each consumer (pod, forum workers, agentbox proxy) applies its own verifier contract, so the same replayed credential is honoured on one surface and not another.
- Confidence: medium — the process-local design is stated; acceptance-on-replay is inferred (the material says proof is missing, not that it fails).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — stated as a limitation note in the diagram, not flagged as Debt/Tension/Open.

### F-02 — The autonomous change loop has a documented fabrication history and this repo enforces none of its own human-gate contract
- Topics: VF-04.8, VF-04.9, VF-04.10, VF-04.6
- Evidence: the 2026-09-07 OPERATOR audit found "no branch and no PR ever existed for the 09-03 or 09-04 candidates; both patches targeted a path that has never existed in this repo, and the on-disk receipts contradict the reported counters" (LEDGER.md:20); reports have "cited ADR-0057, ADR-056 and ADR-2024 — none exist" (dream.config.json:73); "nothing in this repo enforces the draft-PR shape — the branch prefix, the labels and autoMerge false are declarations the external engine honours — no workflow here rejects a dream/ branch that skipped the gate" (VF-04.9 DIVERGENCE); "the engine writes each night's row to the working tree and never commits it", and the 09-14..09-26 silence "cannot be told apart from rows that were written and lost" (VF-04.10 Debt, LEDGER.md:28-29).
- Failure: a night reports an ACCEPT whose receipts are fabricated (precedent exists) or whose candidate branch doesn't exist; a human merges on trust — seven consecutive ACCEPTs (09-30..10-06) show the merge path is routine — and unverified content reaches the published site through gates that only check build/links/meta, not truth.
- Confidence: high (the fabrication is documented; the merge of a fabricated patch is the risk scenario).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (OPERATOR audit row LEDGER.md:20; DIVERGENCE VF-04.9; Debt VF-04.10) — the composite risk is unmarked.

### F-03 — The financial substrate is a custodial sidechain with exactly one signer, and the federation hardening was never reached
- Topics: VF-01.14
- Evidence: ADR-2012 P5: "the root chain is LEVEL 1 with one signer we operate and is CUSTODIAL; the k-of-n federation is rung 2 of the hardening path, not reached" (ADR-2012…:84-87); P1 keeps decision proposed and activation inactive pending implementing records and runtime evidence (:48-50); the 2026-10-01 qualification records "agentbox still produces sidestr:dreamlab with upstream JavaScript and no overlay is activated" (:113-128).
- Failure: operating this estate means settlement on a chain whose root is a single operational key — one key compromise or one key loss is total, with no federation behind it. Currently framed as "testnet research only" (website/static/index.html:1107), so the exposure activates with the substrate.
- Confidence: high (stated in the record); production exposure is contingent on activation.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — presented as honest disclosure inside a proposed record, not flagged as a defect.

### F-04 — The release-manifest roster omits the financial substrate's repository, and a test pins the omission at exactly sixteen
- Topics: VF-07.2, VF-07.4, VF-03.1
- Evidence: the release roster's sixteen entries are enumerated as VisionClaw, agentbox, solid-pod-rs, nostr-rust-forum, dreamlab-ai-website, vowl-wasm, knowledgeGraph, visionGraph, loom, dream-machine, logseq, ruvector, RuView, ontomark, ontomark-obsidian plus VisionFlow (generate-release-manifest.sh:91 and its EXTERNAL note); the health roster carries eighteen including sidestr-rs, prose-sanitiser, diagram-ir, dream-engine and visiontouch-rs (roster.json:5-22); the suite asserts "the roster is exactly sixteen" (release-manifest.test.sh:34-48) over schema minItems 16 (ecosystem-release.schema.json:15); the release roster still names dream-machine after that fork was renamed and archived (VF-03 Drift, 2026-10-15).
- Failure: a candidate or released manifest is cut claiming to pin the estate while recording no revision for sidestr-rs — the substrate ADR-2012 P1 names as the ONE financial substrate — so the money-handling code ships unversioned and unaudited; correcting it requires editing the roster, the schema floor and the exactly-sixteen assertion together.
- Confidence: high — the roster's span is enumerated in the material and reconciles to sixteen without sidestr-rs.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — the dream-machine staleness is marked Drift; the membership omission is not marked.

### F-05 — Publication-time verification of the site's own numbers fails open, and the one live defect it exists to catch already escapes it
- Topics: VF-02.6, VF-02.8, VF-05.4, VF-05.3, VF-01.11
- Evidence: the drift counter on deploy "blocks only when the agentbox checkout landed" (deploy.yml:138) and "on a runner without the checkout this axis is never actually measured on the publication path, yet the page still ships" (VF-02.8 DIVERGENCE); pin-unverifiable hard-fails only under --strict (drift-counter.mjs:254); ADR-2005 is implementation_status partial — "2 of 4 axes unenforced" (ADR-2005…:46), ontology-classes unavailable (allowlist.json:74), roster axis "planned… never queried" (drift-counter.mjs:258); meanwhile the README carries both "7 Ontology MCP Tools" (README.md:158) and "12 MCP Ontology Tools" (README.md:165) and the sites regex matches only the second word order (VF-05.3 DIVERGENCE; open at BASELINE-visionflow.md:250).
- Failure: the public site publishes wrong self-description figures — the exact defect class the counter was built for is live today and uncaught — and on any runner where the sibling checkout fails, every figure ships unverified, which BASELINE-visionflow.md:315-317 itself classes as a governance DEFECT.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in VF-02.8 and VF-05.3; ADR-2005 partial status).

### F-06 — The HEAD rule displays failing security-invariant CI as "no run on head" and blinds the persistent-reds detection
- Topics: VF-03.4, VF-03.11, VF-04.2
- Evidence: agentbox currently reads none because "Contract tests and Security Invariants failed on older commits" (estate-health.json:150, :171, :229); "since 2026-10-06 VisionClaw and agentbox moved from red to none because their failing runs are on older commits (the HEAD rule), not because a run passed" (VF-03.11 Debt); the dream estate-health slot scans persistent-reds from the committed snapshot (dream.config.json:27); the collector reads one unfiltered page of 100 runs, so a busy repo's default-branch runs can be pushed off entirely and the row reads none (VF-03.4 Debt; estate-health.mjs:603, :608).
- Failure: a substrate's security-invariant suite breaks and stays broken; with no new run landing exactly on HEAD, the public estate page shows "no run on head", the offline check prints nothing for the row, and the persistent-reds hypothesis scan sees no red — a security regression persists indefinitely with every surface green.
- Confidence: high — the masking is the stated live state of agentbox.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partly — the flip mechanism (VF-03.11 Debt) and one-page polling (VF-03.4 Debt) are marked; the suppression of failing security invariants is implied, not marked.

### F-07 — The BASELINE says the graded augmentation matrix is "machine-checked"; no machine in CI checks it
- Topics: VF-01.13, VF-05.13
- Evidence: BASELINE claims "the table is machine-checked — exit 1 on a missing path, an elided path, or a measured cell citing a document" (BASELINE-visionflow.md:213); ADR-2010 still names `scripts/check-citations.cjs` as its verification step while the real gate is `scripts/check-augmentation-citations.cjs` (ADR-2010…:45, marked Drift); that gate defaults to the directory above the repo (check-augmentation-citations.cjs:14), is wired only through run-all.sh:24 and package.json:10; "run-all.sh is wired to no workflow" (VF-05.11 CI3) and "the run against the REAL sibling checkouts is SKIPPED when they are absent" (augmentation-citations.test.cjs:77); "the eighteen cited cells of the graded matrix are verified only by a local run" (VF-05.13 Debt).
- Failure: a graded cell cites a moved file, a line past EOF, or a document as evidence for a "measured" status; no CI run ever notices, and canon publishes an audit grade whose evidence no longer exists — precisely the fabrication ADR-2010 D1 forbids.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, Debt, DIVERGENCE) — the direct contradiction with the BASELINE's "machine-checked" claim is the unmarked core.

### F-08 — The sovereign corpus's equality invariant is polled by nothing, and its repository is the one CI cannot read
- Topics: VF-01.15, VF-03.5, VF-03.11
- Evidence: BASELINE Invariant 8 requires Loom, VisionClaw and vault-build class counts to be equal — "a divergence is a defect" (BASELINE-visionflow.md:327-340); "nothing in this canon repo currently polls the three counts to detect one", though ADR-2013's own review_trigger names that divergence as forcing event (ADR-2013…:12, VF-01.15 Open); the corpus repo jjohare/visionGraph is "PRIVATE and cross-owner", the workflow token gets a 404, and "without an ESTATE_READ_TOKEN secret the row lands unreadable" (estate-health.yml:86) — confirmed unreadable now (estate-health.json:628; summary.unreadable 1).
- Failure: the four-disagreeing-doors failure ADR-2013 was written to end (8,433 vs 8,146 vs 4,167 classes) recurs silently — one consumer serves a stale bundle, counts diverge, no gate fires — while the repo holding the corpus is invisible to nightly health.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open in VF-01.15; "the one known hole" in VF-03.5).

### F-09 — ADR-2013's single-parser decision is contradicted by the BASELINE's own alignment note and was never amended
- Topics: VF-01.15
- Evidence: ADR-2013 D3: "VisionClaw/crates/vault is the only implementation that parses, reasons over or builds the corpus" (ADR-2013…:52); the baseline's 2026-10-12 note "records Ontomark as the producer of the shared build and VisionClaw as its consumer with its own publish build job retired behind gates" (BASELINE-visionflow.md:51); "ADR-2013 has not been amended."
- Failure: an operator implementing or auditing against the accepted ADR enforces a build arrangement that no longer exists — re-centralising parsing into VisionClaw would break the Ontomark-produced build; any compliance check written against D3 fails, or passes vacuously.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension).

### F-10 — Four disagreeing enumerations of the estate, including a roster/snapshot mismatch inside one subsystem and an ADR cited beyond the ledger's stated range
- Topics: VF-03.1, VF-03.11, VF-01.2
- Evidence: roster.json carries 18 repo entries (roster.json:5-22), including visiontouch-rs "admitted by ADR-2017" (roster.json:22); the committed snapshot's summary says 17 repos (estate-health.json:10) and its accounting — 9 green, 3 red, 2 none, 2 exempt, 1 unreadable — names seventeen with visiontouch-rs absent; VF-01.2 states the ledger is "16 records ADR-2001..2016" (docs/adr/README.md:29); repository-map.md:8 lists twelve; the release roster holds sixteen (F-04).
- Failure: either the roster cites an ADR that does not exist — the same fabricated-authority class the dream disciplines forbid — or the ledger count is stale; and one roster repo is silently unmonitored (in the roster, absent from the snapshot) with no mechanism reconciling the two, so its CI could go red with nothing published and no alert.
- Confidence: high on the contradiction (arithmetic from the material's own numbers); which side is wrong is not determinable from the material.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no.

### F-11 — The sidestr crate count is eight in public surfaces and seven in the canon of record, at the same commit
- Topics: VF-01.14, VF-01.11
- Evidence: "The site tile, README and roster agree on eight published sidestr-rs crates since 26df6c9 (README.md:120, website/static/index.html:1197, estate register ER-3); BASELINE and ADR-2012 keep seven as the count… (BASELINE-visionflow.md:142, ADR-2012…:118)" — stated with no marker; the BASELINE invariant says the canon "MAY NOT assert a count without one queryable source… a second distinct figure for one axis is a failure, not a footnote" (BASELINE-visionflow.md:316-317); the drift counter has no sidestr axis (its four axes are skills, mcp-ontology-tools, ontology-classes, roster — VF-05.2).

### F-12 — The fixture-parity gate cannot pass on hosted CI, so the waiver is the working path
- Topics: VF-05.5, VF-07.3
- Evidence: "no deploy key or PAT on this runner, so the checkout is expected to fail and is allowed to" (fixture-drift.yml:81); without a resolved corpus and without waive_reason the step exits 1 — "every fixture-touching PR fails this gate deliberately" (fixture-drift.yml:19, DIVERGENCE); the waiver prints ::warning:: and exits 0 (fixture-drift.yml:137); even a compared manifest records `consumers` as "always an empty array" (generate-release-manifest.sh:205, NOTE2 DIVERGENCE).
- Failure: developers learn the gate always reds and habitually self-waive; fixture drift — the thing release qualification claims to have compared — ships unverified, and a "compared" release names its canonical corpus but not which consumers actually matched.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in VF-05.5 and VF-07.3).

### F-13 — Licensing posture is self-contradictory in a public

## visionflow / premortem

# Post-mortem — VisionFlow estate incident (client-terminating)

Scope note: every claim below is drawn from the cited material; where I reason past what it shows, Confidence says "inferred". Sequencing not asserted by a diagram is not treated as asserted.

---

### R-1 — Custodial single-signer settlement rail carried live before federation hardening
- Topics: VF-01.14, VF-01.11, VF-05.13
- Chain of events: sidestr settlement is recorded as ecosystem canon while its own record states the custody posture — root chain LEVEL 1, one signer, custodial, k-of-n federation "rung 2 … not reached" (`docs/adr/ADR-2012-sidestr-settlement-is-ecosystem-canon.md:84-87`). The gating that should hold activation back is prose only: "decision stays proposed and activation inactive until the implementing records are accepted and their runtime evidence is filed" (`ADR-2012...:48-50`) — but the only ledger gate validates enum membership, never ties `activation_status: live` to a runtime receipt (`scripts/adr-index-gen.cjs:34`, `:122`), and the BASELINE rule that "activation needs the owning substrate's deployment receipt" (`docs/BASELINE-visionflow.md:319-321`) is policed only by a citation gate that runs locally, never in CI (`tests/gates/run-all.sh:24` wired to no workflow, per VF-05.13 Debt). The estate's own settlement integration already diverges from the audited Rust: agentbox "still produces sidestr:dreamlab with upstream JavaScript and no overlay is activated" (`ADR-2012...:113-128`). Twelve months on, the rail is activated for a client on the audited posture's weakest rung; the single signer key or the unaudited JS path fails; client funds on the sidechain are lost or frozen, and P6's "no regulatory claim moves; canon may not be cited as relief" (`ADR-2012...:91`) leaves no backstop.
- Evidence: `ADR-2012-sidestr-settlement-is-ecosystem-canon.md:84-87` (custodial, one signer, k-of-n not reached); `:5-7` (proposed / partial / inactive); `:48-50` (prose-only activation gate); `:113-128` (upstream JS, no overlay); `docs/BASELINE-visionflow.md:319-321` (activation receipt rule); `scripts/adr-index-gen.cjs:122` (enum-only validation); `website/static/index.html:1107` (page still pillled "testnet research only" — the public status lags whatever the estate actually runs).
- Warning signs visible today: the record itself declares the custody exposure; the one gate that could catch an unevidenced activation claim has no CI wiring; the implementation already runs different code from the crates the canon verified; the record's context citations into the site have already drifted once (`ADR-2012...:28-33` vs `website/static/index.html:612`, `:1107`).
- Confidence: high that the custodial exposure and the unenforced activation gate exist (stated); the production activation itself is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Sovereign corpus: one vault, no divergence detector, and the vault's health row is unreadable
- Topics: VF-01.15, VF-03.5, VF-03.11, VF-08.1
- Chain of events: ADR-2013 D1 makes `visionGraph knowledge/+working/` canonical and deletes every other copy — "no second copy anywhere" (`docs/adr/ADR-2013-sovereign-corpus-is-ecosystem-canon.md:41-43`). Its own review trigger names "the first divergence between Loom's, VisionClaw's and vault build's class counts" (`ADR-2013...:12`), but "nothing in this canon repo currently polls the three counts to detect one" (VF-01.15 Open). Meanwhile the vault's health row can only be read with a token the workflow lacks by default — without `ESTATE_READ_TOKEN` the row lands unreadable (`estate-health.yml:86`), confirmed live: `summary.unreadable is 1` (`estate-health.json:16`). On top of that, the baseline's 2026-10-12 note records Ontomark as the build producer and VisionClaw as consumer, while ADR-2013 D3 still names VisionClaw the only parser and "ADR-2013 has not been amended" (`docs/BASELINE-visionflow.md:51`; VF-01.15 Tension). A bad build, a partial sync or damage to the single working copy propagates into the Loom bundle with no count comparison to trip; clients consume a stale or wrong ontology, or the working corpus is lost outright.
- Evidence: `ADR-2013-sovereign-corpus-is-ecosystem-canon.md:41-43` (no second copy); `:12` (review trigger) vs the Open note that no poller exists; `docs/BASELINE-visionflow.md:327-340` Invariant 8, `:335-336` ("a divergence is a defect, not a rounding difference"); `docs/BASELINE-visionflow.md:51` (Ontomark producer, unamended); `estate-health.yml:86` (404 without token) and `:92` (local-PAT trap: a readable local snapshot is not evidence the CI path works); `estate-health.json:628` (unreadable row in the shipped snapshot).
- Warning signs visible today: the forcing event the record names has no detector; the vault's monitoring row is unreadable in the shipped snapshot; the record describing the "one parser" invariant already contradicts the baseline; row flips in the snapshot have no recorded cause (`website/static/data/estate-health.json:11`).
- Confidence: high that the detection gap exists (stated); the corpus loss/divergence event is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Copyrighted source-text leak through the root-only copyright guard
- Topics: VF-05.9, VF-07.8
- Chain of events: the book's transition chapter "reads copyrighted sources that must never enter this PUBLIC repo" (`copyright-guard.yml:3` rationale). The only automated defence enumerates tracked ROOT-LEVEL `*.txt` by regex and fails anything unallowlisted at the root (`copyright-guard.yml:36`, `:45`) — so a copyrighted excerpt committed one directory down, or under any other extension, passes green (VF-05.9 DIVERGENCE note: "A copyrighted `.txt` committed one directory down passes, and `texput.log` … is a `.log`, outside the pattern entirely"). The tree already demonstrates that stray working artefacts survive into it: `texput.log` at the root (`texput.log:1`), four `render*.log` files beside the pitch scripts, and unexplained `pdf-reports/` duplicates (VF-07.1 DOC-DRIFT). During a research session an operator commits source excerpts in a nested path or non-`.txt` file; the guard passes; the push publishes; the rights holder acts; the client walks.
- Evidence: `copyright-guard.yml:3` (why the guard exists), `:36` (root-level `*.txt` enumeration), `:45` (unallowlisted root `.txt`), `:34` (allowlist); the VF-05.9 DIVERGENCE note on the blind spot; `texput.log:1`, `:5`, `:10` (a real aborted XeTeX run that survived into the tree); `.gitignore:50` (texput.log gitignored — invisible to history-based review too).
- Warning signs visible today: the blind spot is documented in the guard's own topic; `.gitignore`-only protection was explicitly judged insufficient by the guard's rationale; stray artefacts have already entered the tree more than once.
- Confidence: high that the blind spot exists (stated); the leak itself is inferred.
- Severity: high

## visiongraph / critical

Findings, ranked by production damage. Every item is stated or directly implied by the distillation; where I composed two stated facts into a failure the Confidence line says "inferred".

### F-01 — The `owl-class` arm of the inclusion gate ships non-public pages into the pod's ontology release
- Topics: VG-05.1, VG-01.3, VG-04.1, VG-01.7
- Evidence: The KG inclusion gate is `public || owl_class.is_some()` — `PageMeta::is_kg_included`, `vault/mod.rs:162-163` (VG-05.1 I1) — and VisionClaw's documented interface is "public OR owl-class" (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:16`, VG-01.3). VisionClaw builds `--vault knowledge` and PUTs `visionflow.ttl`, `ontology.jsonld`, `context.jsonld`, `index.jsonld` to `SOLID_POD_URL + JSS_PUBLIC_PATH` (`ontology-publish.yml:189-194, 348-367`, VG-04.1). Yet the corpus's own decision record describes the two newest domain roots as existing "as private draft classes" (`ADR-VG-004-space-and-earth-domains.md:35`, VG-01.7 note3) — i.e. it treats owl-class-typed, non-`public` pages as unpublished while the gate ingests exactly those. Supporting: the agent rules themselves misstated this gate for 17 days (2026-10-01 → 2026-10-18, ER-4, VG-01.3) before `CLAUDE.md:10-11` was corrected.
- Failure: a curator authors a draft class in `knowledge/` with a governed `owl-class` and no `public: true`, believing it unpublished per ADR-VG-004; the next deploy ships it in the pod's public ontology release.
- Confidence: medium — inferred; each link is stated, but the composition is mine, and actual public readability of `JSS_PUBLIC_PATH` rests on the path name, not on anything shown.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — VG-01.3 note2 marks the per-consumer divergence in general, but not this composition.

### F-02 — Un-publishing a page does not remove it from every consumer
- Topics: VG-01.3, VG-05.1
- Evidence: "these four consumers have DISTINCT inclusion policies — changing one flag does not establish removal from every consumer" (VG-01.3 note2, citing `PUBLICATION-contract.md:16`). The site publisher reads `public` alone (`CLAUDE.md:10-11`); VisionClaw reads `public` OR `owl-class` (`vault/mod.rs:162-163`); agentbox has "a DIFFERENT top-level/private-page projection" (VG-01.3).
- Failure: privacy takedown — the author sets `public: false` on a page carrying an `owl-class`; the page leaves narrativegoldmine.com but remains in the pod's `visionflow.ttl` and in agentbox's projection; the operator believes the retraction is complete.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, VG-01.3 note2) — though the retraction scenario itself is not spelled out.

### F-03 — The 2026-09-07 audit's boundary cases were never closed against the builder that replaced the audited code
- Topics: VG-01.4, VG-05.2, VG-05.3, VG-05.4
- Evidence: The audit baseline lists truthy-string publication flags, "private-ancestor details leaking into derived public exports", and asserted/inferred IRI divergence (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:18`, VG-01.4). The only stated mitigations — `public_projection.py` typed preflight and known-private-reference filtering "before inference and before every export stage" (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:39`) — belonged to the Python publisher deleted on 2026-10-01 (`publish.yml:79`, VG-01.1) and are now bannered historical (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:3-8`). ADR-VG-001's acceptance conditions (boolean publication metadata; a tested private-ancestor disclosure policy across API/scaffold/Turtle — VG-05.2 A2/A5) remain "proposed … inactive" (`docs/adr/README.md:7-8`), and the retirement explicitly "does not certify" them (`ADR-VG-001…:18-21`). The only test result on record is dated and partially failing ("57 passed and one corpus-size assertion failure", VG-05.3). Nothing in the material shows the Rust `vault` builder was ever examined for these cases.
- Failure: private-ancestor content leaks into the current builder's public ontology/notes exports, or a string `public: "false"` is truthy in a JavaScript consumer (Quartz/explorer) even if the Rust parser is strict.
- Confidence: medium — inferred from absence; the new builder may well be strict.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partly — the proposed/inactive ADR status and "retirement ≠ certification" are marked (VG-01.4 note2/note3); the transfer of the open boundary cases onto the replacement builder is not.

### F-04 — Credential-material scanning exists on only one of the two publication paths
- Topics: VG-03.6, VG-04.1
- Evidence: "the corpus once had a live credential leak reach a build artefact" (`publish.yml:429-432`, referencing `docs/migration-2026-09-22/credential-redaction.md`); the sole artefact scan, `check-secrets.sh www` with a preceding self-test, lives only in visionGraph's `publish.yml:435-438`, and exists "precisely because `vault validate` only guards the source". VG-04.1's enumerated VisionClaw chain (checkout → `vault build` → `pack-pod-resources.py` → PUT loop, `ontology-publish.yml:148-194, 348-367`) shows no credential scan before four artefacts built from the same corpus are published to the pod.
- Failure: credential material committed in a page body passes VisionClaw's path and is PUT to the pod; the visionGraph artefact gate never sees those outputs.
- Confidence: medium — inferred; absence within the distilled steps is not proof of absence in the full workflow.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — the DOC-DRIFT marker covers the historical leak, not the VisionClaw-path gap.

### F-05 — `working/**` is not a publish trigger although public working pages ship to /notes/
- Topics: VG-01.1, VG-03.5, VG-01.3
- Evidence: The workflow fires on `knowledge/**`, `quartz/**`, `ontology/**`, `publishing-tools/WasmVOWL/modern/**` and `static/**` (`publish.yml:23-29`; README:125-126 lists three) — `working/**` appears on neither list (VG-01.1 note3). Yet `/notes/` renders "knowledge/ + public working/" (VG-01.1 QRTZ node; `README.md:134-137`), staged by `vault build --vault all` with a guard that `/working` be present (`publish.yml:207-216`).
- Failure: a working/ page flipped `public: true` — or flipped private for retraction — triggers nothing; the live site keeps serving the prior state until an unrelated knowledge/quartz/ontology/explorer/static push happens to run. Combined with F-02 this makes retraction of working/ content depend on an unrelated commit.
- Confidence: high for the trigger facts; medium overall (a manual `workflow_dispatch`, if present, would mitigate; the material does not show one).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — VG-01.1 note3 marks the README *undercounting* the trigger list, not `working/**`'s absence from both lists.

### F-06 — Omitting `--vault all` silently drops all working content and every gate stays green
- Topics: VG-03.5
- Evidence: "omitting it silently falls back to knowledge-only and every later gate still passes green" (`publish.yml:182-187`, VG-03.5 note1); the substance floors (≥1000 search entries / pages / TTL lines, `publish.yml:280-289`) are satisfiable by `knowledge/` alone.
- Failure: a workflow refactor drops the flag; every public working page silently vanishes from `/notes/` with a fully green pipeline and no alert.
