### F-07 — Site and pod are built by different `vault` revisions from the same source sha
- Topics: VG-04.1, VG-03.1
- Evidence: The site is built by `vault` at pinned `VAULT_GIT_REV=90a7a85c` (`publish.yml:50`); VisionClaw "builds vault from VisionClaw's own checkout … NOT visionGraph's VAULT_GIT_REV pin" (`ontology-publish.yml:145-146, 167-168`); "the site and the pod release can be produced by different `vault` revisions from the same source sha. No record says whether that difference is intended."
- Failure: VisionClaw main changes serialization or semantics — identical corpus sha yields a pod TTL that diverges from the site's `ontology.json` (silent split-brain between the two public artefacts); or VC main breaks and the pod release stops updating while the site continues.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, VG-04.1)

### F-08 — The 1 GB deploy gate sits 39 MB above the assets tree, or is blind to it
- Topics: VG-01.1, VG-03.5, VG-03.6
- Evidence: `working/assets` is 961 MB, stored once and symlinked into `knowledge/assets` and `quartz/content/assets` (VG-01.1; `publish.yml:202-206`); the finalize gate "hard-fails over 1000MB, warns over 900MB" apparent size (`publish.yml:331-354`). Either the assets count toward the gate — the warn threshold is already exceeded and only 39 MB of headroom remains before every deploy hard-fails — or the symlinked tree is not counted and the gate is blind to the largest content in the corpus. The material does not say which.
- Failure: (a) one ~40 MB podcast-asset batch bricks all deploys until someone edits the gate; (b) unbounded asset growth passes the gate and breaks serving.
- Confidence: medium — inferred; which branch holds depends on unstated cp/du/Quartz symlink semantics.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — Push-to-production single environment: no staging, and the only rollback machinery on record is retired
- Topics: VG-03.1, VG-05.1, VG-05.4, VG-01.1
- Evidence: The enumerated pipeline is one job whose terminal content step is DEPLOY to production (`publish.yml:449-457`) followed by dispatch; "a push deploys to narrativegoldmine.com" is the stated reason agents must never push (`CLAUDE.md:23-26`, VG-05.1 I7); the trigger surface includes the vendored explorer and `static/**` (`publish.yml:23-29`, VG-01.1 note3). The only rollback/promotion machinery described anywhere — rename backups, retained recovery directory, rollback on failure — is bannered historical and belonged to the deleted Python publisher (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:39`, banner `:3-8`, VG-05.4). No staging, canary, or rollback step appears in the current chain.
- Failure: a defective explorer or ontology change passes the gates and is live to every reader; recovery is revert-and-redeploy through the same serialised single pipeline (concurrency group `deploy-ontology`, `publish.yml:32-38`).
- Confidence: medium — inferred from absence in the distilled workflow.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — The sole producer of all machine artefacts is built from source every run, at one pinned external revision, with no fallback
- Topics: VG-03.1, VG-03.5, VG-01.1
- Evidence: `VAULT_RELEASE_TAG` is empty, so "every run builds `vault` from source at `VAULT_GIT_REV`" (`publish.yml:43-50`, DEBT); `vault build` is "the sole producer of machine artefacts and Quartz content staging" (VG-03.5); ADR-VG-003 removed every fallback — "no Python ontology pipeline fallback" (`README.md:128-131`) — and the pin is a single VisionClaw revision on which "the eight-domain publish depends".
- Failure: the pinned VisionClaw revision becomes unbuildable (toolchain drift, history rewrite, repo loss) → every deploy fails and nothing else can produce the site's artefacts.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, VG-03.1)

### F-11 — ADR-VG-004's consequences contradict the deployed state in three ways at once
- Topics: VG-01.7, VG-03.5
- Evidence: Three mutually inconsistent states: the ADR index says "implementation complete, activation staged" (`docs/adr/README.md:10`); ADR-VG-004:35 says the roots "exist as private draft classes" and "the changes are not deployed"; the release gate requires each root present in the public search index (`check-space-domains.mjs:22-23`) and the workflow running it deploys on push (`publish.yml:296-297`). Additionally `vocabulary.yaml:125` and `:316-317` still say six domain roots (VG-01.7 note2).
- Failure: an operator trusts the ADR, treats domains 6–7 as undeployed private drafts (directly feeding F-01), or repeats deployment work that is already live.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT, VG-01.7 note2 and note3)

### F-12 — The release gate hard-codes corpus totals, coupling every content batch to a gate edit
- Topics: VG-03.5
- Evidence: The gate pins exact totals — 934 expansion identities and 660 researched drafts (`check-space-domains.mjs:53-54`) — "so every research batch must edit the release gate in the same commit; the gate file changed in 56 commits between `b382a1275` and `9d667562`, almost all of them article batches."
- Failure: a research batch lands without the co-edit → deploy hard-fails and blocks all publication (including unrelated fixes) until the gate is bumped; or bumping becomes mechanical and the check stops asserting anything but the count.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, VG-03.5)

### F-13 — The shipping explorer is an unsynced fork whose own runbook is false and whose only sibling cannot substitute for it
- Topics: VG-06.1, VG-06.2, VG-06.3, VG-06.4, VG-06.5
- Evidence: 33 shared files now differ, up from 24 (VG-06.1). The shipping tree's own CLAUDE.md documents a `rust-wasm/` crate that does not exist — "none of these commands can succeed" (VG-06.2, lines 25/39/96) — while the corrected text landed only in the non-shipping sibling. CAPABILITIES.md measures rustc/wasm-pack/`python -m pipeline.build`, none of which run (VG-06.3). `router.tsx` diverged in the shipping tree's favour (basename support, `router.tsx:64-75,135`) and `mesh.ts` in the sibling's (LOOM export, `explorer/modern/src/site/mesh.ts:38`) with "no shared sync discipline either way" (VG-06.4). The sibling carries six domains and "can no longer be dropped in as a replacement" (VG-06.5).
- Failure: during a live-site incident, on-call follows the shipping CLAUDE.md rebuild recipe and burns the window; there is no known-good second copy to roll back to; fixes continue landing on whichever copy someone happens to edit.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, DOC-DRIFT and DIVERGENCE across VG-06)

### F-14 — Identity separation is mandated but no release manifest exists to implement it
- Topics: VG-04.3, VG-05.2, VG-03.1
- Evidence: Source, distribution and deployed-artefact identities "must NOT be collapsed into one revision" (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:14`), and ADR-VG-002 proposes "ONE release manifest: source/corpus/pipeline/explorer/dependency/policy identity + output hashes" — but it is "proposed, NOT yet activation_status: live" (VG-04.3 note1, VG-05.2 B1). No manifest-emitting step appears anywhere in the publish.yml chain (VG-03.1).
- Failure: incident forensics cannot establish which `vault` revision and consumer generation produced the currently live TTL and notes (compounded by F-07); choosing a rollback target is guesswork.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (VG-04.3 note1; proposed-status markers in VG-05.2)

### F-15 — The governing contract's body still specifies a rejected input format and a deleted publisher, in the present tense
- Topics: VG-01.3, VG-05.4
- Evidence: `PUBLICATION-contract.md:16` still says the site publisher "reads JSON-LD Page vc:public" — "a rejected format" (`README.md:50`) — and the body states in the present tense that "both publishers use `public_projection.py`" (`:39`) and that the frozen notes SPA receives a preserved scoped stylesheet (`:41`) whose file (`publishing-tools/notes-mobile.css`) was deleted 2026-10-01; only a top banner (`:3-8`) marks the body historical.
- Failure: a new consumer implementer follows the body and builds `vc:public` JSON-LD parsing against a corpus that carries no fences — producing an empty consumer, the same failure class `publish.yml:79` records for the deleted pipeline.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT "narrowed", VG-01.3 note1; Drift, VG-05.4)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Pod exposure (decisive for F-01/F-02):** whether `JSS_PUBLIC_PATH` on the Solid pod is publicly readable or access-controlled, and what the `deploy-jss` condition at `ontology-publish.yml:307` actually gates — the pull-model note ("a GitHub-hosted runner cannot reach the LAN pod") leaves the real delivery path unclear.
2. **The Rust builder's boundary behaviour (decisive for F-03):** whether `vault` enforces typed-boolean `public` values, filters private-ancestor references before export, and normalises asserted/inferred IRIs — needs VisionClaw's `docs/VAULT-corpus-format.md` (ADR-2040/2041/2042) and the vault crate's tests (VC-21), neither of which is in these sources.
3. **The two workflows in full:** whether VisionClaw's `ontology-publish.yml` contains any credential scan outside the steps VG-04.1 distils (decisive for F-04), and whether `publish.yml` offers `workflow_dispatch` or a schedule that would mitigate F-05's stale working pages.
4. **The finalize/apparent-size script:** whether the 1 GB gate counts the 961 MB of symlinked assets (and whether Quartz copies through symlinks into `www/`) — decides which branch of F-08 actually holds.
5. **Any staging or rollback procedure outside publish.yml:** whether a canary, staging deploy, or documented gh-pages/pod rollback runbook exists elsewhere in the estate — decisive for F-09, since the material only permits saying none appears in the workflow it distils.

## visiongraph / premortem

# Post-mortem — visionGraph publication estate

Incident class: data/custody/security failure with client loss, twelve months after the `bc4845c34` / `9d667562` stamps. Everything below is drawn from the verified claims in VG-01 through VG-06, cited as the material cites them. Where a step of a chain is not in the material, confidence says "inferred". Every finding is to be re-checked against the cited `path:line` before action.

---

### R-1 — One corpus, four inclusion gates: private material shipped through the consumer whose gate the operator had been told was narrower
- Topics: VG-01.3, VG-05.1, VG-01.4, VG-01.1, VG-01.6
- Chain of events: 1. A curator or agent "privatises" a page — removes or never sets `public: true` — believing that one flag controls all publication. 2. The page is either in `knowledge/` carrying an `owl-class` key, or in `working/`, where unknown frontmatter keys are tolerated. 3. The site publisher (reads `public` alone) and Quartz (`ExplicitPublish({ key: "public" })` over the staging tree) hold it back — nothing visible on narrativegoldmine.com, so nothing looks wrong. 4. VisionClaw's `vault build --vault knowledge` applies `public || owl_class.is_some()` and ingests it. 5. `ontology-publish.yml` packs the output and PUTs `visionflow.ttl` / `ontology.jsonld` / `index.jsonld` to `SOLID_POD_URL + JSS_PUBLIC_PATH` — a public path. 6. With ADR-VG-002's release manifest still inactive, the scope of what shipped cannot be reconstructed after discovery.
- Evidence: gate code `vault/mod.rs:162-163` (VG-05.1 I1); "these four consumers have DISTINCT inclusion policies — changing one flag does not establish removal from every consumer" (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:16`, VG-01.3 note2); the agent rules read typed `public: true` alone as the KG gate from 2026-10-01 until corrected 2026-10-18 at `bc4845c34` (VG-01.3 "Why it is this way"; `CLAUDE.md:10-11`, ER-4); audit boundary cases B2 (truthy string flags) and B3 (private-ancestor leakage) still open (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:18`); ADR-VG-001's requirements for BOOLEAN publication metadata and a tested disclosure policy are `proposed`/`inactive` (`ADR-VG-001-publication-policy-boundaries.md:29`; `docs/adr/README.md:7-8`); `working/` is `unknown_keys ALLOW` (`vault.toml:45`) yet public working pages render to `/notes/` (`README.md:134-137`); pod PUTs at `ontology-publish.yml:348-367`.
- Warning signs visible today: the 17-day window in which the documented gate was wrong while the code never changed; ADR-VG-001/002 still proposed, partial, inactive with banners saying their acceptance conditions are not certified; `README.md:24` calling `working/` "not published" while the build publishes public working pages; `working/bases/public.base` ("Everything public") as a one-flag path to publication.
- Confidence: high — the gate divergence is stated as fact by the material; the incident scenario is inferred from it.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — The pod release was built by a converter nobody validated: the dispatch pins the corpus sha, not the tool
- Topics: VG-04.1, VG-03.1
- Chain of events: 1. visionGraph's publisher validates and builds with `vault` at `VAULT_GIT_REV` = `90a7a85c`, deploys, then dispatches `corpus-sync` with `source_repo` + `source_sha`. 2. VisionClaw checks out the pinned corpus revision but builds `vault` from its own default-branch checkout (`cargo build -p vault`). 3. That revision's parsing/inference behaviour differs from the validated one — inclusion semantics, or IRI representation (audit case B4). 4. The cited pod pipeline shows only the substance floor (4000 classes / 100k triples) between build and PUT, so a semantic regression passes. 5. The client's embedded system consumes triples the site's gates never evaluated; site and pod disagree on the same source sha, and with no release manifest the difference cannot be attributed after the fact.
- Evidence: "the dispatch pins the corpus revision but not the converter … No record says whether that difference is intended" (VG-04.1, Open); `publish.yml:50` vs `ontology-publish.yml:145-146,167-168`; `VAULT_RELEASE_TAG` empty — every run builds `vault` from source (`publish.yml:43-50`, VG-03.1 Debt); floor 4000/100k against 8,434 classes / 265,455 triples (`ADR-2106-ontology-pull-model-into-the-embedded-pod.md:38`); B4 asserted-vs-inferred IRI divergence across outputs (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:18`).
- Warning signs visible today: the Open finding itself, unresolved; no released, versioned `vault` binary anywhere in the chain; the pod path's only cited gate is a count floor, which cannot catch a semantic change.
- Confidence: medium — the divergence is explicitly flagged by the authors; its conversion into this incident is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — The disclosure protections retired with the Python publisher: private-ancestor detail in derived public exports
- Topics: VG-05.4, VG-05.3, VG-01.4, VG-03.5
- Chain of events: 1. The 2026-09-07 execution update gave the Python publishers typed input preflight and known-private-reference filtering "before inference and before every export stage". 2. On 2026-10-01, ADR-VG-003 retired that publisher and the `pipeline/` was deleted; the contract body describing those protections is now marked historical by a banner. 3. No cited record shows the Rust `vault` binary reimplements the filtering, and the tests that asserted the exclusion policy were the Python pipeline's own. 4. `vault build`'s inference derives public content that references private ancestors, and exports `ontology.ttl`, `ontology.json`, `api/`, `search-index.json` to the public site root (`vault.toml:70-72`) and, via VisionClaw, to the pod. 5. Audit case B3 — documented as reproducible — becomes a live leak as the corpus grows.
- Evidence: `PUBLICATION-contract.md:39` (public_projection.py filtering; the real-corpus test asserting the exclusion policy; the held-page fixture); banner at `PUBLICATION-contract.md:3-8` marking the body "not current operating instructions"; `ADR-VG-003-obsidian-only-authoring.md:16-24`; `publish.yml:79` (pipeline deleted); B3/B4 at `PUBLICATION-contract.md:18`; ADR-VG-001's "deliberate, TESTED private-ancestor disclosure policy across API/scaffold/Turtle" still proposed/inactive (`ADR-VG-001:29`; `docs/adr/README.md:7-8`); both ADRs' banners stating retirement does not certify acceptance (`ADR-VG-001-publication-policy-boundaries.md:18-21`).
- Warning signs visible today: VG-05.3's explicit statement that no real private-page disclosure is established and that boundary cases are unfixed; the protections described only in text now marked historical.
- Confidence: medium — the protection gap is documented as uncertified; whether the Rust build filters equivalently is unstated, so the leak itself is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Silent knowledge-only fallback when `--vault all` is omitted, with every later gate green
- Topics: VG-03.5, VG-03.1
- Evidence: "INVARIANT: --vault all is required — omitting it silently falls back to knowledge-only and every later gate still passes green" (`publish.yml:182-187`); the both-vaults guard (`publish.yml:207-216`) and substance checks (`publish.yml:274-289`) do not catch it per the note.
- Failure: a workflow refactor drops the flag; the site and the corpus-sync dispatch ship knowledge-only; public `working/` content silently vanishes from `/notes/` and downstream; all gates pass and detection comes from the client.
- Confidence: high (behaviour stated; occurrence inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (flagged as an invariant with the silent-green failure stated)

### F-02 — Substance floors are static counts; release-gate counts are exact-pinned and edited every batch
- Topics: VG-03.5, VG-04.1
- Evidence: floors of ≥1000 for search-index entries, api/pages count and `ontology.ttl` lines (`publish.yml:280-289`); pod floor 4000/100k against 8,434/265,455 (`ADR-2106:38`); exact pins 934 expansion identities / 660 researched drafts (`check-space-domains.mjs:53-54`); the gate file changed in 56 commits between `b382a1275` and `9d667562`, "almost all of them article batches" (VG-03.5, Debt).
- Failure: a build that loses most of the corpus still clears the ≥1000 floors; conversely a legitimate shrink below 934 hard-fails all deploys until the gate is edited, and routine gate-editing normalises loosening the release gate under pressure.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-03 — No release manifest binds source, converter and output identities across the three identities
- Topics: VG-04.3, VG-05.2, VG-05.3
- Evidence: ADR-VG-002 B1 — "ONE release manifest: source/corpus/pipeline/explorer/dependency/policy identity + output hashes — proposed, NOT yet activation_status: live" (VG-04.3 note1; `ADR-VG-002-generation-and-consumer-identity.md:29`); "source, distribution and deployed artefact identities must NOT be collapsed into one revision" (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:14`).
- Failure: after any incident, the operator cannot prove which `vault` revision produced the live site or the pod artefacts; scoping exposure and choosing a rollback target is guesswork.
- Confidence: high (gap certain; consequence inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (proposed/inactive flagged)

### F-04 — Credential scanning covers `www/` only; the pod artefact path shows no scan; a real leak precedent exists
- Topics: VG-03.6, VG-04.1
- Evidence: `check-secrets.sh www` guards the artefact precisely because `vault validate` only guards the source, referencing a real prior leak (`publish.yml:429-438`, citing `docs/migration-2026-09-22/credential-redaction.md`); the cited pod path — pack (`ontology-publish.yml:189-194`) then PUT (`:348-367`) — shows no secrets scan.
- Failure: credential-bearing content passes format validation, is packed by `pack-pod-resources.py` and PUT to the public JSS path; only the site artefact, not the pod artefact, is ever scanned.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — absence of a scan is from the cited lines only)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift — the artefact-vs-source gap and the precedent; the pod-path extension is unmarked)

### F-05 — The assets symlink publishes unreviewed `working/` material with no frontmatter gate
- Topics: VG-01.1, VG-03.5, VG-05.1
- Evidence: `knowledge/assets` is a symlink to `../working/assets`, 961 MB "stored once, shared" (VG-01.1; VG-05.1 I5, "never replace it"); the build symlinks `knowledge/assets` into `quartz/content/assets` (`publish.yml:202-206`); `working/` is the research vault with `unknown_keys ALLOW` (`vault.toml:45`).
- Failure: any file dropped into `working/assets` — research material, client documents — becomes reachable under the published `/notes/` tree; assets carry no `public` flag, so no gate applies. Whether the whole symlink target is served or only referenced assets is unstated.
- Confidence: medium (inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (the invariant warns about replacing the symlink, not about exposure)

### F-06 — Non-boolean publication flags meet two gates with independent semantics
- Topics: VG-01.4, VG-01.3, VG-05.2
- Evidence: audit case B2 — "truthy string publication flags ('false' as a Python truthy value)" — unfixed in the 2026-09-07 baseline (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:18`); ADR-VG-001 A2 "require BOOLEAN publication metadata" proposed/inactive (`ADR-VG-001:29`; `docs/adr/README.md:7-8`); Quartz applies its own `ExplicitPublish({ key: "public" })` over the vault staging tree (`quartz/README.md:25-26`); four consumers with distinct inclusion policies (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:16`).
- Failure: a quoted `public: "false"` is interpreted differently by the vault's typed gate, Quartz's key gate and VisionClaw's `public ||` — the page is excluded from the validated projection but rendered by Quartz, or ingested by VisionClaw; exclusion believed done is not. The current Rust/Quartz parsers' string handling is unstated in the material.
- Confidence: medium (the unfixed audit finding is certain; current parser behaviour is inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (audit finding; Divergence note)

### F-07 — README says `working/` is "not published" while the build publishes public working pages
- Topics: VG-01.1, VG-01.3, VG-01.6
- Evidence: `README.md:24` — `working/` "not published" (VG-01.1); `README.md:134-137` — `/notes/` renders the `vault build` staging over knowledge plus public working pages; `vault.toml:48` — `roles.working … publishable false`; `working/bases/public.base` — "Everything public" (`working/bases/public.base:32`).
- Failure: a curator treats `working/` as private scratch per the README and the manifest, stores client-sensitive drafts there; one `public: true` — typo, base-driven edit, or agent — ships them to narrativegoldmine.com, with no governed-key enforcement in that vault (`vault.toml:45`).
- Confidence: high (the conflict is on the face of the material; the scenario is inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (both facts are presented; the contradiction itself is unflagged)

### F-08 — The release gate forces publication of ADR-VG-004's "private draft" roots
- Topics: VG-01.7, VG-03.5
- Evidence: ADR-VG-004:35 — "the two roots exist as private draft classes … the changes are not deployed"; `check-space-domains.mjs:22-23` — the gate fails unless each root is present in the public search index; `publish.yml:296-297` runs it on every deploy; flagged as Drift in VG-01.7 note3.

- Evidence: `VAULT_RELEASE_TAG` empty — every run compiles `vault` from source at `VAULT_GIT_REV` = `90a7a85c`, and "the eight-domain publish depends on that exact VisionClaw revision" (`publish.yml:43-50`, VG-03.1 Debt); the explorer depends on the pinned `@dreamlab-ai/vowl-wasm` 0.1.2 (`WasmVOWL/modern/package.json:20`).

- Evidence: audit case B4 — "asserted vs inferred IRI representation divergence across outputs" in the 2026-09-07 baseline (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:18`); ADR-VG-001's explicit-disposition requirement remains proposed/inactive (`ADR-VG-001:29`; `docs/adr/README.md:7-8`).

## vowl-wasm / critical

### F-01 — Crate-wide `deny(unsafe_code)` contradicted by an `unsafe` SIMD function
- Topics: VW-01.1, VW-01.4, VW-03.4, VW-05.2
- Evidence: `src/lib.rs:141-142` declares `#!deny(missing_docs, unsafe_code)` (VW-01.1), while VW-03.4 shows `unsafe calculate_leaf_force_simd()` at `src/layout/simd.rs:386` under `target_feature = "simd128"`, enabled by the documented `simd` feature (Cargo.toml:92). Either an `#[allow(unsafe_code)]` exists — making the crate's advertised no-unsafe guarantee false whenever `simd` is on — or the `simd` feature cannot compile. Additionally, the dispatch gate shown (`src/layout/quadtree.rs:341,343`) tests only feature flag and `sources.len() >= 4`; `is_simd_available()` (simd.rs:516) exists but is not shown gating the call, so the target-feature function may be reachable on hardware without simd128.
- Failure: someone enables `simd` for a customer build: the release compile breaks, or (with an allow present) the "no unsafe" audit premise silently fails and an unprobed call traps or executes UB on non-SIMD128 devices.
- Confidence: high that the two claims conflict; the resolution (allow vs broken feature) and the missing runtime probe are medium, inferred
- Severity: medium (latent — `simd` is not in the published bundle per VW-04.5)
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — Zero-copy position views can be invalidated by WASM memory growth
- Topics: VW-01.7, VW-03.6, VW-03.9, VW-04.2, VW-04.6
- Evidence: the invariant "NggExplorer never allocates across the WASM boundary **per frame**" (explorer.rs:9) is a per-frame guarantee only. `loadCsr → load` allocates fresh `positions`/`velocities` Vecs (csr_sim.rs:193-194), and the worker builds `new Float32Array(wasm.memory.buffer, ptr, len)` views (VW-04.6). Any growth of linear memory after a view is created detaches the buffer.
- Failure: the worker caches the Float32Array after the first `loadCsr`; a second tier load (or any allocating call) grows wasm memory; the cached view detaches and per-frame reads return undefined/NaN or throw — an intermittent, timing-dependent rendering failure in the shipped explorer.
- Confidence: medium, inferred — depends on whether the worker re-fetches `positionsPtr()` per load and whether memory is pre-reserved; the "SharedArrayBuffer or transferable double-buffer" note (VW-04.6) suggests a mitigation exists but which one is unstated
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — Classes beyond `max_classes` are silently truncated, not rejected
- Topics: VW-02.2, VW-02.1
- Evidence: `ParserConfig.max_classes` (parser.rs:17) is enforced at parser.rs:62-64 such that "a `class` array longer than the configured cap is silently truncated, not rejected."
- Failure: a production ontology exceeding the cap loads "successfully"; classes vanish with no error or warning; edges whose endpoints were truncated hit `graph.add_edge(source, target, ...)` (builder.rs) with unknown nodes — behavior unstated. The user sees and trusts an incomplete graph with wrong statistics.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — NGG1 binary format compatibility rests on an unenforced cross-repo handshake
- Topics: VW-03.6, VW-04.6, VW-05.3
- Evidence: the reader "must stay byte-compatible" with visionGraph's `modern/src/lib/ngg1.ts`; the canonical spec (`FORMAT-NGG1.md`) lives in the consumer repo (EXTERNAL); the brief was already once "self-inconsistent" (20 vs 24 bytes — ngg1.rs:15-16). The release pipeline (VW-05.3) verifies tag/version and npm identity but nothing against visionGraph's writer or spec.
- Failure: the format evolves in the consumer repo; vowl-wasm independently tag-publishes via OIDC; the consumer bumps its pinned npm dependency; the Rust reader misparses tier bytes — a magic/version check may catch it, or offsets silently shift and the deployed explorer renders corrupted positions/edges.
- Confidence: medium, inferred — current TS/Rust agreement is not shown; the prior 20/24 discrepancy and the EXTERNAL spec location are stated
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (the byte-compat requirement and the 20/24 correction are disclosed in VW-03.6 prose; no Drift marker)

### F-05 — A settled `WebVowl` graph cannot be re-annealed; the only restart re-seeds from a circle
- Topics: VW-03.7, VW-03.9, VW-03.1, VW-04.1
- Evidence: at this commit only `CsrSimulation`/`NggExplorer` expose `reheat` (csr_sim.rs:368, explorer.rs:106); "a settled `WebVowl` graph can only be restarted via a fresh `initSimulation()`" (VW-03.7). `run()` also calls `initialize` first (simulation.rs:318-319). `initialize` re-seeds on a circle unless `preserve_seeded_positions` is set and nodes are already placed (simulation.rs:101-105) — and the frozen JS surface (VW-04.1) has no setter for that flag; its default is unstated.
- Failure: after alpha decays, any need to re-settle (unpin, filter, structural change) forces `initSimulation()`/`runSimulation()` which either wipes the user's layout back to a seed circle or silently depends on an unstated default; alternatively the layout stays frozen with no re-anneal at all.
- Confidence: medium, inferred — hinges on the unstated `preserve_seeded_positions` default
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: borderline — stated in VW-03.7 prose with the fixing commit referenced (`ac22cce`, ER-20); no formal marker

### F-06 — The shipped WASM artifact is never executed in CI
- Topics: VW-05.1, VW-04.5, VW-01 audit note
- Evidence: the wasm32 CI job only *builds* (`--target wasm32-unknown-unknown --release --features ngg1,interaction`); the wasm-pack job verifies file names and d.ts symbol strings; the test job is native `cargo test`. The audit qualification concedes coverage is "native logic, not optional ngg1, markdown-ontology, interaction, browser loading or a deployed consumer."
- Failure: a regression in `init()`, the zero-copy pointer path, memory growth behavior, or binding marshalling passes CI green (it compiles, symbols present) and is first discovered by end users of the explorer.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes — audit qualification paragraph in VW-01

### F-07 — `CsrSimulation` is O(n²) with no load-time node-count guard above its 1,500-node design point
- Topics: VW-03.5, VW-03.10, VW-03.6
- Evidence: the crate doc scopes the engine to ≤1,500-node T1 tiers and defers Barnes-Hut to "a follow-up slot" (csr_sim.rs:9-11); `SimConfig::default()` is the only configuration (csr_sim.rs:51); the `loadCsr` path shown (VW-03.6) contains no size check.
- Failure: a tier above the design point (or a future T2) loads without complaint; per-tick repulsion cost grows quadratically in a per-frame worker — frame budget blown, worker/UI freezes, no error raised.
- Confidence: medium — absence of a guard is inferred from the shown load path
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes — the algorithmic gap carries a "DIVERGENCE" label (VW-03.5); the missing load guard is unmarked

### F-08 — No panic hook plus `panic=abort`: production traps are undiagnosable
- Topics: VW-01.5, VW-05.5
- Evidence: `init()` is a no-op, "reserved for panic-hook install" (src/lib.rs:169); the release profile sets `panic = "abort"` (VW-05.5).
- Failure: any panic in the shipped module (e.g., a slice index on malformed NGG1 bytes) surfaces as a bare "unreachable" trap with no message or stack; incident triage from browser telemetry is blind to cause.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no formal marker; acknowledged by the "reserved for panic-hook" comment cited in VW-01.5

### F-09 — Two parallel force engines with no shared interface: fixes must land twice
- Topics: VW-03.7, VW-03.4, VW-03.5, VW-03.10
- Evidence: "CsrSimulation does not implement LayoutAlgorithm … share a design … but not a common Rust interface" (VW-03.7 note); the coincident-node nudge is implemented independently in both engines (quadtree.rs:377-389 and csr_sim.rs:219-226); already-divergent semantics: computed vs latched `finished`, reheat on one engine only, f64 vs f32 physics.
- Failure: a physics bug fixed in one engine persists in the other; WebVowl and explorer layouts behave differently for identical inputs, and the divergence mechanism is already visibly operating.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (stated factually in the VW-03.7 note)

### F-10 — The CI bundle check asserts only ~7 of the 25+ frozen-surface symbols
- Topics: VW-04.5, VW-04.1
- Evidence: CHECK2 verifies only `NggExplorer, positionsPtr, positionsLen, loadCsr, getMetadata, getNodePositions, writePositionsInto, getNodeIds`; the frozen surface (VW-04.1) additionally lists `loadOntology`, `initSimulation`, `runSimulation`, `tick`, the pinning family, the filtering family, `getStatistics`, `checkNodeClick`, etc.
- Failure: a feature-gating or refactor mistake silently drops e.g. `checkNodeClick` (interaction is in the bundle) or `pinNode` from the d.ts; CI stays green; the consumer breaks at runtime — precisely the failure mode the CI comment itself names ("the explorer breaks at runtime rather than at build").
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — All binding errors are flattened to message strings; error kinds never reach JS
- Topics: VW-01.6, VW-02.1
- Evidence: every `#[wasm_bindgen]` method returns `Result<T, JsValue>`; "the crate's typed `VowlError` never crosses the boundary, only its rendered message" (error.rs From impl); each `loadOntology` stage maps via `JsValue::from_str` (bindings/mod.rs:59,63,67).
- Failure: consumers cannot branch on error class (parse vs layout vs interaction); any message rewording breaks string-matching consumers; support cannot programmatically classify failures.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — phase3 bench targets `getGraphData()` which its own required-features cannot compile and which is absent from every shipped bundle
- Topics: VW-05.4, VW-01.4, VW-04.5
- Evidence: the phase3 header targets "getGraphData() serialisation <5ms" (benches/phase3_benchmarks.rs:1-6); `getGraphData` exists only under `debug-serde` (bindings/mod.rs:254, VW-01.4); both `interaction_bench` and `phase3_benchmarks` carry `required-features = interaction` (VW-05.4 diagram → Cargo.toml); the published bundle's features are `ngg1,interaction` (VW-04.5) — no `debug-serde`.
- Failure: either the bench fails to compile under its declared required features, or it measures a path excluded from every production artifact — in both cases the crate's only numeric performance targets are unenforced and unrepresentative of what ships.
- Confidence: medium — Cargo.toml may list additional required-features the diagram omits
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no (VW-05.4 notes the targets are "aspirational … not enforced")

### F-13 — The unsafe wasm32 SIMD path is never compiled by any CI job
- Topics: VW-03.4, VW-05.1, VW-05.2, VW-04.5
- Evidence: the feature-matrix job runs natively, where the wasm32 branch is cfg'd to a scalar shim (simd.rs:479); the wasm32 CI job builds with `--features ngg1,interaction` (no `simd`); the published bundle likewise omits `simd`. The unsafe simd128 wasm32 function (simd.rs:386) is therefore compiled by no CI configuration.
- Failure: simd-on-wasm32 breakage (deny lint, attribute error, codegen fault) lands silently; if `simd` is later added to the bundle it ships having never been built in the configuration in which it runs — compounding F-01.
- Confidence: medium, inferred from the job lists plus the cfg split shown in VW-03.4
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — Plain `cargo test` silently skips feature-gated tests; the audit's own run demonstrates the trap
- Topics: VW-05.2, VW-01 audit note
- Evidence: `[[test]] required-features` — `markdown_parser_test`/`owl2_validation_test` need `markdown-ontology`, `ngg1_explorer_test` needs `ngg1`; "a plain `cargo test` without those flags silently skips them." The audit qualification itself reports only "136 default-feature tests … not optional `ngg1` …".
- Failure: a contributor or release-notes verification reports green while the feature-specific tests never executed — false assurance in local sign-off; only the CI matrix (which does pass `--all-features`) mitigates.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes — demonstrated by the audit qualification in VW-01; the VW-05.2 bullet states the mechanism

### F-15 — `checkNodeClick` uses a fixed 20.0 hit radius for every node
- Topics: VW-04.3
- Evidence: `radius=20.0 fixed` built per node in the binding (bindings/mod.rs:501-513); the code comment concedes "In a real implementation, this could be configurable or per-node" (503-504).
- Failure: ontologies with varied node visual sizes — clicks inside a large node's rendered extent miss; clicks in empty space near a small node select it; selection errors in the production UI, worst for exactly the dense graphs this tool targets.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (the acknowledging code comment cited in VW-04.3)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. `PinManager.enabled` default and whether any JS-reachable path enables it (VW-02.8) — if it defaults to disabled and the frozen surface (VW-04.1, which lists no `enablePinning`) cannot change it, `pinNode` always errors from JS.
2. `GraphBuilder::add_edge` behavior when a property's source/target class is absent (e.g., after F-03's truncation) — error, silent drop, or panic (VW-02.3); this decides F-03's blast radius.
3. The default value of `preserve_seeded_positions` (VW-03.9) — it alone determines whether `initSimulation()`/`runSimulation()` preserve or wipe a laid-out graph, i.e., F-05's true severity.
4. NGG1 malformed-input handling: whether truncated/misaligned records and anything beyond `NGG1_MAGIC` (a version field?) are validated (VW-03.6) — clean `Ngg1Error` versus a bounds panic that becomes a trap under F-08.
5. Whether the visionGraph worker re-fetches `positionsPtr()` after every `loadCsr` and whether wasm memory is pre-reserved/shared (VW-04.6) — this decides whether F-02 fires in the deployed explorer or is fully mitigated.

## vowl-wasm / premortem

# Post-mortem — client-loss incident, vowl-wasm / @dreamlab-ai/vowl-wasm

Basis: the VW-01…VW-05 distillation only, at vowl-wasm `65e2d1e784bf5eb04b3cbc122d36d6926d889c22` (consumer visionGraph at `ac6274f9…`). All `path:line` citations below are the material's own and have not been re-verified against code; every item states confidence, and items reasoning past the material say "inferred". The incident narrative is the premise of this document, not evidence for any finding.

---

### R-1 — `loadOntology` silently truncates classes at the `max_classes` cap
- Topics: VW-02.2, VW-02.1, VW-02.3, VW-02.4
- Chain of events: JS calls `loadOntology(json)` → `StandardParser::parse` → `parse_classes` walks the `class` array → the cap check truncates the array instead of erroring → `OntologyData` carries only the first `max_classes` classes → `GraphBuilder::from_ontology` builds a `VowlGraph` missing those classes → statistics, pinning, filtering, `getNodeCount`/`getNodePositions` all describe the partial graph → JS receives success; no `VowlError` is raised anywhere in this chain. Properties whose endpoint classes were truncated then reach `graph.add_edge(source, target, edge)` — behavior on missing endpoints is unstated in the material (suspect: panic on `node_map` lookup, or silent edge drop; verify).
- Evidence: VW-02.2: "a `class` array longer than the configured cap is silently truncated, not rejected" (src/ontology/parser.rs:62-64; `ParserConfig` at src/ontology/parser.rs:17). VW-02.1: load path returns Ok and stores graph+metadata (src/bindings/mod.rs:71-72). The material never states the default cap value — it must be read from code. The load path's validate stage is `StandardParser::validate` (src/ontology/parser.rs:485), whose checks are unenumerated and separate from the OWL2Validator (which is not even compiled into the published bundle, VW-01.4).
- Warning signs visible today: the truncation is documented as current behavior, not a hypothetical; no test asserting rejection — or even a warning — on truncation is cited anywhere in VW-02/VW-05.
- Confidence: high — the truncation behavior is stated verbatim; its role in this incident is this post-mortem's premise. The missing-endpoint behavior is unstated: low, inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Zero-copy `Float32Array` over `wasm.memory` invalidated by any allocation on the `NggExplorer` path
- Topics: VW-01.7, VW-04.2, VW-04.6
- Chain of events: the worker builds `new Float32Array(wasm.memory.buffer, positionsPtr(), positionsLen())` (VW-04.6) and reads positions per frame with no copy (VW-04.2 "ZeroCopy"). This is safe only while nothing on the `NggExplorer` path grows wasm memory — an invariant held by convention ("never allocates across the WASM boundary per frame", src/bindings/explorer.rs:9), with no cited test enforcing it. Any allocation after view construction — error-path string building via the `to_string` mapping at src/bindings/explorer.rs:42-44, a `setParam` path (listed as an optional live-tuning call in VW-04.6), or an engine change such as the in-flight reheat work (VW-03.7, commit `ac22cce`) — grows the memory: the held view's buffer detaches (reads throw, physics loop dies) or a re-derived pointer using a stale `ptr` reads unrelated memory and garbage positions are published as graph data.
- Evidence: positionsPtr/Len at src/bindings/explorer.rs:82,89; the per-frame no-alloc invariant at src/bindings/explorer.rs:9 (VW-01.7); VW-04.2 calls this "the only genuinely copy-free path" (src/bindings/mod.rs:369-374); VW-04.6 shows the worker's SharedArrayBuffer/transferable double-buffer technique and permissive method resolution. The CI bundle check (VW-04.5) verifies symbol presence in the d.ts, not view lifetime.
- Warning signs visible today: the safety argument is one sentence of prose on one binding file; allocation sites demonstrably exist on the same object (error mapping); the consumer's usage surface (optional `setParam`, permissive name lookup) is already wider than the invariant's phrasing.
- Confidence: medium, inferred — the view-over-memory architecture is stated; the detach/stale-pointer consequence and the specific trigger are reasoning beyond the material.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Malformed NGG1 tier bytes panic; `panic = "abort"` kills the worker unrecoverably and silently
- Topics: VW-03.6, VW-05.5, VW-01.5, VW-05.2
- Chain of events: worker calls `loadCsr(bytes)` → the reader decodes a 32-byte header and node records at a 24-byte stride (src/ngg1.rs:21-24, `N_DEGREE` offset 20 at :31). The material documents alignment care and "no unsafe" (src/ngg1.rs:9-13) but states no validation that header-derived node/edge counts fit the supplied slice. A truncated or count-corrupted tier (bad download, partial write, or writer drift — see F-02) indexes past the slice → panic → the release profile is `panic = "abort"` (Cargo.toml `[profile.release]`, VW-05.5) → the module traps with no unwinding; `init()` is a no-op and the panic hook it was "reserved for" was never installed (src/lib.rs:169, note at :162, VW-01.5), so no message reaches the console; the worker dies with no in-module recovery. The ngg1 tests are behind `required-features` and were skipped by the audit's own green run (VW-05.2, VW-01 audit note) — no cited test feeds malformed bytes.
- Evidence: citations above; VW-03.6 asserts only the happy-path `Result<(), Ngg1Error>`.
- Warning signs visible today: the format has already required one spec correction (24 vs 20 bytes, src/ngg1.rs:15-16) and its canonical spec lives in another repo; `panic = "abort"` and the missing hook are stated facts of the shipped profile.
- Confidence: low, inferred — this is an absence-of-evidence finding (bounds checks may exist in code); reported for verification rather than omitted.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — SIMD leaf-force kernel omits the coincident-node guard documented everywhere else, and CI never executes it
- Topics: VW-03.4, VW-05.1, VW-05.2, VW-01.4
- Evidence: VW-03.4's INVARIANT names exactly two guarded paths — the scalar leaf path (src/layout/quadtree.rs:384-387, `distance_sq < 0.0001`) and the CSR tick (VW-03.5, src/layout/csr_sim.rs:219-226) — and is silent on `calculate_leaf_force_simd()` (src/layout/simd.rs:386). CI never runs it: the wasm32 job builds `--features ngg1,interaction` without `simd` (ci.yml, VW-05.1); the feature matrix only `cargo check`s it (VW-05.2); native runs take the scalar shim because `is_simd_available()` is hardcoded `false` off-wasm32 (src/layout/simd.rs:525). The `simd` cargo feature also silently no-ops unless the simd128 target-feature is set (VW-01.4).
- Failure: a simd-enabled build with coincident nodes (duplicate NGG1 seeds, re-loaded identical positions) divides by ~0 in the kernel → NaN/Inf force → NaN position → propagates through every subsequent distance computation → whole-graph NaN written to the position buffer.
- Confidence: medium, inferred from the invariant's explicit enumeration and the CI job list.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — NGG1 is dual-maintained across repos with no cited conformance test; a stride mismatch misparses silently
- Topics: VW-03.6, VW-04.6
- Evidence: the reader "must stay byte-compatible" with the TypeScript counterpart `modern/src/lib/ngg1.ts`; the canonical spec `publishing-tools/WasmVOWL/FORMAT-NGG1.md` lives in the visionGraph repo (src/ngg1.rs:3-4); the crate doc already corrected a self-inconsistent brief on record size (24, not 20 bytes, src/ngg1.rs:15-16). No cross-repo round-trip test is cited in VW-05.
- Failure: the writer changes in visionGraph (new field, changed stride) while the pinned npm reader lags (VW-04.6: the consumer depends on the published package, never source) → every field after the shifted offset decodes as garbage ids/degrees/seed positions → corrupted graph rendered as client data.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (drift risk from stated history).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift — the 24-vs-20 correction is recorded in the crate doc and the external-spec dependency stated at src/ngg1.rs:3-4)

### F-03 — The `ids[i] ↔ xy[2i]` index-stability contract is enforced only by doc comments over an index-indirected graph
- Topics: VW-04.1, VW-04.2, VW-02.4
- Evidence: the guarantee ("ticking, pinning, filtering and folding never reorder or remove nodes") is sourced to doc comments at src/bindings/mod.rs:288-334; no test is cited. `VowlGraph` holds a `DiGraph<Node,Edge>` plus `HashMap<String,NodeIndex>` node_map (src/graph/mod.rs:24) — any future node removal typically reorders indices (swap-remove-style churn, inferred) and silently breaks the zip JS is told it may do "once per load".
- Failure: a reordering change ships → labels and positions mispair → client sees classes at wrong coordinates with no error anywhere.
- Confidence: medium, inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — Validation is advisory end-to-end and duplicate-id handling is unstated; `node_map` overwrite can merge two client classes
- Topics: VW-02.6, VW-02.1, VW-02.4, VW-01.4
- Evidence: VW-02.6's own INVARIANT is internally strained: IRI-uniqueness failures are "hard `errors`" yet "`validate_block` never rejects a block outright" (src/ontology/owl2_validator.rs:37-43) — errors do not gate ingestion. In the published bundle the OWL2Validator is not compiled at all (`validateOWL2` is markdown-ontology-gated, VW-01.4); the load path calls `StandardParser::validate` (src/ontology/parser.rs:485) with unenumerated checks. `GraphBuilder::from_ontology` adds a node per class with no stated duplicate-id handling; `node_map` is `HashMap<String,NodeIndex>` (src/graph/mod.rs:24), so a second insert under the same id overwrites the first index.
- Failure: two same-id classes in client JSON → one graph node orphaned from the map, edges/labels attach to the survivor; `getNodeCount` (graph-derived) and `getNodeIds` (derivation unstated) can disagree in length, breaking the VW-04.1 zip.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (duplicate handling is unstated behavior).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — Feature-gated tests silently skip; the audit's own green run covered none of the optional paths
- Topics: VW-05.2, VW-01 (audit note), VW-04.5
- Evidence: `markdown_parser_test`/`owl2_validation_test` need `markdown-ontology`, `ngg1_explorer_test` needs `ngg1` (Cargo.toml `[[test]]` required-features) — "a plain `cargo test` without those flags silently skips them" (VW-05.2). The audit qualification records the passing run as default-features only, covering "not optional ngg1, markdown-ontology, interaction, browser loading or a deployed consumer". The only shipped-bundle guard is d.ts symbol presence (VW-04.5).
- Failure: a behavioral regression in the explorer — the production worker path — passes a local default test run; CI catches it only if a symbol name disappears.
- Confidence: high (stated).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open — the audit qualification paragraph flags exactly these uncovered areas)

### F-06 — `panic = "abort"` plus a never-installed panic hook: any panic is an opaque, unrecoverable trap
- Topics: VW-05.5, VW-01.5
- Evidence: `[profile.release] panic=abort` (Cargo.toml, VW-05.5); `init()` is "no-op today, reserved for panic-hook install" (src/lib.rs:169, note at :162, VW-01.5) — still unfilled at this snapshot.
- Failure: any runtime panic (indexing, unwrap) aborts the module with no message; no console signal, no in-process recovery, blind diagnosis.
- Confidence: high (both facts stated; consequence follows).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — `writePositionsInto` silently writes a partial frame on an undersized buffer
- Topics: VW-04.2
- Evidence: writes `min(out.len(), n*2)` and returns the count written (src/bindings/mod.rs:376, 395-407); no error or warning on short buffers is stated.
- Failure: JS passes a buffer sized to a stale node count → the frame's tail keeps previous coordinates → mixed-frame positions rendered as current data unless the caller checks the return value.
- Confidence: medium (behavior stated; caller neglect inferred).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — `loadOntology` replaces the graph with no stated reset of pins, filters or tuned parameters
- Topics: VW-02.1, VW-02.8, VW-04.1
- Evidence: load "store[s] metadata and graph on self" (src/bindings/mod.rs:71-72); nothing in VW-02/VW-04 states that `PinManager` state (`HashSet<String>` of pinned ids, src/graph/pinning.rs), visibility filters (`filterByDegree` etc.) or `setCenter`/`setLinkDistance`/`setChargeStrength` values are cleared on load.
- Failure: reload an ontology reusing ids → inherited pins fix new nodes at old coordinates, inherited filters hide classes in the fresh load; wrong layout, no error.
- Confidence: low, inferred (material silent on reset semantics).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — CSR engine is hard-scoped to ≤1,500 nodes with Barnes-Hut an unimplemented "follow-up slot", and no stated load-side size guard
- Topics: VW-03.5, VW-03.10
- Evidence: crate doc scopes `CsrSimulation` to T1 ≤1,500-node tiers and calls Barnes-Hut for this path "a follow-up slot, not yet implemented" (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:9-11); tagged DIVERGENCE in VW-03.5; no node-count check is stated in `load_csr` (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:182).
- Failure: a larger tier loaded via `loadCsr` runs O(n²) repulsion every tick → worker frame budget collapses → frozen visualization for that client.
- Confidence: high (limitation stated); the missing size guard is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open — labeled DIVERGENCE / "follow-up slot")

### F-10 — Frozen 0.1.x surface blocks signature-changing fixes; `WebVowl` has no reheat, so re-anneal means a full, layout-discarding re-init
- Topics: VW-04.1, VW-03.7, VW-03.9
- Evidence: README.md:132-135 invariant — names/signatures fixed for 0.1.x, additions only. VW-03.7: at this revision only `CsrSimulation`/`NggExplorer` expose reheat (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:368, src/bindings/explorer.rs:106); a settled `WebVowl` graph restarts only via fresh `initSimulation()`, and initialize is "a clean restart, not a resume" (VW-03.9) — current layout discarded unless `preserve_seeded_positions` was set (src/layout/simulation.rs:101).
- Failure: during an incident, a fix requiring signature changes cannot reach pinned npm consumers (VW-04.6) without a major bump; meanwhile recovering a settled graph loses its layout.
- Confidence: high (facts stated); the operational cost is implied.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open — the reheat gap and pending commit `ac22cce` are flagged in VW-03.7; the semver rule is stated as an invariant, not as debt)

### F-11 — Typed `VowlError` is flattened to a string `JsValue` at every binding
- Topics: VW-01.6, VW-02.1
- Evidence: "the crate's typed `VowlError` never crosses the boundary, only its rendered message" (VW-01.6); every `#[wasm_bindgen]` method returns `Result<T, JsValue>` (e.g. src/bindings/mod.rs:57).
- Failure: JS cannot programmatically distinguish `ParseError` from `InvalidData` from `GraphError` → no targeted retry/compensation; operator error reports carry prose only.

- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

- Topics: VW-04.3, VW-02.4

Node positions are 2D (`VisualAttributes` has x,y only, src/graph/mod.rs:101) while the API takes `ray_origin[3]`/`ray_direction[3]` and does `ray_sphere_intersection` (src/interaction/mod.rs:99); how z is derived is not stated.

- Topics: VW-03.4, VW-03.5

### F-15 — Crate-level `#!deny(unsafe_code)` coexists with an unsafe kernel, voiding the advertised guarantee exactly where testing is absent
- Topics: VW-01.1, VW-03.4
- Evidence: src/lib.rs:141-142 `#!deny(missing_docs, unsafe_code)` (VW-01.1) vs `unsafe calculate_leaf_force_simd()` at src/layout/simd.rs:386 (VW-03.4). For both to compile, a local `#[allow(unsafe_code)]` must exist somewhere the material does not cite.
- Failure: reviewers and downstream auditors rely on the crate-level deny as a "no unsafe" guarantee; the single unsafe kernel — the one CI never executes (F-01) — is exempted without a visible marker at the crate root.

- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no
